# Gap Worklist — the gap list as tracked, actionable work items

**Type:** Concept page — **work tracker**, version-independent (folder: `Atlas 0.1/common/`)
**Status:** opened 2026-08-26. The actionable counterpart to [[generalization-requirements]]: that page says *what is missing and why*, this one says *what to do, in what order, and how you know it is done*. **Update the Status column as items land; do not delete rows.**
**Related:** [[tier0-measurements]] · [[generalization-requirements]] · [[master-error-bound]] · [[general-coupling-scheme]] · [[probed-dtn-coupling]] · [[open-problems-atlas-0.1]] · [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] · [[composition-error-theory]] · [[temporal-error-accumulation]] · [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] · [[theory-closure-audit]]

> **How to use this page.** Items are ordered by *what unblocks the most*, not by G-number. **W1–W3 come first and are not negotiable**, because until the bound's constants exist nothing below them can be prioritized rationally — every ranking of the rest is currently a guess. Each row states a **definition of done** precise enough that the item cannot be quietly declared finished.

**Legend.** `open` · `in progress` · `done` · `refused` (with reason, the way `schwarz > 1` was refused).

---

# Tier 0 — measure the bound (nothing else can be ranked until these exist)

| # | Gap | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W1** | G20 | **Fit $L$.** Perturb a converged state by $\Delta$, roll the perturbed and unperturbed trajectories, fit $\lVert e^n\rVert$ against $L^n$ | A number for $L$ with an error bar, plus $T_{\text{pred}}=\lambda_1^{-1}\ln(\delta_{\text{tol}}/\delta)$; and a stated verdict on whether OP-2's drift rate matches the fitted $\eta=L-1$ | one paired rollout | **`done`** 2026-08-27 |
| **W2** | G20, G21 | **Assemble one $\tilde\Lambda$** for a `WindowNS` agent on a 16-mode Fourier interface basis | $\kappa(S)$, $\beta=\sigma_{\min}$, null-space dimension (**must equal $n_0(\Gamma)$**, which is $1$ for this fluid–fluid seam — anything else indicts the probe or the declaration, not the physics; [[interface-transfer-theory]] §7), and $\mu=\lambda_{\min}$ of the symmetric part, reported raw beside the floored defect $\pi$ | $m{+}1$ window solves; hours | **`done`** 2026-08-27 — **and the null check failed** |
| **W3** | G20, G12 | **Three-way split of one macro-step's defect** into $\tau$, $\sigma$, $\gamma$ against the classical reference, with the assembly share separated | Three numbers summing to the measured defect, and a verdict on the prediction $\gamma\lll\tau,\sigma$ | one instrumented step | **`done`** 2026-08-27 |
| **W4** | G20 | **Emit the full instrumentation from every run**: $(\tau,\sigma,\gamma,L,\beta,\kappa,\pi,\mathcal R,T_{\text{pred}})$ | The `emit` line of [[general-coupling-scheme]] §4.1 present in the coupled step, and a rollout artefact carrying all nine | days; all are by-products of existing machinery | `in progress` |

**Why this tier is first.** [[master-error-bound]] §7: the framework reports $\gamma$ — the smallest term — and has never reported the other two. **W1–W3 are each under a day and together they decide the whole ordering below.**

## Closed 2026-08-27, against `reference.WindowNS` — full record on [[tier0-measurements]]

**Cost, corrected.** W2's estimate said *hours*. The whole tier ran in **under fifteen minutes** on a laptop: one window macro-step is $0.069$ s, so $m{+}1=17$ solves per block is seconds, not hours. The estimate was made when the only expert in view was a neural checkpoint. **Anything priced against the frozen checkpoint is mispriced by two to three orders for a classical expert**, which reorders every remaining cost column on this page.

| | measured | verdict against the definition of done |
|---|---|---|
| **W1** | $L=0.948441\pm0.0048$ (composed, one-pass); $0.979649\pm0.00041$ (monolith); amplitude-independent to six digits | Met. $L<1$, so $T_{\text{pred}}$ is **unbounded** — the contractive branch, reached empirically. OP-2's implied $\eta=+1.43\times10^{-2}$ against a fitted $-5.16\times10^{-2}$: **opposite signs, stated as no match**, with the caveat that OP-2's configuration differs in three variables at once |
| **W2** | $\beta\in[3.10,6.44]\times10^{-3}$, $\kappa\in[18.8,38.5]$, $\mu>0$ and $\pi=0$ on all four seams | Met — every quantity reported, $\mu$ raw beside $\pi$. **The null check failed: measured $0$, declared $1$.** The theory's instruction ("indicts the probe or the declaration") is right and it is the **declaration**: [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.1 cannot both hold pressure global and claim the null space incompressibility gives |
| **W3** | total $3.7071\times10^{-4}$; $\tau=3.7014\times10^{-4}$, $\sigma=2.6995\times10^{-5}$, $\gamma=3.48\times10^{-15}$ | Met, with one correction to the wording: **the three do not sum to the total** and cannot, being norms of partly cancelling errors. $\gamma\lll\tau,\sigma$ **confirmed by eleven orders of magnitude** |

**Three findings that change work below rather than closing it.**

1. **$\tau$ is not agent infidelity.** It dominates at $99.8\%$ while the agent *is* the reference solver. It is $\Delta t$-independent across a $25\times$ range, lives away from the seams ($42\%$ beyond 32 cells), and is exactly the per-window pressure projection: `tile.last_mismatch` $=$ `tile.last_div` $=2.76\times10^{-4}$ against the monolith's $8\times10^{-15}$. **[[plug-in-composition-theorems]] §1.4's attribution theorem, observed** — a $\sigma$ one level down wearing a $\tau$ label. **W34 (depth-tag every defect) is promoted from tiny to necessary.**
2. **The interface condition is a steady condition, and the scheme built on a healthy operator is wrong.** The true trace does **not** satisfy $\sum_i\Lambda_i\lambda=\chi$ ($\lVert r\rVert$ goes $2.6421\times10^{-5}\to2.6520\times10^{-5}$ when it is substituted), so solving it exactly overshoots by $41\times$ — growing to $150\times$ as $\Delta t$ is refined — and makes one composed step $8.9\times$ worse than not solving it. **W7 (R9 on the time-integrated flux) and W17 (waveform relaxation) are both promoted**: see below.
3. **The master bound holds with every constant measured** — tight at $N=1$ (ratio $0.93$), $14\times$ loose asymptotically, and the measured error **saturates** at $5.4\times10^{-4}$ rather than drifting. First bound this vault has quoted rather than named.

> **2026-08-28.** Everything the paragraph below lists as open in this tier is now closed except the last sentence, and that one is only partly: $\lVert\mathcal A\rVert$ is a theorem, $\Xi$ is measured **and** its positive control run, $\lVert\Lambda-\tilde\Lambda\rVert$ is measured and the $\sigma$ factorization reconciled on the branch that applies (W49). **"One configuration" is now five** — $\mathrm{Re}$ and $\Delta t$ each varied $4\times$ — but **"one expert" stands**, and it is now tracked as **W55**. **W4** also moves: with `condition`, $\Pi$ and a set exchange interval, the emit line carries every field it was specified to.

**What is still open in this tier and is not being rounded up.** $\lVert\mathcal A\rVert$ was **not** computed — `AssemblyCertificate` stays a named hole, `condition` stays empty, E6 stays `unchecked`, and W28's second and third fields are untouched. $\Xi$ was not measured. $\lVert\Lambda-\tilde\Lambda\rVert$ was not measured, so the $\sigma$ above is an observed field defect and **not** the bound's product form $\tfrac{C_\mu}{\beta}\lVert\Lambda-\tilde\Lambda\rVert\lVert\lambda^\star\rVert$; the two have not been reconciled. All numbers are one configuration, one state, one expert.

# Tier 1 — silent correctness bugs (wrong answers, no signal)

| # | Gap | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W5** | G11 | **Make the conservative-vs-consistent mapping explicit per port.** An `ADVEC` flow maps conservatively (integral preserved); a `THERM` effort consistently (pointwise). Currently chosen implicitly, per edge, by accident | A `mapping: conservative\|consistent` field on every port declaration, defaulted **per port type not per edge**, with a test that catches a reversed pair | small — already diagnosed in [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §9.3 | `done` |
| **W6** | G1 | **Cross-point rule.** Decide and document the convention for 3+ subdomains meeting: most likely primal (single-valued) corner DOFs, dual elsewhere | A written rule in [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]], plus a test on a 4-agent junction showing $\beta$ does not collapse | medium — **required before** [[probed-dtn-coupling]] is built on any non-chain graph | **`done` 2026-08-28 — see Tier 12.** The rule was written in the 2026-08-27 amendment; the TEST ran now, on the four-window junction, and it needed a **multi-port** probe first (**W64**) because a per-seam assembly is block-diagonal by construction. **Beta does not collapse** (1.00x, with a real 1.8% off-diagonal coupling). **The defect is multi-valuedness**: the shared cell gets one value per seam and they differ by 1.62% in u and **66.7% in v, disagreeing about the sign** |
| **W7** | G4 | **Multirate conservation.** Enforce flux matching on the **time-integrated** flux over the macro-step, not pointwise in time | A rule in [[general-coupling-scheme]] (R9 promoted from prose to a check), plus a two-rate test where pointwise matching demonstrably leaks and integrated matching does not | medium — the rocket needs it | `in progress` — **promoted 2026-08-27 and the promotion RETRACTED 2026-08-28.** [[tier0-measurements]] §9.3 built the single-rate time-integrated condition and measured it: it fails both of §5's tests identically to the pointwise one (reference-trace ratio $1.002$–$1.028$, and solving degrades the step by $2.00\times$ against pointwise's $1.59\times$). The defect §5 found is **spatial**, not temporal — at a shared-layer seam $F_A+F_B=-\nu h\,\partial_{nn}w$ exactly — so integrating in time cannot touch it. **W7 reverts to what R9 is actually about: multirate**, and the two-rate test is still owed |
| **W8** | G3 | **Field↔lumped adjointness.** Require $\langle e,\mathcal P_\Gamma f_0\rangle_\Gamma=\langle\mathcal R_\Gamma e,f_0\rangle_0$ for every reduction/prolongation pair | The condition stated in [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]], a numerical check on the wind farm's actuator disk, and a verdict on whether it currently holds | small to check, unknown to fix | `done` |
| **W132** | G3 | **Two different physical quantities can be declared under one `response_half`, and `L3/C9` cannot see it.** Lifted out of CS-10 (Tier 24, [[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]] §5) so Phase C does not rediscover it. `ResponseHalf` names *which half of the conjugate pair* a callable returns, and `L3/C9` refuses a seam whose two sides disagree about that. At CS-10's wing seam **both sides correctly declared `EFFORT`** and returned different things: the fluid side returned the **viscous traction alone** ($\nu\,\partial v/\partial n$) and the lumped side returned the **whole** traction. Both are efforts, C9 passes, and the two blocks differ by $\mathbf{1.8\times10^{4}}$ — which is the whole of the $19.4\times$ one-sidedness W97 was about. **W69's *"the wrong half differs from the right one by an unseparable $O(1)$"* has its counter-example here: the two CORRECT halves differ by four orders.** At a fluid–fluid seam the pressure and advective parts cancel between the two sides and the class is invisible; at a field-to-lumped seam nothing cancels, which is why five case studies never met it | An **`effort_form`** string beside `response_half`, compared across a seam the way `governing_family` is compared by E3 — a string equality, not a measurement — refusing on disagreement and decertifying when either side leaves it undeclared. Plus a test on the wing seam's three efforts showing the declaration separates what C9 does not | small — one field and one comparison; the discriminating measurement already exists in `out/w127/w127.json` | `open`, and it is **Tier 1 by this tier's own test**: nothing raises, nothing fails a gate, and the number that comes out is wrong by four orders. It is W69's class arriving in a **verifiable** form for once — a form string can be compared across a seam even though a half cannot be checked against the callable |

**Common property, and the reason this tier ranks above everything except measurement:** none of these raises an error, fails a gate, or produces a suspicious number. W8 additionally **silently disables the passivity theorem**, so W12 is worthless until W8 is checked.

**W132 is the newest and it sharpens what the tier is about.** The other four are checks that were never written. W132 is a check that *is* written, *does* run, and *passes* — `L3/C9` compared the two declarations and they agreed — while the quantity underneath differed by four orders. The failure is not a missing comparison; it is a declaration that does not name the thing that varies. Tier 24's mechanism tally states the general form: *a verified number can be verified against the wrong question.*

# Tier 5 — instrumenting the holes (opened 2026-08-27 by [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]])

**The principle behind this tier, and it is the reason all six items are small.** Five gaps have no rule and no importable answer, so the architecture gives each a **named slot with a declared interface** instead of an assumption — and requires **the measurement the missing rule would constrain**, emitted now, before the rule exists. When the rule eventually arrives it arrives with a dataset. These items do not attempt to fill the holes; they instrument them.

| # | Gap | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W27** | envelope (E1–E7) | **Emit the envelope stamp** from every run: each of the seven hypotheses valued `holds`, `fails` or `unchecked` | No rollout artefact exists without a stamp, and **E1–E4 are decided at compile time before compute is spent** | small | `in progress` |
| **W28** | G12 | **`AssemblyCertificate`.** Emit the partition-of-unity identity residual $\lVert\sum_i R_i^{\!\top}\chi_i R_i - I\rVert$, the norm $\lVert\mathcal A\rVert$, and the blend defect $\alpha$; **refuse** on a failed identity | Four fields emitted per run; the identity check present in the coupled step; $\alpha$ compared against OP-5's 19% subtraction share, with a verdict on whether the two agree | small — rides on W3 | `in progress` — **one field of four landed 2026-08-27.** The identity residual is machine zero on a shared-layer tiling with $\chi_i=1/(\text{owner count})$, where it holds *by construction* — which is a weaker result than a check that could have failed. **$\lVert\mathcal A\rVert$ was not computed and $\alpha$ was not compared against OP-5's $19\%$**, so the slot stays a named hole, `condition` stays empty and E6 stays `unchecked` |
| **W29** | G2 | **`SeamReference`.** Declare `governing_family` per expert; emit the list of seams where $\tau$ is `UNDEFINED`, plus the per-side surrogate $\lVert\Lambda^{\text{expert}}-\Lambda^{\text{ref}}\rVert$ | Multiphysics seams are identified at compile time by a string comparison, and their $\tau$ is **marked rather than silently computed** | small to declare | `in progress` |
| **W30** | G17 | **`InterfaceMotion`.** `motion_class` per port with `static` as the default and anything else refused; emit the operator drift $\lVert S(t+K\Delta t)-S(t)\rVert$ **even on static runs** | A drift number exists, so probed-DtN's cached-$S$ economics are priced rather than assumed | small — one extra assembly | **`done`** 2026-08-27 — **the drift number exists**: $2.4\times10^{-4}$ to $3.5\times10^{-3}$ relative over $K=10$ macro-steps on four seams ([[tier0-measurements]] §2.4). The cached-$S$ economics survive; refreshing every $\sim50$ steps holds $S$ within about $1.5\%$. **2026-09-03: the comparison this row was built for is made, and it inverts the economics.** On CS-10's *moving* field-to-lumped seam the same measurement reads $\mathbf{3.77\times10^{-1}}$ over $K=10$ — $108$ to $1572$ times the band above, and $15.0\times$ the same seam held still — while the fluid–fluid seam beside it stays inside the band whether the wing moves or not. At a $2\%$ staleness tolerance a cached $S$ survives **one macro-step**, so re-probing costs $75.0\%$ of the march for one seam of eight. The other two `measurements_required` are supplied with it: the re-probe count, and the $\mathbf{43.4\%}$ of the seam's power exchange the moving interface carries and a static accounting reports as zero ([[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]] §4) |
| **W31** | G9 | **`TopologyEvent`.** An event must carry a state map **and** a conservation ledger giving the jump in each conserved functional, plus the injected $\lVert e^0\rVert$ | An event cannot be declared without a ledger; ledger values are emitted and **unconstrained**, because no rule yet says what they should be | small to specify | `in progress` |
| **W32** | G16 | **`PortAmendment`.** Write the six-field amendment procedure into [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] as a binding section, and exercise it once on `ADVEC`'s passenger list | Adding a sixth port type is a **defined operation**; a case study introducing one inline is refused | small | **`done` 2026-09-01 — see Tier 22.** [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §10 is binding, `ports.spec_for` enforces it, and the first exercise (thermal strain, not `ADVEC`) **refused** and found a **seventh** field |

# Reframed by the 2026-08-27 end-to-end spec

[[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] **closes nothing** — a specification closes no measurement, and saying so plainly is better than finding something to claim. What it does is change what several existing items *are*. Rows above are unchanged; this table records the reinterpretation.

| # | Reframed how |
|---|---|
| **W5** | From *a field to add* to a **compile-time refusal** (spec L3, condition C3). Definition of done gains: the compiler refuses a connection whose `mapping` is unset |
| **W6** | From *a rule to write* to **a refusal that already exists in the spec** (L2) while the rule does not. The refusal preceding the rule is the correct order, not a gap in the spec |
| **W8** | From *a check to run* to a **connection precondition** (L3, condition C6) that every field↔lumped port must pass. This raises its effective priority |
| **W9** | Definition of done **grows by five fields** — `governing_family`, `lambda_ref`, `claim_types`, and per-port `mapping`, `motion_class`, and the `reduction`/`prolongation`/`adjointness` triple |
| **W10** | [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] **is the compiler's specification.** The nine rules become layer gates; add the three-verdict system (`admit`, `admit-uncertified`, `refuse`) and the W27 stamp to its definition of done |
| **W4** | The emit set **grows from nine quantities to eight groups**, adding the assembly certificate, the envelope stamp, the claim type with its horizon table, the slot measurements, and the refusal/decertification record |
| **W14** | From *a decision plus an implementation* to **an implementation of a decided rule**: trajectory inside $T_{\text{pred}}$, statistical outside, statistical **refused rather than approximated**, `UNTYPED` when $\hat L$ is unknown. Also **three horizon branches** instead of one — exponential at $L>1$, linear at $L=1$, unbounded at $L<1$ |
| **W16** | Promoted from Tier 4 to **the specified decomposition policy** (L2), with its **[AI Inference]** status preserved verbatim — adopted as policy, not upgraded to a result. **Superseded 2026-08-28 (Tier 10): the policy is derived as L2/C2 and the scalarization carrying it is falsified.** Preserving the tag rather than quietly promoting it is what made this checkable, and this is what it was preserved for |
| **W17** | Reclassified from *a $\Delta t$ constraint* to **a component of $\sigma$ in time**, which is where it belongs in the bound |
| **W22** | **Splits into two**: `TopologyEvent` (W31) and `InterfaceMotion` (W30). They were bundled because both were untouched; they have different declared interfaces and different owning layers |

> **One item is *unblocked* rather than reframed.** W14 was waiting on a judgement call about whether the architecture types claims at all. §11.1 of the spec makes that call, so W14 is now implementation of a decided rule. It stays `open`.

# Tier 6 — the composition operation itself (opened 2026-08-27 by [[interface-transfer-theory]] and [[plug-in-composition-theorems]])

**Only four genuine additions, and that is the point of the two pages.** [[interface-transfer-theory]] mostly *merges and rewrites* existing rows rather than adding to them — six of the nine importable mechanisms turn out to be one declared object — while [[plug-in-composition-theorems]] opens four gaps (**G25–G28**) that no existing G-number covered, because every prior gap was about a mechanism and these are about the composition operation.

| # | Gap | Task | Definition of done | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W33** | **G27** | **Run the conformance suite.** One probe per expert certifies `bc_channel` ($\Xi_i>0$), `storage` ($\lambda_{\min}$ of the symmetric part), and — with one extra solve per generator — `equivariances`. Issue a `ConformanceCertificate` bound to the weight hash and the probe state | Every expert in the library carries a certificate; a record whose measured value contradicts its declaration is **refused at admission**. `validity` records *"not falsified on suite X"*, never *"valid"* | **small** — rides entirely on W2's probe. **`done` 2026-08-28 — see Tier 12.** Five real records plus one that lies; the liar is **refused at admission**. `validity` decertifies on all six, which is correct rather than a gap. **The first run found W63**: the suite refused on the block where W48 says the seam decides |
| **W34** | **G25** | **Depth-tag every emitted defect.** $\tau$, $\sigma$ and $\gamma$ are properties of a decomposition at a stated depth, not of the problem | No defect is emitted without the depth it was measured at; a nested run's numbers are comparable with a flat run's | **tiny** — one field, and **no longer optional**: [[tier0-measurements]] §4.1 measures a $\tau$ that is $99.8\%$ of the defect and is really a decomposed elliptic solve one level down. Without the depth tag that number reads as "the expert is bad" |
| **W35** | **G26** | **Substitution certificate.** On any expert swap, emit $\lVert\Delta\rVert=\lVert\tilde\Lambda_i'-\tilde\Lambda_i\rVert$ and check $\lVert\Delta\rVert<\beta-\beta_{\min}$ before the rollout | A swap either passes the inequality or triggers re-certification of $\sigma$, $L$ and $T_{\text{pred}}$. **No swap is accepted on the grounds that the new expert benchmarks better** | **small** — two probes, no rollout |
| **W36** | **G28** | **Emit the declination rate.** Per-agent per-macro-step $p$, and the abstention horizon $T_{\text{abs}}\approx\Delta t/(Kp)$ alongside $T_{\text{pred}}$ | Every rollout reports $T_{\text{usable}}=\min(T_{\text{pred}},T_{\text{abs}})$. Blocked behind W13, since $p$ is undefined until an expert declares `validity` | **small**, after W13 |

## Reframed by the 2026-08-27 theory pages

| item | reframed how |
|---|---|
| **W5, W8, W11** | **Merge into one item.** [[interface-transfer-theory]] §1–§4: declare a common interface space $M$ per seam and one prolongation $P_i:M\to V_i$ per side; the reduction is forced to be $P_i^\ast$. Then the conservative-vs-consistent choice (W5) is **derived, not declared**; field↔lumped adjointness (W8) is the case $\dim M=1$; and non-conforming geometry (W11) stops being a restriction of the construction. Definition of done for all three: **one declared $P_i$ per side per seam, and a compile-time refusal when it is absent** |
| **W6** | The rule is now **written and conditional on $\mathcal D$** — no action under overlapping+PoU, primal cross-point DOFs under non-overlapping. Detection rides on the probe's existing null-space check: any excess null direction is a defect |
| **W7** | R9 becomes a **stated condition with a refusal**, plus two consequences it did not carry: the interface representation order **caps the scheme order**, and the stability limit is set by coupling stiffness — conjecturally $\kappa(\tilde\Lambda)$, which the probe already prints |
| **W15** | **Splits.** The per-seam indicator $\eta_\Gamma$ needs only the port residual and is **available now, including at multiphysics seams**; the per-agent $\eta_i$ needs a governing residual and therefore **inherits O2** |
| **W17** | Identified as the **consistent half of the time-axis transfer pair**, with $\sigma_{\text{time}}$ defined and shown not to be reduced by iteration. R3's `bc_time_varying` is its precondition — an expert declaring `false` has a floor on $\sigma$ that no scheme removes |
| **W18** | Gains a **checkable identity**, $s_e s_f=s_P$ per port, and scale-set completeness becomes **decidable at compile time from the port list alone** |
| **W12** | **Promoted, and on both goal lists.** Passivity is the *only* property that survives an expert swap without re-certification ([[plug-in-composition-theorems]] §2.4), so a declared storage function is the enabling condition of plug-in, not only the structural route to $L\le1$ |
| **W13** | Gains **composition semantics**: a declination halts the rollout and types the claim on $[0,t_{\text{decline}})$; a fallback expert is a mid-rollout substitution and reconciles as a restart with $e^0\neq0$, sharing machinery with W31 |
| **W19** | Gains a **criterion**: rank the feasible set by composability $\Xi$, tie-break on $\tau$, and **refuse on an empty feasible set** rather than falling back to the nearest expert. Static routing is compile-time; dynamic routing is a topology mutation |
| **W4** | The emit set grows again — a **depth tag** on every defect (W34), $\lVert\Delta\rVert$ and $\beta$ on any swap (W35), and per-expert $p$ (W36) |
| **W9** | The record's definition of done gains the **conformance certificate**: it is not enough to declare the fields, the declaration must be measured against the expert |
| **W16** | Unchanged in status. Gains only a **measurable form** for the criterion, $\mathcal Q(\Gamma)$, explicitly flagged as a guess at the scalarization. **The flag was pointing at the right thing: the scalarization is exactly the part that turned out to be wrong** (Tier 10), and the measurable form that replaced it is the restriction-defect bound |

> **The honest accounting.** Two pages of theory, and the worklist grows by four rows. Everything else was already tracked and is now *smaller* — three Tier-1/Tier-2 rows collapse into one declaration, and two more turn from open questions into written rules. That is the expected shape when the missing artefact was a unification rather than a mechanism.

# Tier 7 — what the implementation found (opened 2026-08-27 by `atlas/`)

**Read the status column above with this section next to it.** The specification was implemented end to end on 2026-08-27 (`atlas/`, 23 modules, 120 tests). Nineteen rows moved off `open`, and **the two statuses mean different things**:

- **`done`** — the definition of done is a property of the *compiler* and the compiler has it: **W5**, **W8** and **W11** (one declared prolongation per side per seam, with the mapping class, the reduction and adjointness all derived, and a compile-time refusal when the declaration is absent), **W10** (a new case compiles with no hand-written coupling config, inadmissible schemes refused citing the rule number, and the stamp emitted), **W18** (scale-set completeness decidable per port type, plus the identity $s_e s_f=s_P$), **W34** (a depth tag on every emitted defect).
- **`in progress`** — the **mechanism exists and is tested, and the measurement it was opened for has not been taken.** No expert in the library declares a capability record, no probe has been run against a real checkpoint, and $L$ is still unmeasured. Every one of these rows is now blocked on a *measurement* rather than on a *construction*, which is a different and much cheaper kind of blocked.

**Tier 6 has no status column; its state is:** W33 **`done` 2026-08-28** (run on five real records; see Tier 12, and note that its first run found W63), W34 `done`, W35 `in progress` (the certificate is implemented and untested on a real swap), W36 `in progress` (the abstention horizon is implemented; $p$ is undefined until W13 supplies a `validity` predicate).

**W1, W2, W3 have not moved and could not have.** They are measurements, the implementation is not one, and nothing below re-prioritizes them.

---

## Six new items, from the seven places the theory was wrong

Full reasoning in [[log]] under *"The specification is implemented"*. Each is a correction to a page rather than a new mechanism, all but W43 are small, and **all six were closed the same day they were opened** — read the closing note below for what that does and does not mean.

| # | Where | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W39** | [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §1, §5.3 | **Separate the envelope stamp from the gate verdict.** A seam can satisfy E2 — static and geometrically coincident — and still be refused at L3 for want of the declaration the 2026-08-27 amendment requires. Likewise a refusal for a *missing* declaration must leave E7 `unchecked`, not `fails`: only a *measured* non-adjoint pair or negative passivity mode fails it | Both rows say which of the two they are setting; §13.1's stamp is reproducible from §1 and §5.3 without contradiction | small — two clarifications | `done` — [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §1.1 states the rule (*only a measurement writes `fails`*), and §1's E2/E7 rows and §5.3's two rows now name which of the two they set |
| **W40** | [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §2 | **Correct the layer ordering.** The transmission rung is decidable from L1's records alone and it *fixes the decomposition axis*, which decides whether cross-points exist. Deciding it at L5 lets a compile check cross-points against the declared axis and then silently switch to the one that has them | The stack reads L1 → rung → L2, and §4.3's cross-point trade cites the ordering it implies | small | `done` — §2.1 carries the dependency chain and the wind-farm worked case; L1's row produces the rung, L2's consumes it, and §6.2 assembles at a rung already fixed |
| **W41** | [[interface-transfer-theory]] §7 | **The expected null-space dimension is a property of the assembled seam.** At a field↔lumped seam the lumped side responds in the direction incompressibility leaves open, so the expected count there is $0$, not $1$. Declaring it per port type produces a false defect report on every such seam. Add the *deficit* case, which no page gives a verdict for | The count is stated per seam as a function of which agents meet there; a measured deficit is a decertification and an excess a refusal | small | `done` — [[interface-transfer-theory]] §7 carries $n_0(\Gamma)$ and a three-row verdict table; [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.1's *"not one-dimensional"* check, which was the origin, is now stated per seam |
| **W42** | [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §4.2 | **Give the passivity defect a noise floor.** $\pi_i=\lvert\min(0,\lambda_{\min})\rvert$ taken literally reports arithmetic noise as a physical defect on every symmetric operator the probe assembles — measured at $1.3\times10^{-15}$ on a matrix positive semidefinite by construction, which is enough to fail E7 on a clean graph | The defect is compared against a scale-relative floor, and the raw eigenvalue is reported alongside so the clipping is visible | tiny | `done` — [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §4.2 defines $\pi_i$ against $\varepsilon_i=c\,\varepsilon_{\text{mach}}\lVert S_i\rVert$ and emits $\mu_i$ raw |
| **W43** | [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §3.3 | **The $K$-threshold refusal is a gate that cannot fire.** L1 requires a refusal for any graph at or above the $K$ at which $P(\text{some agent OOD})\to1$, *"a threshold this spec does not set"*. Either set it, or restate the line as a decertification so the spec does not carry an unfireable rule | The threshold has a value with an argument behind it, or the rule is a decertification | medium — it is a research question wearing a rule's clothes | `done` **by the second disjunct** — §3.4 retracts the refusal, decertifies at every $K$ with $K$ reported, and names $K^\star(\epsilon)=\lceil\log(1-\epsilon)/\log(1-p)\rceil$ as what a measured $p$ (**W13**) would reinstate. *The threshold itself is still unset, and now says which measurement it waits on* |
| **W44** | [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §3.2 | **The two sides of an `ADVEC` seam must declare the same passenger list.** A multibond's passenger list is part of its type, so a disagreement is an unnamed sixth port type in disguise and belongs in a `PortAmendment`. This is the discipline W21 asks for, stated as a connection condition | The condition is written into §5's connection rule; a seam whose two sides disagree is refused | small | `done` — §5's rule now reads *"declare $P$ **with the same passenger list**"*, §3.2 gives the per-face argument from §7.1's agent $f$, the example record shows the per-face form, and [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] C1 checks it |

**Two smaller findings, folded into existing rows rather than opened as items.** A record declaring `differentiable: jvp` with no JVP callable is a contradiction *inside the declaration* and belongs among L1's refusals (**W9**). And the zero-probe term $\chi=-\sum_i\partial_n\mathcal E_i[0]$ is load-bearing at *runtime*, not only in the probe: with $\chi\equiv0$ the interface problem is homogeneous, the converged trace is trivially zero, and every seam diagnostic is vacuous (**W4**). The first is now [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §3.3's fifth refusal row. The second needed no page change — [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2 defines $\chi$ correctly and §5.2 already works the $\Lambda\equiv0$ case; it is a warning to whoever writes the next fixture, and it lives with W4.

# Tier 8 — what the first real measurement found (opened and mostly closed 2026-08-27 by [[tier0-measurements]])

**Four rows opened, four closed the same day, one new one opened by the closing.** W45, W47 and W48 are `done`; W46 is done as a *refusal* and open as a *construction*; W49 is new.

> **2026-08-28: the tier is closed.** W46's construction half and W49 are both `done`, and the closing opened **Tier 9** below rather than another row here. W45's own bug turned out to have a second instance one call site further on (`eps_tol`), which is recorded there.

> **Two new compiler rules came out of this tier and they are the substantive part.** **R10** refuses to decompose an agent that embeds a global elliptic solve — measured as 99.8% of the first defect, wearing a `tau` label. **R2b** gates probed-DtN on `time_discretization`, because flux balance is the interface condition of a boundary-value problem and an explicit macro-step poses none. Together with a **halo rule** (`stencil_radius * substeps_per_macro_step`) and a partition of unity that vanishes at each window's artificial edge, the composed macro-step improved **$220\times$**, the interface conditioning from $\kappa=21.7$ to $\kappa=1.20$, and the fitted $L$ went from disagreeing with the monolith's to **matching it to five decimal places**.
>
> **The methodological lesson outlasts the numbers.** The three ingredients — global elliptic part, wide enough halo, PoU vanishing at artificial edges — were each measured alone first, and each looked like a failure (28%, 0%, and *worse*). None is sufficient and all three are necessary. The control that should have been run first — four windows each covering the whole domain, which returns exactly $0.0$ — would have shown the harness was sound and sent the search to the right place immediately.

**The rows as opened:**

| # | Where | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W45** | `atlas/compiler.py`, `atlas/capability.py` | **A measured constant has no way into a compile.** `_Context.unmeasured()` hard-codes `["L", "sigma", "tau", "C_mu"]` and consults no record; `ExpertCapabilities.L_fitted` exists and **is read by nothing**. So $L$, $\tau$ and $\sigma$ are measured, written down, and the compile still reports them unmeasured — **W4 built an emit path and there is no ingest path** | A fitted constant declared on the record (with its probe state and its error bar) drops off `unmeasured()`, and L9 types the claim from it rather than from `UNTYPED` | small, and it is the difference between `admit-uncertified` and `admit` | **`done`** — `graph.MeasuredConstants` carries $L$, $\tau$, $\sigma$, $\gamma$, $C_\mu$, $\lVert\mathcal A\rVert$ with **probe state, scheme and depth**, `unmeasured()` consults it, and L9 types from a measured $L$ and stamps E5 with the horizon branch. Verified: five unmeasured constants go to zero and E5 flips `unchecked` to `holds` |
| **W46** | [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.3, §9 | **State the interface condition for a one-step map**, and add the test that catches a wrong one. Flux balance is a steady condition; substituting the reference's own trace must reduce the residual, and it does not | The page states the condition for an initial–boundary-value operator (time-integrated flux, per **W7**), and §9's table carries the reference-trace check as a named measurement | medium — it is the theory question **W17** answers | **`done` 2026-08-28, and not the way the row expected.** The refusal half was R2b. The construction half is closed by [[tier0-measurements]] §9.3 measuring that **there is no flux-balance interface condition to fix**: an overlapping decomposition has no common interface at which a flux jump is defined, each subdomain's artificial boundary being somewhere else. **The valid construction for an explicit one-step map is the overlapping halo update itself**, and its error bound is W49's $\Pi$. `probe.reference_trace_check` is the named measurement, in the package, tested; `probe.shared_layer_flux_sum` makes the $-\nu h\,\partial_{nn}w$ identity checkable |
| **W47** | [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.2 / §4.1 | **Pin the flux convention.** Two are proposed, they agree on the assembled seam and differ by $10^3$ per block, and the per-agent passivity verdict flips between them | One convention is normative, the other is marked as an equivalent seam-level variant, and §4.2's equivalence names which one it holds for | small — a decision, not a measurement | **`done`** — **§2.2's $\partial_n$ form is normative.** §4.1's is an *approximate* seam-level equivalent (exact only at a shared-layer seam; $3.9\times10^{-2}$ at a 21-cell halo) and a block-level trap: $\pi=0$ against $\pi=2.01$ on the same block |
| **W48** | [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §4.3, W33 | **Say which level a $\beta$/$\pi$ verdict is taken at.** *"Both are printable per port"* does not, and per-block numbers are misleading by two orders | The pages state that the verdict is the **seam's**; the conformance suite certifies on the seam and reports blocks as diagnostics | small | **`done`, and the case is stronger than the row assumed.** Under the corrected agent the seam is $\kappa=1.196$ and its downstream block $\kappa=7061$ — **$5900\times$**. The block pathology **survives** the R10 fix, so it is genuinely a property of being the outflow side, and a per-block gate would refuse a seam whose conditioning is essentially perfect |
| **W49** | [[master-error-bound]] §4 | **The $\sigma$ bound's product form is vacuous for an overlapping scheme.** Every factor is now measurable, and with $\tilde\Lambda=0$ (a halo scheme uses no interface operator) the bound reads $\sigma\le1.72$ against a measured $3.73\times10^{-8}$ — an overestimate of $4.6\times10^{7}$, i.e. $C_\mu^{\text{implied}}=2.2\times10^{-8}$. The bound has **no term for overlap width**, which is where a halo scheme's accuracy comes from | A $\sigma$ term valid for the overlapping branch, or an explicit statement that §4 bounds the substructuring branch only and a halo scheme is out of its scope. $C_\mu$ stays **unmeasured** meanwhile: back-fitting it from $\sigma$ would make the bound tautological | medium — it is a derivation, and the measurement that motivates it exists | **`done`** 2026-08-28 — **both disjuncts.** §4 is scoped to substructuring and [[master-error-bound]] **§4.1** carries the overlapping form $\sigma\le C_\mu\,\Pi\,\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$, with $\Pi=\max_j\sum_i\chi_{ij}\mathbf 1[b_i(j)\le d_i]$ the weight the assembly gives to cells a stale artificial-boundary datum can reach. **Bound holds on 16/16 configurations at $1.02$–$4.04\times$** against §4's $4.6\times10^{7}$. $C_\mu$ is **measured** — $[0.228,1.178]$, so $1.2$ — because *this* factorization keeps one constant while §4's needs $2.2\times10^{-8}$. The row asked for an overlap-width term and the measurement says that is the wrong term: **overlap width is a precondition, the partition of unity is the mechanism** |

---

**All six of Tier 7 are `done`, and `done` here means one specific thing:** the pages now say what the implementation does **in their own body text**, rather than in a note bolted beside a line that still says otherwise. The 2026-08-27 amendment blockquotes that carried these findings have been folded in and removed, the pages read correctly top to bottom, and the theory layer and `atlas/` no longer disagree anywhere. **W43 is the one to read carefully**, because it is done by its *second* disjunct: the unfireable refusal is gone and the row now names $p$ (**W13**) as the measurement that would restore it, but the threshold is still unset. *Retiring a rule that could not fire is not the same as building the gate it promised, and the row says which of the two happened.*

> **What the implementation did not find.** Nothing in the master bound, the port algebra, the three-verdict system, the transfer unification or the composition theorems needed changing. The unification of [[interface-transfer-theory]] §2 in particular holds up under construction and is the strongest single thing in the three pages: with one declared prolongation and the reduction derived, the reversed-mapping bug is **not expressible**, and reproducing the actuator disk's historical hand-written reduction requires deliberately reaching for an escape hatch that the conformance suite then catches.

# Tier 9 — what the second session found (opened and mostly closed 2026-08-28 by [[tier0-measurements]] §9)

**The last named hole is closed, the last unmeasured constant is measured, and every one of E1–E7 stamps `holds` for the `split-step` graph.** What stands between it and `admit` is a single **[AI Inference]** that was never derived (`G5/W16`), which is a theory-status item rather than a missing measurement — a different kind of end state from anything this project has had before.

> **Closed the same day by Tier 10 below, and the end state moved again.** `G5/W16` is derived (**L2/C2**) and the scalarization carrying it is falsified, so **`split-step` now compiles to `admit`** — zero refusals, zero decertifications, zero unmeasured constants. Reaching it exposed **W56**: `unmeasured` was reported by every compile and gated nothing, which no graph could reveal for as long as every graph carried a decertification anyway.

> **Two rules and one retraction are the substantive part.** **R11** refuses a non-convex partition of unity, because convexity is *necessary and sufficient* for the blend to be at least as accurate as the local solves it blends — and nothing else detects its failure: the identity check passes, both blend-defect definitions read as passing, and the composed step is $166\times$ worse. **R10b** requires the composition layer to apply an exposed elliptic part at the agent's own sub-step cadence; a factor-two mismatch costs two orders of magnitude in $\tau$ with every other diagnostic healthy. And the **time-integrated interface condition W7/W17 promised was built and does not work**, for a reason that is an identity rather than a subtlety.

# Tier 10 — the decomposition policy, closed both ways (2026-08-28, second session; [[tier0-measurements]] §10)

**`G5/W16` is closed, and `compile_scheme` returns `admit` for the first time.** The row asked for a decomposition policy and the vault had adopted one as an **[AI Inference]**: *"cut where the exact operator is closest to local and the conditioning is comfortable."* Tier 9 recorded it as the single thing standing between `window_ns` in `split-step` mode and `admit`, and as a **theory-status item that no amount of running the case study could close**. It is closed by doing both of the two things that could close it.

> **Derived, and falsified, and they are different halves.** The **locality** half is now **L2/C2**, a theorem: the composed defect over one exchange interval is exactly $\sum_iR_i^\top\chi_iD_i$ with $D_i=\mathcal E_iR_i-R_i\mathcal E$ the *restriction defect*, and L6/C1's convexity bounds it cellwise by $\sum_i\chi_i\lvert D_i\rvert$. The **conditioning** half is **wrong**: the scalarization $Q=\beta^{-1}\lVert S-\operatorname{diag}S\rVert/\lVert S\rVert$ ranks cut placements at $-0.853$ against the measured composed defect, costs $92\%$ of the available range when followed, moves by $361\times$ under a re-declaration of the same interface space that leaves the scheme bit-identical, and is constant to $8\times10^{-11}$ where the truth spreads $2.29\times$. **The un-normalized off-diagonal mass ranks correctly at $+0.853$, and both of the operations $Q$ performs on it invert the sign.**

> **The instrument is the finding's other half.** No graph in this vault could run this experiment: the four-window tiling's cut is at the centre by construction. `tests/strip_model.py` is a linear advection-diffusion torus with a movable cut and everything else held fixed --- **neither a fixture nor a case study**, and it earns nothing on Tier 0. It is a model problem for a *rule*, which is what `test_tier9_assembly_condition.py` was for L6/C1 and what this tier needed for L2/C2.

| # | Where | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

The two are **provably equal** only when the agents' contaminated sets are pairwise disjoint; on the four-window tiling they are not, and the surrogate agrees to $1.00007$ anyway | The record carries which of the two was measured, and the compile reports whether the disjointness condition that makes them equal holds --- it is decidable from the `contaminated` geometry already declared for W49's $\Pi$ | small --- a provenance field and a geometric check | **`done` 2026-08-31, and larger than the row.** The field, four named forms and the compile-time disjointness check are built — and the measurement found a **second hypothesis nobody had stated**, the surrogate’s *aggregation*, which moves the ratio by 3x where the stated one moves it by 4%. # Tier 11 — W55 closed: three experts, and the rule a learned operator broke (2026-08-28; [[tier0-measurements]] §11)

**W55 was the largest standing caveat on this project and it is closed.** Two more real experts now exist: `reference.ChannelNS`, a **second discretization** (advective-form advection, rolled Laplacian, and `pressure.project_outflow` — a projection with an outlet Dirichlet and therefore **non-singular**, where `WindowNS`'s all-Neumann one is singular in the constant mode); and **Poseidon-T**, a frozen **20.8M-parameter neural operator**, which is not a discretization at all.

> **Every rule held. One rule's *implementation* did not, and only the third expert could have found it.** **R10, R10b, R11/L6/C1 and L2/C2 all survive a second discretization**, two of them with numbers that transfer: L2/C2's bound is tight to $0.17\%$ on `ChannelNS` against $0.20\%$ on `WindowNS`, and the composed $L$ matches its own monolith to $0.00$ standard errors on both. **$\Pi$ transfers exactly** — both limits — because it is computed from the declaration and is not a measurement of the expert. **$C_\mu$ transfers as a *bound* and not as a *calibration*.** And **W61**: R2b left the transmission rung lifted for an agent declaring `time_discretization=unknown`, producing a **runnable probed-DtN scheme** — the construction §5 measured at $8.9\times$ worse than doing nothing with every diagnostic reading healthy.

> **R10 is the sharpest single result, because it was set up to be able to fail.** §4.1 identified the elliptic defect by a specific mechanism: `WindowNS`'s all-Neumann pressure solve is singular in the constant mode and needs the ring's net flux to balance, which a subdomain of a through-flow violates. **`project_outflow` has no compatibility condition at all** — the imbalance simply leaves. R10's measured *mechanism* is absent on the second expert and R10's stated *reason* is untouched, and $\tau$ is **flat in $\Delta t$ to a ratio of $1.05$ over a $25\times$ range** anyway. **The rule generalizes past its own evidence**, which is the first time that has been shown on this project.

| # | Where | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W55** | everything | **One expert.** $C_\mu=1.2$, $\Pi$'s calibration and R10b's cadence rule are measured against `reference.WindowNS` and its no-projection subclass only, plus one `SpectralNS` control | A second real expert — a different solver class, or a trained checkpoint with a boundary channel — reproducing $\Pi$'s two limits and a $C_\mu$ inside the stated range | large | **`done` — with two experts rather than one, and the row's own definition of done is met with one qualification.** $\Pi$'s two limits reproduce **exactly** ($0.750$ at ramp 1, $0.020$ at ramp 21). $C_\mu$'s implied range is $[0.088,0.708]$ against $[0.228,1.178]$: **overlapping, and $1.2$ bounds all 22 configurations of both experts** — so it is inside the stated range as a bound and outside it as a calibration, and the row could not have anticipated the distinction because one expert cannot exhibit it |
| **W61** | `atlas/compiler.py`, `atlas/cases/wind_farm.py` | **R2b left the rung lifted on `time_discretization=unknown`.** The decertification ended *"It is not assumed to"* and the code assumed it: rung `probed-DtN`, axis non-overlapping, **runnable** | The rung is held at `dirichlet` for `unknown` as it is for `explicit`, the decertification stays (unlike `explicit`, nothing is *established*), and both fixtures declare the field they were getting by default | small to fix, and it is the silent-wrongness class | **`done`** — 5 tests. **Why nothing found it:** `window_ns` and `channel_ns` declare `explicit`; the two fixtures declared nothing and `unknown` is the field's **default**, so the fixture whose documented purpose is the rung-lift chain was getting its lift from an *omission*. Poseidon-T is the first expert for which `unknown` is the honest answer |
| **W59** | `atlas/capability.py`, [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.2 | **`BCChannel` cannot express a channel that is an initial condition.** A frozen one-shot map has nowhere to hold a trace *during* the step, so the ring is written into the initial condition instead — strictly weaker than a Dirichlet BC, and **not the object §2.2's Steklov–Poincaré operator is defined from**. The record says `DIRICHLET` because there is nothing else to say | A value for it, and §2.2 stating which of the two its $\Lambda_i$ is | small — an enum value and a paragraph | `open` — **sharpened 2026-08-29, see Tier 13**: the trigger is exact (any learned expert declaring `time_discretization=implicit`) and W61 currently masks it. The size of the difference is measured: $\Xi=0.0061$ against a solver's Dirichlet ring on the same window and state |
| **W60** | `atlas/capability.py`, `atlas/cases/poseidon.py` | **`EllipticSubsolve` has no `unknown`**, so a black-box checkpoint's record cannot be honest: `none` asserts something unmeasured *and switches R10 off*, `embedded` asserts something unmeasured *and makes L2 refuse* | An `unknown` value that decertifies, and `elliptic_signature` promoted from a diagnosis to a gate once it has more than two calibration points | small for the enum, medium for the gate | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 13**: the `unknown` value is added and the *gate* is refused, because the extra calibration points falsify `elliptic_signature` (**W68**). When opened it was **partly pre-empted**: an incompressible `governing_family` with `elliptic_subsolve=none` is already challenged rather than believed. `poseidon.elliptic_signature()` measures the statistic ($\kappa$, asymmetry) against §8.3's two calibration points; Poseidon-T reads $\kappa=9.41$, asymmetry $0.272$ — **above the embedded point on asymmetry**, and it is a **diagnosis and not a proof** |
| **W62** | `atlas/cases/channel_ns.py` | **`ChannelNS`'s constants are measured and not declared.** $L=0.979793$, $\tau=5.8732\times10^{-7}$, $\sigma=8.6623\times10^{-7}$, $C_\mu\le0.708$, `cut_defect_bound` $=2.5433\times10^{-7}$ all exist in §11 and the record carries `measured=None` | The record declares them with provenance and the graph reaches `admit`, in the same commit that re-measures them | small | `open` — deliberately not taken in the commit that measured them, for §9.1's reason about ramp 21: a number and the page that scopes it should land together |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

| | | |
|---|---|---|
| **R10** | was justified by a compatibility violation | **survives its absence.** The rule is about the elliptic operator being global; the Neumann compatibility condition was one solver's route to expressing that |
| **R10b** | measured at ~$200\times$ in $\tau$ on `WindowNS` | **$1231\times$ on `ChannelNS`.** Same rule, harder consequence |
| **§8.3's near-self-adjointness** | read as the signature of the exposed elliptic part | **partly the advection form.** `ChannelNS` reproduces $0.0038$ across the flow and $0.29$ along it, because the skew form is antisymmetric by construction and the advective form is not. Only a second discretization could separate them |
| **§1's probe floor** | *"machine epsilon, not OP-6's $10^{-6}$"* | **correct, and scoped to a float64 solver.** On the checkpoint the floor is $\sim10^{-4}$ and OP-6's $10^{-6}$ is right to a factor of $1.5$ |
| **$\beta$** | the substructuring amplifier, retired as a cut criterion by §10.2 | **and the least measurable quantity on a learned expert**: it moves $2.2\times$ across an $\epsilon$ window where $\lVert S\rVert$ is stable to $0.5\%$, because it inherits the whole noise floor |
| **W45 / W52 / W56** | *"a measured value exists and one call site does not consult it"*, three times | **W61 is the neighbouring failure: a default that reads as permission.** `unknown` should be a field's most conservative value and it was its most permissive |

# Tier 12 — the topology: W33 run, W6 treated, the port algebra pointed at real ports (2026-08-28; [[tier0-measurements]] §12)

**Three rows had the same shape: the machinery exists and has never been pointed at anything real.** W33's conformance suite was built with every field's test implemented and had never certified an expert. W6's cross-point rule was written and conditional on the decomposition, with a definition of done — *"a test on a 4-agent junction showing $\beta$ does not collapse"* — that had never been run on any graph. W5's `ADVEC` passengers, `ROT` port and field-to-lumped seam were fixture-tested only.

> **All three ran, all three produced findings, and none of them needed anything new built.** They needed existing machinery aimed at a real object. Between them: **two live defects** (`W63`, `W66`), **one rule interaction** (`W65`), **one interface gap** (`W64`), and W6's own stated test **passing** while the defect a cross-point actually causes turned out to be something else entirely.

| # | Where | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W33** | `atlas/conformance.py`, [[plug-in-composition-theorems]] §3 | **Run the conformance suite.** One probe per expert certifies `bc_channel` ($\Xi_i>0$), `storage` ($\lambda_{\min}$ of the symmetric part) and `equivariances` | Every expert in the library carries a certificate; a record whose measured value contradicts its declaration is **refused at admission**. `validity` records *"not falsified on suite X"*, never *"valid"* | small — rides entirely on W2's probe | **`done`** — five real records (`WindowNS` embedded and exposed, `ChannelNS`, Poseidon-T, `SpectralNS`) **plus one that lies**. The liar is the same expert with one field changed, `bc_channel` claiming `DIRICHLET` where $\Xi=0$, and it is **refused at admission**. `validity` decertifies on all six, which is the correct answer and not a gap. **And the first run found W63** |
| **W63** | `atlas/conformance.py` | **The suite refused on the BLOCK where W48 says the SEAM decides.** W48 closed with *"the conformance suite certifies on the seam and reports blocks as diagnostics"* and the suite did not. Poseidon-T: block passivity defect $3.218\times10^{-3}$, **seam defect $0$** — a record refused for not being passive, on an expert whose seam is passive | `seam_defect` supplied $\Rightarrow$ the verdict is the seam's, block a diagnostic; absent $\Rightarrow$ the block number is reported and the field **decertifies** | small | **`done`** — 3 tests. **The fourth instance of one pattern and a new sub-species of it**: W45, W52, W56 and W61 were about a measured constant or a default; this is about a **level**, and the page said which level for a whole session before the code did |
| **W6** | [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]], `scripts/w6_cross_point.py` | **Cross-point rule.** Decide and document the convention for 3+ subdomains meeting | A written rule, plus **a test on a 4-agent junction showing $\beta$ does not collapse** | medium | **`done`, and the test's answer is not the interesting part.** $\beta$ does **not** collapse — $\min_s\beta_s=\beta_{\text{global}}=2.8773\times10^{-1}$, collapse $1.00\times$, with a real $1.8\%$ off-diagonal coupling that a per-seam probe cannot see. **The defect a cross-point actually causes is multi-valuedness**: the shared cell gets one value per seam, and they differ by $1.62\%$ of the trace scale in $u$ and **$66.7\%$ in $v$, disagreeing about the sign**. It is a property of the **declared interface space**, not of the expert, so no better probe removes it. The primal treatment costs $64\to61$ multipliers and leaves $\beta$ unchanged, because $\beta$ was never the problem |
| **W64** | `atlas/capability.py`, [[interface-transfer-theory]] | **The cross-point coupling is not expressible through `boundary_response`.** The declared signature is *"trace on $V_i$ $\to$ flux on $V_i$"*, and a cross-point is the off-diagonal block $\partial(\text{flux on }B)/\partial(\text{trace on }A)$ for two faces of **one** agent. No sequence of single-port calls produces it — each call restarts from the base state — so a per-seam assembly is **block-diagonal by construction** and $\beta_{\text{global}}$ equals $\min_s\beta_s$ for a reason that has nothing to do with the physics | A multi-port response on the record, or an explicit statement that cross-point coupling is out of the probe's scope and what that costs | medium — it is a `PortAmendment`-sized decision on the record | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 13**, decided against extending the record. Until then, **the vault had been refusing on cross-points it could never have measured.** `scripts/w6_cross_point.py`'s `respond_multi` is the generalized probe, deliberately in the harness rather than the package |
| **W5** | `atlas/cases/wind_farm_real.py` | **`ADVEC` passengers, a `ROT` port and a field-to-lumped seam, against a real expert** rather than a fixture | The fixture's topology with real `boundary_response`s, compiling | medium | **`done`** — eight agents, **11 `MECH` + 4 `ADVEC`** edges, four `ADVEC` ports carrying a real `h0` passenger (agent `N` on **two different faces** — the per-face case a per-agent list cannot express), **two `ROT` ports, `OUT`, unconnected**, six `reference.WindowNS` windows and two `disk.ActuatorDisk` rotors. `split-step` compiles with **zero refusals**. **The geometry is schematic and says so**: a number measured there is about the port algebra, not about wakes |
| **W66** | `atlas/ports.py`, [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] | **The scale set is checked and the callable is not.** An `ADVEC` port returning $(\mathbf u\!\cdot\!\mathbf n)h_0$ — the enthalpy *power* — against a velocity trace made **E7 fail** with a passivity defect of $3.915\times10^{-2}$ on the assembled seam `e5a`. Returning $h_0$, the declared **effort**, makes E7 stamp `holds`. `check_scales` takes `(port_type, scales, passengers)`, never sees the callable, and `PortDecl` has no field naming which conjugate half the response is | A declaration of which half the callable returns, and an L3 check that the pairing is a power. **It cannot be inferred** — no property of the assembled operator says which physical variable its rows are in | small for the field, medium for the rule | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 13**, by declaration (`ResponseHalf`, L3/C9) after all three cheaper routes were measured and failed. When opened, **the case study was fixed and the gap was not.** This is §2.2 / W47 on a new port type: there a *transport* term reported a passivity defect of $2.39$ on a passive block, and the fix was to pin the diffusive form. An `ADVEC` port has **no diffusive form to fall back on**, so the conjugate pairing is the only defence and nothing validates it |
| **W65** | `atlas/compiler.py`, `CASE-STUDY-GUIDE.md` | **R10's plausibility guard misfires on an algebraic closure.** A rotor declares `elliptic_subsolve = none` (**true** — a closed-form zero-parameter law has no solve) and `governing_family = "incompressible-navier-stokes-2d"` (**required**, or E3 fails at every rotor face and $\tau$ goes `UNDEFINED`). R10 challenges exactly that pair | The guard distinguishes a solver from an algebraic closure, or the record can say which it is | small | `open` — it **decertifies rather than refuses**, which is what keeps it survivable, and it is a false positive on the two agents in the graph that are telling the truth. **The guide's own instruction is what walks every lumped closure into it** |

| **W47** | pinned the MECH flux convention because a transport term reported a false passivity defect | the same failure on `ADVEC`, where there is **no diffusive form to fall back on**. **W66** |

| **the fixture's topology** | assumed to be richer than `window_ns`'s | derived: **one** 3-clique, $\{N,F,B_p\}$ — the **same** cross-point count as the four-window tiling. The interesting structure is the port types |

# Tier 13 — the fifth expert, the pairing declared, and two rules exercised against a real disagreement (2026-08-29; [[tier0-measurements]] §13)

**§12.5 ended with a diagnostic and this tier is the test of it.** Four mechanisms produce the holes on this project, and only the first — *a theory rule with several call sites and one that does not consult it* — is found by auditing. The other three are found only by pointing unmodified machinery at an object nobody wrote with this framework in mind. **Mechanism 1 produced nothing this session, because there was nothing left to audit. Mechanisms 2, 3 and 4 produced everything below**, and all of it came from `solvers/compressible2d.py` and `solvers/thermostruct2d.py` in the build repo.

> **The fifth real case study.** `cases/thermal_seam.py` puts a compressible gas against a thermoelastic shell across one `THERM` seam — the first seam in this vault whose two sides do not solve the same equations, the first real expert whose `bc_channel` is above `dirichlet` (`ROBIN`), the first real `IMPLICIT` time discretization, and the first honestly `EMBEDDED` elliptic sub-solve that is not a pressure projection. Both solvers are imported **unmodified** and are graded against closed-form oracles by the build repo's own M1 suite. **The geometry is schematic and says so.**

| # | Where | Task | Definition of done | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **W55** (remaining half) | `atlas/cases/`, [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] | **One governing family.** All four real case studies are 2-D incompressible Navier–Stokes, so E3's `governing_family` comparison — which the spec calls load-bearing — has only ever been compared against itself, and every graph that fails it is a fixture built to fail it | A real seam where two genuinely different physics meet, confirming E3 fails cleanly, marks $\tau$ `UNDEFINED`, and the probe still runs because it references no governing equation | medium | **`done`** — a conjugate-heat-transfer seam, `compressible-navier-stokes-2d` against `thermoelastic-shell-2d`. **All three clauses hold**: E3 `fails`, $\tau$ `UNDEFINED` on the seam, and the probe assembles $\beta = 4.8062$, $\kappa = 1.000231$, $\dim\ker = 0$ on a graph with **zero refusals**. And a fourth thing nobody asked for: **the multiphysics seam is the best-conditioned interface problem in this vault**, $\kappa = 1.0002$ against `window_ns`'s $1.20$ and $21.7$ |
| **W66** | `atlas/ports.py`, `atlas/capability.py`, `atlas/admissibility.py` | **The scale set is checked and the callable is not.** `check_scales` takes `(port_type, scales, passengers)`, never sees the callable, and `PortDecl` has no field naming which conjugate half the response is | A declaration of which half the callable returns and an L3 check — **or** a derivation that something cheaper exists | small for the field, medium for the rule | **`done`, and the "something cheaper" question is answered with three measurements.** `ResponseHalf` + **L3/C9**: same half both sides `admit`, different halves **refuse** (the assembled operator would sum an effort and a flow), undeclared **decertifies**. **The positive control is the number**: before C9, `window_ns` — the only graph that reaches `admit` — compiled to **`admit`** with one side of every seam returning the *velocity* where its pair says *traction* |
| **W64** | `atlas/capability.py`, `atlas/compiler.py`, [[interface-transfer-theory]] | **Cross-point coupling is not expressible through `boundary_response`.** The vault has been refusing on cross-points it could never have measured | A multi-port response on the record, **or** an explicit statement that it is out of the probe's scope and what that costs | medium — a `PortAmendment`-sized decision | **`done` — decided against extending the record.** §12.2 measured what the extension would buy and it buys a diagnostic no rule consumes: the coupling is **1.8%** of the operator norm, $\beta$ does **not** collapse ($1.00\times$), and the real defect is multi-valuedness, which a *declaration* fixes and no probe does. `CROSS_POINT_COUPLING_SCOPE` states it permanently and both branches cite it. **And L2's refusal is rewritten**: it cited $\beta$, which §12.2 measured as unaffected; it now cites `cross_point_multivaluedness`, which is decidable from the declaration |
| **W57** | [[master-error-bound]] §4, `atlas/compiler.py` | **No cut criterion is derived for the substructuring branch.** L2/C2's hypotheses are a partition of unity and an assembly, and a non-overlapping decomposition has neither | A criterion derived from §4's own factorization — where $1/\beta$ genuinely *is* the amplifier — or an explicit statement that cut placement there is unconstrained | medium | **`done` — a theorem and its measurement.** **L2/C3**: $Q_{\text{sub}}(\Gamma) = \lVert S_M a^\star - \chi_M\rVert/\beta$, which is §4's chain stopped one step earlier, before the operator-mismatch bound. Over **11 configurations** of `tests/substructure_model.py`: ranks **positive in 11/11** ($+0.579$ to $+1.000$), tight to a factor of $1.7$, and following it costs at most $\mathbf{1.105\times}$ the best cut — against the retired $Q$'s **92%** of the available range. **Two further results**: $1/\beta$ *helps* here and inverted the ranking there, confirming §4's box by measurement rather than by assertion; and **§4's own product form ranks negative in 9 of 11**, so it is a bound and must never be a criterion — and it is the shape the retired $Q$ had |
| **W60** | `atlas/capability.py`, `atlas/cases/poseidon.py` | **`EllipticSubsolve` has no `unknown`**, so a black box cannot be declared honestly. The row proposed resolving it by promoting `elliptic_signature` from a diagnosis to a gate once it had more than two calibration points | An `unknown` value that decertifies, and the gate if the calibration supports one | small for the enum, medium for the gate | **`done` — the enum is added and the gate is *refused*, because the extra calibration points falsify it.** The shell's backward-Euler conduction is a **known** embedded elliptic part and the statistic reads $\kappa = 1.0002$, asymmetry $8.8\times10^{-5}$ — which its own thresholds call *"consistent with EXPOSED or NONE"* — and stays wrong across a $10{,}000\times$ range of exchange interval. **W68** |
| **W59** | `atlas/capability.py`, [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §2.2 | **`BCChannel` cannot express a channel that is an initial condition.** Measured at $\Xi = 0.0061$ on Poseidon-T | A value for it, and §2.2 stating which of the two its $\Lambda_i$ is | small | `open` — **deliberately not closed, and sharpened instead.** The bar was a second learned expert or a re-reading showing the enum load-bearing wrong; neither arrived. What did arrive is the **exact trigger**: `bc_channel` decides the rung *only* when `time_discretization = implicit`, and Poseidon declares `unknown`, so **W61's fix currently masks W59 entirely**. The two errors cancel and the cancellation is not stable |

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W68** | `atlas/cases/poseidon.py`, [[tier0-measurements]] §8.3 | **`elliptic_signature` measures non-normality and has been read as measuring globality.** They coincide for a pressure projection, where the elliptic part is a *constraint* that makes the interface operator strongly non-normal, and come apart for a **self-adjoint** elliptic operator: $(M/\Delta t + K)$ is SPD, so its Schur complement is near-normal *however global it is*. The class it cannot see contains every diffusion problem | **`done` 2026-08-30 — see Tier 14.** The diagnosis held and was one level too high: the per-agent block reads the same, because the block IS the film coefficient. Superseded by `open` — the statistic is kept as a diagnosis, must not gate, and §11.6's *"a diagnosis and not a proof"* was righter than it knew |
| **W69** | `atlas/ports.py`, `atlas/conformance.py` | **`response_half` is unverifiable, and is the second such declaration** after `validity`. §12.1 said the architecture rests on exactly one; it now rests on two. The conformance suite catches a lying `bc_channel` by probing ($\Xi = 0$) and **cannot** catch a lying `response_half` — §13.3's three legs are the proof that nothing can | **`done` 2026-08-30 — see Tier 14.** Audited: **three** unverifiable declarations, and it was three before W66. It is the first physics in this vault the vocabulary cannot express, and a sixth *port* type is not the fix, because the object is not a bond | **`done` 2026-08-30 — see Tier 14.** Reframed: the coupling is one expert's internals and one-way, so nothing needs a bond; the OUTPUT needs disclosure. Superseded by `open`, low priority — nothing rests on it, and §13.2's one-paragraph argument (both sides are near-multiples of the identity over one exchange interval) is a sketch and not a derivation |

Found the way W38 was: **one edit produced both signatures at once**, `\beta` into 0x08 (caught) and `\tau` into a tab (not). **And it is a mechanism-1 instance in the tooling rather than the theory**: W38 closed on 2026-08-27 prescribing a scan for `\t` among others, and `vault_scan.py`, written the next day, implemented every other byte in that list and not this one. A rule with two consuming sites and one that does not consult it | **`done`** — the tab check is added, the vault uses no tabs for any purpose so it has no false positives, and a planted-tab positive control is part of the fix |

# Tier 14 — locality, and the price of the boundary condition (2026-08-30)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §14. Reproduce with `python scripts/w73_locality_and_scope.py`; artifact `out/w73/w73.json`.

**Four of Tier 13's five open rows were one question.** *How much of a probed block is the expert's operator, and how much is the boundary condition sitting on top of it?* One statistic — $\omega = \lVert S - cI\rVert_F / \lVert S\rVert_F$, the identity defect — answers W68, W71, half of W69 and the framing of W70. Measuring it opened **W74**, which moves $\beta$ on a real seam by $12.6\times$.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Where | What it was | How it closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W68** | `atlas/cases/poseidon.py`, `atlas/probe.py` | `elliptic_signature` measures non-normality and was read as measuring globality | **`done`** — the diagnosis was right and one level too high. Measuring the shell's own block per-agent gives $\kappa = 1.0001$ too, because the block **is** $5.0001\,I$, its film coefficient, with the conduction operator $300\times$ underneath. The statistic was **swamped, not blind**, and no threshold could have helped. Fix: a **precondition** and a third outcome, `NO OPERATOR RESOLVED`, plus `L4/operator-content` decertifying a thin seam and naming $\beta$. Floor $10^{-3}$, calibrated at a $2370\times$ separation |
| **W71** | [[tier0-measurements]] §13.2 | a multiphysics seam is better conditioned than a single-physics one, explained only by a sketch | **`done`** — $\omega \approx C\,\mathrm{Bi}\,(k_{\max}\ell)^2$ with $C \in [0.135, 0.307]$ over **11 points and 5 decades of $\Pi$**, from two independent sweeps. The two groups are the two ways an operator can hide and **either alone suffices**, which is why §13.6's $10{,}000\times$ sweep of $\Delta t$ moved nothing: it held $\mathrm{Bi} = 0.033$ fixed. Conditioning at a seam is set by **locality**, not by whether the sides share equations |
| **W69** | `atlas/conformance.py`, `atlas/capability.py` | `response_half` is the second unverifiable declaration; §12.1 claimed one | **`done`** — audited, and it was **three** all along: `governing_family` is a free-form string that E3 compares and nothing tests, and a *matching false pair* makes E3 read `holds`. It **promotes** where the other two decertify. Two adjacent-but-testable fields now have tests (`deterministic`, `stencil_radius`), and the stencil test found `required_halo()` consulting two of the three fields it needs |
| **W70** | `atlas/cases/thermal_seam.py` | a thermoelastic body coupling has no port and a sixth port type is not the fix | **`done`** — reframed. **The coupling is not between two agents**: it is one expert's internals and one-way (`step_thermal` takes no mechanical argument), so nothing needs a bond and E7 against `storage` is not wrong. What is missing is **disclosure of the output**: $\delta\lVert\sigma\rVert/\lVert\sigma\rVert$ *rises* $5.6\times$ over the retained band to $1.74\times$ the certified flow's, while $\delta\lVert u\rVert/\lVert u\rVert$ falls $1207\times$ — so the truncation argument points the wrong way for the stress. `VOLUMETRIC_COUPLING_SCOPE`, not a field, because a field would be a fourth unverifiable |
| **W72** | `scripts/vault_scan.py` | the byte scan was necessary and not sufficient; the tab was added | **`done` a second time** — adding one byte to a hand-written list leaves a hand-written list. Now: the escape table is **derived from Python's decoder**, a `$`-parity symptom check catches the class **without any list** (including `\n`, which no byte rule can see), and a **generated positive control** per byte makes a missing detector a failing test. It found a **live corruption** on its first run, in the vault since 2026-08-26 |

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W74** | `atlas/probe.py`, `atlas/capability.py` | **The probe linearized about the zero trace**, which on a port whose effort is a temperature is $0$ K. Measured against the experts' own operating point, $\beta$ on seam `cht` moves $4.8062 \to 0.3807$ and $\lVert S\rVert_F$ moves $19.23 \to 1.52$ — a factor of $12.6$ — while the verdict and all **7** decertifications are identical. So the conclusions were robust and every *number* the certificate carried was not, with `probe_state` reading `"T_hot=900 K"` throughout. **The cause is the declared bond**: `PORT_SPECS[THERM]` pairs $(T, q_n/T)$ so that effort times flow is a power, and dividing by $T$ is what makes the response nonlinear; the pseudo-bond would have been affine. Two correct choices with incompatible hidden assumptions | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 15.** The field was on the wrong OBJECT: a base belongs to a seam, and this seam's two sides are 500 K apart. Superseded by **`done` for the instance, open for the class** — `ExpertCapabilities.probe_base` is added and defaults to zeros, so every earlier measurement reproduces bit-for-bit (verified: 6058 of 6059 numbers identical from cold, the difference being wall-clock). What is open is that **nothing detects the conflict**: no rule notices that a port spec's own choice of bond has invalidated a probe assumption |

The honest gate would need a probe at $\Pi \gtrsim 10^{-2}$, i.e. a longer exchange interval or a stronger coupling | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 15.** Reachable, and the spectral gate fails there anyway; the gate is a delta poke. Superseded by `open` — and it may be **unreachable**: the cadence that would resolve the operator is not necessarily one the composition can run, so this could be a permanent scope statement rather than a measurement |

On `window_ns`'s seam `sx0` the two blocks differ by $21\times$ in norm and $20\times$ in $\omega$ — resolved, stable to $1.0003\times$ over four decades of $\varepsilon$ — between two instances of **the same solver on a symmetric tiling**, and the assembled $\kappa = 1.196$ shows none of it | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 15.** 3486x in kappa, and the substitution certificate cannot see an under-responding swap. Superseded by `open` — mechanism 4: the assembled diagnostics were never shown to be jointly sufficient for the block ones. `alpha_star` in particular is *the measured symbol* and no rule has ever read it |
| **W77** | `atlas/conformance.py` | **`probe_state` is a free-form caller-supplied string**, and W74 is the proof it can be wrong while everything else is right: it read `"duct, T_hot=900 K, T_wall=400 K"` while the probe ran at $0$ K. The architecture's rule that *a certificate is valid at a state, never globally* is enforced by a label nothing checks against the probe | **`done` 2026-08-29 — see Tier 15.** Derived from the base; it removes a declaration rather than adding one. Superseded by `open` — a fourth undetectable declaration in everything but name, and unlike the other three it could be **derived** rather than declared, since the probe knows its own base |
