## The mechanism tally, and it is a finding

§12.5's diagnostic says mechanism 1 is found by auditing and mechanisms 2–4 only by contact. This tier ran on Tier 13's leftovers rather than on a new object, and the result sharpens the diagnostic rather than confirming it:

**Every hole closed here was reachable by auditing, and not one had been found that way.** `required_halo()` ignoring `time_discretization` is textbook mechanism 1 and sat through four audits; the probe's zero base is one line of code with a docstring that discusses the choice and omits the assumption. What made both findable was a measurement taken for an entirely different purpose. So: **auditing finds mechanism 1 once you know which rule to audit, and contact is what tells you which.**

# Tier 15 — what the probe assumed about its own base (2026-08-29)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §15. Reproduce with `python scripts/w78_base_blocks_and_reach.py`; artifact `out/w78/w78.json`.

> **Dating.** Tier 14 and its log entry are stamped `2026-08-30` and were written on **2026-08-29** — clock, environment and file mtimes all agree. Nothing is renamed; the log is append-only and rewriting it would hide the drift. This tier is dated by the clock, so the log reads 08-30 then 08-29.

Tier 14 closed W74's *instance* and left the class in one sentence: *nothing detects that a port spec's own choice of bond has invalidated a probe assumption*. Chasing it found the field Tier 14 added was **on the wrong object**, closed W77 with it, and inverted W75.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Where | What it was | How it closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W74** (class) | `atlas/probe.py`, `atlas/compiler.py` | nothing notices that a bond choice invalidated the probe's affine assumption | **`done`** — the field belongs to a **seam**, not an expert. $\Lambda_M = \sum_i P_i^{*}\Lambda_i P_i$ is a sum of Jacobians, which is a Jacobian only if the terms share a point, and `cht`'s two sides are **500 K apart** — the gas at the wall temperature it sees, the shell at the gas temperature it sees, each honest alone. The reported $\beta = 0.3757$ is attainable at **no** admissible interface state (the whole range is $[0.4200, 1.7689]$); at the consistent $\lambda^{*} = 371.97$ K it is $1.23807$, a further $3.30\times$ after Tier 14's $12.6\times$. `seam_base`, `base_disagreement`, `base_sensitivity`, `L4/probe-base` |
| **W75** | `atlas/conformance.py`, `atlas/probe.py` | `elliptic_subsolve` unmeasurable on a film-dominated seam; the fix may be an unrunnable cadence | **`done`, and the hypothesis was wrong twice.** The cadence *is* reachable — three of eight satisfy both requirements. And it does not help: on the same shell under two solvers `elliptic_signature` is identical at $\Delta t = 1$ and **ranked backwards** at $\Delta t = 100$ ($\kappa$: implicit $3.20$, explicit $9.67$). A Fourier basis cannot report support. **Poke a delta**: an implicit step inverts a sparse SPD matrix and the inverse is dense. $0.986$ / $0.210$ on `window_ns`'s two modes, right on all five known points, two solves, no spectral threshold — and it **resolves `UNKNOWN`**, which is what W60 asked for |
| **W76** | `atlas/probe.py`, `atlas/composition.py` | per-block $\beta$/$\kappa$/`alpha_star` computed and read by nothing | **`done`** — non-sufficiency is two lines ($\operatorname{diag}(1,10^{-12}) + \operatorname{diag}(0,1)$ is ideal); measured at $\mathbf{3486\times}$ in $\kappa$ and $22{,}500\times$ in $\beta$ on `sx0` under an assembled $\kappa = 1.196$; and the derivable consequence is that a substitution certificate **cannot see an under-responding replacement** when $\lVert S_i\rVert < \beta - \beta_{\min}$. Demonstrated by certifying an expert that ignores its boundary data entirely. `SubstitutionCertificate.blind` downgrades the pass; `L4/block-share` reads `alpha_star`, the first rule ever to |
| **W77** | `atlas/probe.py` | `probe_state` is a caller-supplied string nothing checks | **`done`** — **derived**, not declared. The probe knows its base and the base *is* the state: `gas@400; shell@900 INCONSISTENT(spread 223.6)` against a declared `"duct, T_hot=900 K, T_wall=400 K"` that was not wrong about the values, only about there being one of them. Removes a declaration rather than adding a fourth to W69's three |

Reported: the certificate exposes `visible_above` and `fails_above` and returns them instead of a verdict when no tolerance is supplied. | §14.9's *"the resolving cadence may be one the composition cannot run"* | §14.9 | **falsified.** Three cadences satisfy both requirements, and the gate fails there anyway — W75 |
| §14.9's *"the two blocks differ by $21\times$ in norm and $20\times$ in $\omega$"* | §14.9 | true and understated: $3486\times$ in $\kappa$, $22{,}500\times$ in $\beta$ — W76 |
| §14.8's `window_ns` split-step **`admit` 0/0** | §14.8 | `admit-uncertified` **0 refusals / 2 decertifications**. The vault has no clean `admit` any more, and the composition is untouched — what was decertified is a substitution claim that was never true |

## The mechanism tally, and it inverts Tier 14's own lesson

Tier 14 concluded that *auditing finds mechanism 1 once you know which rule to audit, and contact is what tells you which*. This tier had **no new object at all** — it ran on Tier 14's leftovers — and still found three defects by contact with a measurement taken for a different purpose: the base disagreement fell out of writing W74's class detector, the backwards ranking out of testing W75's reachability, the substitution blindness out of asking what per-block $\kappa$ is *for*. Reading the code and thinking about it found nothing, in either session.

**So the generalization is not about new objects. A rule becomes auditable only after some measurement has named it.**

# Tier 16 — multiphysics: what E3 was actually gating (2026-08-29)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §16. Reproduce with `python scripts/w83_multiphysics_attribution.py`; artifact `out/w83/w83.json`. New module `atlas/multiphysics.py`.

**The standing reason no multiphysics graph could be certified.** E3 compares two `governing_family` strings and, when they differ, $\tau$ is emitted as `UNDEFINED` for both sides. But $\tau$ is *measured* as the composed step given the true trace against a **reference trajectory**, and nothing in that mentions a governing equation. At a multiphysics seam the referent exists and is built from the agents themselves: the tightly coupled pair.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Where | What it was | How it closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W88** | `atlas/compiler.py`, `atlas/multiphysics.py` | E3's string comparison makes $\tau$ `UNDEFINED` at every multiphysics seam, so error attribution — and every rule downstream of it — is unavailable for the whole class | **`done`** — E3 was gating the wrong thing. Sharing a family is what lets two agents share a *monolithic* reference; $\tau$ needs a **reference pair**, and `lambda_ref` has declared exactly that since the end-to-end spec while being checked for presence and never consumed. Verified against surrogates with known error: $\tau$ recovers $5\%$, $25\%$ and $100\%$ as $0.0500$, $0.2500$, $1.0000$ **exactly**, per agent, with the unswapped side identically zero. `tau_undefined_seams` is empty on a multiphysics graph for the first time |
| **W89** | `atlas/multiphysics.py` | the master bound sums $\tau + \sigma + \gamma$ as scalars, and at a multiphysics seam there is no single norm — conserved variables against kelvin | **`done`** — the defect is measured in **interface power**, $\int_\Gamma e f$, which every port type already has because `PORT_SPECS` pairs its halves so effort times flow is a power. It is also the only norm that *works*: the gas's own state norm is **bit-identical across 100 K** of wall temperature over one macro-step while its flow moves 24%, so a defect measured there reads exactly zero. Inherits `response_half`, and refuses rather than guessing when it is undeclared |
| **W84** | `atlas/compiler.py` | — (opened and closed in the same tier) | **`done` for the degeneracy.** `eps_tol = min(tau, sigma)` sets the interface tolerance to $0$ once $\tau$ can be exactly zero, which first became possible here. A term that is identically zero names no scale, so the minimum is taken over the terms that carry one |

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W84** | `atlas/compiler.py` | the degeneracy is fixed and **`min` itself was never derived**. The right combinator for two defect terms in one norm is still a choice | `open` for the derivation |
| **W85** | `atlas/multiphysics.py` | the referent costs $2(n+1)$ solves per Newton iteration — 98 here, prohibitive for a learned expert at $128^2$. A scalar secant is not a substitute: it moves only a uniform shift of the trace and **stalls** at $3.04\times10^{-3}$ where Newton reaches $1.32\times10^{-7}$ in 9 iterations. The probed $S$ is the same object on $M$, but $M$ is sized for cheap transmission, not for a Newton solve | `open` — it decides whether multiphysics attribution is affordable for the learned experts this vault is for |
| **W86** | `atlas/graph.py` | $\sigma$ is a **function of the lag**, not a number: $1.0000$ at the initial condition and $3.392\times10^{-5}$ at the lag a real run carries, a $30{,}000\times$ range, linear only to leading order because interface power is bilinear. The certificate carries one value with the lag in a provenance *string* and nothing checks the run against it | `open` — the same shape as W77's `probe_state`, and possibly the same fix |
| **W87** | `atlas/conformance.py` | **`lambda_ref` is now load-bearing and has no test.** It gates whether $\tau$ is `UNDEFINED`; declared falsely it reports $\tau$ measurable when no referent can be built. Falsifiable but not verified — `validity`'s shape exactly, so a *fourth* declaration of that kind | `open` — and the test is cheap, since `tight_couple` either converges or it does not |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

| Claim | Where | Now |
|---|---|---|
| E3 fails $\Rightarrow$ $\tau$ is `UNDEFINED` for both sides | §12, `compiler._stamp_E3` | **wrong consequence, right hypothesis.** E3 still fails and always will; what it costs is the *monolithic* reference and nothing else — W88 |
| W66's choice of $(T, q_n/T)$ over $(T, q_n)$ | §13.3 | pays a second time: the pseudo-bond is not a power and would not have been a common norm. It also cost once, in W74 — the same choice makes the response nonlinear in the effort |
| `lambda_ref`, *"tau at a multiphysics seam"* | `capability.py`, since the end-to-end spec | consumed for the first time. It was the right field, written down and never read — W88 |
| §14.9's *"the rocket should not be the next build: three of the refusals are research"* | Tier 13/14 | **two of its five are now closed** (L1 and L4); multirate, interface motion, lumped-to-field and staging remain, and the assessment stands for those |

## The multiphysics ledger, stated plainly

| blocker | status |
|---|---|
| different governing families, $\tau$ `UNDEFINED` | **closed** |
| no commensurable norm for the defect | **closed** |
| multirate — R9 refuses unless the flux is matched time-integrated | `open` — `thermal_seam` at native clocks (500:1) still refuses on E4 |
| interface motion — a combustion front or plume boundary is not static | `open`, research |
| lumped-to-field coupling, $\dim M = 1$ | `open` |
| topology events (staging) | `open` |
| volumetric two-way coupling between two *agents* | **closed 2026-09-01 (Tier 22).** It has a **bond** — the conjugate pair $(\Delta T,\ \beta\,\mathrm{tr}\,\dot{\boldsymbol\varepsilon})$, with $G$ forward and $G^{\!\top}$ back — and it does **not** have a port, because the carrier has co-dimension zero and the two agents' free energies do not add |

**One of four foundational blockers closed, and it was the one that made the class structurally uncertifiable rather than merely unfinished.**

---

# Tier 17 — the referent's loose ends, and the multirate rule aimed at the smaller term (2026-08-30)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §17. Reproduce with `python scripts/w87_referent_cost_and_lag.py` and `python scripts/w7_multirate_matching.py`; artifacts `out/w87/w87.json`, `out/w7m/w7m.json`.

> **Dating.** Measured **2026-08-29**, written just after midnight and stamped by the clock as **2026-08-30**, per Tier 15's rule. The log therefore reads 08-30, 08-29, 08-30 across Tiers 14–17 and nothing is renamed.

Tier 16's three loose ends were all debt rather than theory, and **all three came back with an answer their row did not anticipate**. Then the multirate blocker: `thermal_seam` at `clocks="native"` compiled to `refuse` on `L7/R9` since it was written, and R9's refusal turns out to be aimed at the term $62\times$ smaller than the one it does not mention.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Where | What it was | How it closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W87** | `atlas/conformance.py` | `lambda_ref` is load-bearing, has no test, and is a *fourth* declaration of `validity`'s kind | **`done`, and it is not a fourth.** The string is not what the rule consumes — the rule consumes the claim that a **reference pair converges**, which is one `tight_couple`. `_test_lambda_ref` runs it and the count stays at three. **And the check sees exactly one thing**: run against four deliberately wrong pairs, **four of five converge** — a blind side and even a sign-flipped side still balance, at a different trace. What converging falsifies is that the interface problem is EMPTY, which is the claim `L1/E3` promotes on, and nothing more. The admissible interval is not a second discriminator either: measured, it separates one wrong pair by $1.0\times10^{-5}$ of the reservoir span. A false referent corrupts $\tau$'s **magnitude** ($2.0000$ for a sign flip) and not its **localization** |
| **W85** | `atlas/multiphysics.py` | the referent costs $2(n+1)$ solves; is the probed $S$ a good enough Jacobian, and does $\omega$ predict when? | **`done`, and the answer is no, for a reason no threshold can reach.** Five operating points, $\omega$ across $17.8\times$: **not one of ten $S$ rows converges.** The step $PS^{-1}Rr$ lives in $\operatorname{range}(P)$, and $\dim M = 16$ against $\dim V = 48$ leaves 32 directions untouchable — measured, the in-subspace residual goes to $10^{-13}$ and the plateau **is** the orthogonal part, $1.04\times10^{-4}$, invariant across the whole $\omega$ range. **No floor is licensed and none would help.** What $S$ is worth is a warm start at $4.8\times$ fewer solves, and only at a consistent seam base: at the split base it is $245\times$ worse. `seam_jacobian` exposes it; the default stays dense FD; a $\dim M$ operator handed to a $\dim V$ Newton is now refused rather than silently becoming a secant |
| **W86** | `atlas/graph.py`, `atlas/multiphysics.py` | $\sigma$ is a function of the lag and the certificate carries one number with the lag in a provenance string | **`done`, and neither of the row's two options was right.** The **slope** is a property of the seam ($3.45001$–$3.45013\times10^{-2}$ K$^{-1}$, five digits over five steps); the **lag** is a property of the run ($3.43\times$ range, non-monotone) and is **not derivable at compile time** — W77's fix does not transfer, because the probe had run and the trajectory has not. And a scalar-lag check cannot certify $\sigma$ either: at drifts agreeing to $8\%$, $\sigma$ differs by $1.48\times$, because its argument is the lag *profile*. Fix: `sigma_lag` typed, `lag_distance` derived by `rollout` from traces it already carried, `check_sigma_lag` at a $2\times$ provenance tolerance that can falsify and cannot confirm. **The declared constant was a uniform-shift extrapolation** — it reproduces as slope $\times$ lag — and its `source` named an artifact carrying a different number |
| **W7** (multirate half) | `atlas/compiler.py`, `atlas/graph.py`, `atlas/capability.py`, `atlas/solve.py` | *"R9's time-integration remains right for what R9 is about — multirate — and the two-rate test is still owed"* (2026-08-28) | **`done` as a test, and the test is a negative.** One 500-step march, sliced: the leak is **$0$ exactly at ratio 1** (the control) and $8.6\times10^{-5}$ at $500{:}1$. In interface power over one interval, **R9's term is $5.9972\times10^{-5}$ and the stale-trace term is $3.7431\times10^{-3}$ — a factor of $62.4$.** R9 has refused every multirate graph over the smaller one. The rule is still right in principle, so `FluxMatching` supplies the declaration the refusal always named, L7 checks that the clocks nest and that both sides supply `boundary_response_integrated`, `_port_fluxes` calls it, and `L7/R9/lag` decertifies beside the admission carrying the $62.4$ |

See **W92** — a *run* has the consistent base for free |
| **multirate** (§16.7 ledger) | `open` — *"`thermal_seam` at native clocks (500:1) still refuses on E4"* | **the graph compiles** (`admit-uncertified`, 0 refusals), and the ledger row was **aimed at the wrong term**. Replaced by **W90** |

| **W90** | `atlas/compiler.py`, `atlas/scheme.py` | **The multirate defect has no rule.** R9 covers the flux transient and is $62\times$ too small; the lag over a long exchange interval is $\sigma$, and nothing bounds it as a function of that interval. R4 forbids shrinking the interval below $\max_i \Delta t_i$, so the obvious remedy is the one axis a frozen expert cannot move. The candidate is **W17's $W > 1$**: a trace carried as a waveform is exactly a trace that is not stale | `open` — and it is the *named* multirate blocker now, which the ledger row was not |

What the shape costs is unmeasured; $1.48\times$ between two steps whose scalar lags agree to $8\%$ is a lower bound from two points | `open` |

A **run** does not have to derive it: the previous macro-step's converged trace *is* the consistent base, and `rollout` already carries it (W86 made it read it). Nothing feeds it to `assemble_seam` | `open`, and cheaper than W80 assumed |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

| Claim | Where | Now |
|---|---|---|
| W87 is a *fourth* unverifiable declaration | Tier 16 | **no.** A declaration that names an experiment is an unrun experiment — W77's lesson generalizes past "derive it" |
| *"the probed $S$ is the obvious cheap substitute"* | `numerical_jacobian`, Tier 16 | the obstruction is **dimensional**, not spectral: $\dim M < \dim V$, measured to every digit — W85 |
| $\sigma = 3.392278\times10^{-5}$ *"at the lag a real run carries"* | §16.5, `MEASURED_W83` | a uniform-shift value at an assumed drift; the run's own is $4.07\times10^{-6}$ to $3.13\times10^{-5}$ — W86 |
| §9.3's *"the two-rate test is still owed"* | Tier 9 | **paid**, and it says R9's premise is true and its magnitude is $62\times$ short |

## The mechanism tally

Tier 15: *a rule becomes auditable only after some measurement has named it.* This tier sharpens it. **All four findings came from measuring the object a rule is stated about, and in three of the four the rule's premise was true and its magnitude was wrong** — W87's check works and sees one case in five, $S$ is the right operator in the wrong subspace, R9's integral is the conserved quantity and is $62\times$ too small to matter here. Only W86's constant was wrong on its own terms.

> **A rule can be correct, derived, and aimed at the wrong order of magnitude, and nothing but a measurement in a common norm will say so.** Interface power has been that norm since §16.3, and the multirate ledger row has stood since 2026-08-27 because nobody put the two terms in it.

# Tier 18 — real turbine geometry, and the attribution machinery on a frozen expert (2026-08-30)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §18. Reproduce with `python scripts/w93_wake_array.py --steps 60`; artifacts `out/w93/w93.json`, `out/w93/state.npz`. New case study `atlas/cases/wake_array.py` — the **sixth** real one, and the first whose *geometry* is real.

> **Dating.** Measured and written **2026-08-30**, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

Tiers 14–17 built an attribution stack and every number it ever produced came from a two-agent duct or from one solver tiled against itself. **None of it had been pointed at a real pretrained checkpoint inside a real composed flow.** This tier does that on the problem operators pay for: three turbines at $3.5\,D$ in an L, six Poseidon-T windows, three zero-parameter actuator disks, a wake, and a power loss.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Where | What it was | How it closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W93** | `atlas/capability.py`, `atlas/compiler.py` | *(opened and closed in this tier)* `required_halo` returns `stencil_radius * substeps` for an agent that has not said its step is explicit, and `L2/R10`'s halo rule believes it | **`done`, and it is W69's own derivation reaching a third case.** W69's sentence is *"that product is an EXPLICIT agent's domain of dependence"*; it fixed the `implicit` branch and left the branch where the record says nothing — which is what a frozen learned one-shot map declares, under R2b, because it is neither. Measured on Poseidon-T: the record says **2 cells**, `probe.support_reach` — the same quantity, by poking a delta — reads **64**, nonzero in all 128 seam cells at every amplitude from $1$ to $10^{-3}$, and the graph's 16-cell overlap had been PASSING on the 2. `required_halo` now returns `None` there and `_halo_rule` decertifies naming the instrument. **A neural operator's receptive field is global by construction**, so the fix is not a bigger integer — there is no integer |

## Rows this tier moves

| # | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| **W85** | *"the referent costs $2(n+1)$ solves — prohibitive for a learned expert at $128^2$"* | **the price was the wrong question and the cost was mispriced in the other direction.** `seam_defect_split` converges the *reference* pair and evaluates the actual pair four times, so with a classical referent the 194 solves are paid at $0.04\,$s each and the checkpoint gives four forward passes. And the referent **does not exist** on the checkpoint's own pair — see W95 |
| **W76** | the substitution blind spot, established on one asymmetric tiling | a property of a **port-type pairing**: every field-to-lumped `MECH` seam is one-sided by the cell Reynolds number, before any expert is chosen — see W97 |
| **W80 / W92** | the seam base is per expert, and nothing feeds a consistent one to `assemble_seam` | **evidenced.** The absolute-trace convention makes the base visible and `L4/probe-base` fires on **10 of 13 seams**, up to $123\%$ of the base norm — the first time the check has said anything about a fluid seam. The three that agree are the three constructed to agree. W92's own fix (a run has the consistent base for free) is exercised by the driver and still absent from `rollout` |
| **W70 / §16.7's volumetric row** | *"the two-way one has no port and no bond"*, with no case behind it | **has the wind farm behind it now** — see W94 |

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W94** | `atlas/ports.py`, `atlas/graph.py` | **An actuator disk has no expressible bond against a frozen operator, by two independent routes.** A disk is a surface across which the traction jumps, and a surface bond needs the two fluid subdomains to *abut* — a non-overlapping cut — which `R2b` refuses for an agent whose `time_discretization` is `unknown` (**W61**). Under the overlapping axis the checkpoint does support, the disk sits inside the overlap, both windows cover it, and the coupling is **two-way volumetric**, which is W70's shape and has no bond. `wake_array` takes a third option that is neither — the rotor's two ports at the two nearest rings, $0.25\,D$ and $0.22\,D$ off the disk plane, `geometrically_coincident=False` — and says so | **`done` 2026-09-01 — see Tier 22.** CS-9 split `ThermoStruct2D` into two co-located agents and answered it: the coupling **is a bond and is not a port**. The disk's third option is a *surface approximation of a co-located bond*, and Tier 22 measures what such an approximation drops. It was still the **flagship case study landing on its own ledger's open row**, and only real geometry could have shown it: a schematic rotor hangs off any ring |
| **W95** | `atlas/multiphysics.py` | **The tightly coupled referent $\tau$ is defined against cannot be built from a frozen checkpoint's own pair.** Dense FD Jacobian in $V$, $96\times96$, $\kappa = 2.17\times10^4$, full rank at a $10^{-8}$ cut — and the same $J$ at a $2\times$ smaller probe step differs by $4.09\times10^{-2}$, so **65 of 96 singular directions sit below the probe's own reproducibility**. Newton diverges from the first iteration; **truncating to the 31 resolved directions does not help** ($8.55\times10^{-2}$) and **damping to $0.2$ does not help** ($1.29\times10^{-1}$), so it is neither W85's subspace, nor conditioning, nor step length | `open` — three mechanisms eliminated by measurement and the fourth unnamed. It decides whether a learned expert can ever be its own referent, and today the answer is no, which makes `lambda_ref` load-bearing at a **single-physics** seam and generalizes W88. **[Tier 41, 2026-09-10: asked again of a bounded donor, and the answer is the same.** NeuberNet is not resolution-fixed and still has no same-class reference pair: it is defined on one disc inside its training ranges, so no larger or undecomposed domain exists to be its referent, and the classical patch that is a referent for its elastic branch measures model error rather than $\tau$ or $\sigma$ ([[case-study-bounded-donor-atlas-0.1]]). **W95 binds for bounded donors too.** Not re-scoped against the certificate in this tier.]** |
| **W96** | `atlas/cases/wind_farm_real.py` | `RotorAgent.respond` returns `ActuatorDisk.force_density` — a force per unit **volume** — against a port whose scale set declares `stress`. At the default $\Delta_d = 0.1$ the two differ by exactly $10\times$. W66's class, with one difference: W69 concluded the wrong quantity differs by an unseparable $O(1)$ once nondimensionalized, and **here the factor is a declared geometric length**, so this instance is separable in principle | `open`, small. §12's `ADVEC` and `ROT` numbers are unaffected; only the rotor's `MECH` magnitude inherits it. `wake_array.RotorDisk` returns the traction |
| **W97** | `atlas/probe.py`, `atlas/composition.py` | **A field-to-lumped `MECH` seam is one-sided by the cell Reynolds number, structurally.** §2.2's normative effort is $\nu\,\partial w/\partial n$ and an actuator disk's traction carries no $\nu$, so $\lVert S_{\text{rotor}}\rVert/\lVert S_{\text{fluid}}\rVert \sim \mathrm{Re}_h$ and `SubstitutionCertificate.blind` is `True` over a $\beta_{\min}$ range **81 times the fluid block's own norm** (measured; $255\times$ in the commuting idealization). The candidate repair is §4.1's conservative co-normal, which **W47 scoped out** at fluid–fluid seams because the advective term cancels there — and at a rotor face the advective term is exactly what does not cancel | **`done` 2026-09-03, by the candidate repair this row names** ([[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]] §5, Tier 24). At CS-10's wing seam §2.2's effort measures $\lVert S_{\text{SUSP}}\rVert/\lVert S_{\text{fluid}}\rVert = \mathbf{19.4}$ at the exchange interval and $77.4$ at the macro-step — the ratio is a property of the pairing **and of the cadence**, and W86's discipline applies to it — and §4.1's co-normal takes it to $\mathbf{0.381}$, **below one**, with $\beta$ up $21\times$, $\kappa$ down $10\times$ and the informative window $85\times$ wider. **And the mechanism is sharper than this row states it:** §2.2's effort is the *viscous traction alone*, and at a fluid–fluid seam the pressure and advective parts cancel across a shared ring cell while at a field-to-lumped seam there is no second fluid side. The two halves were **different physical quantities under the same declared `response_half`** — `L3/C9` checks the half and nothing checks the quantity — and the exact momentum exchange, which is what the lumped side returns, moves the ratio $18\,430\times$. Two things this row did not anticipate: the certificate at a **rigid** lumped seam is **not blind at all** (rank $S_{\text{lumped}} = 1$ against $\dim M = 17$, so $\beta$ collapses $90\times$ below the fluid block's own norm and `visible_above` is negative under every effort), and W97's own $81\times$ is quoted without a cadence |
| **W98** | `scripts/w93_wake_array.py`, `atlas/cases/wake_array.py` | **A composed march bought its two GLOBAL operators from a per-window API, and manufactured a wake $3.5\,D$ upstream of the only turbine in its row.** `build` declares `pressure` a `GlobalField` — *"the composition layer's, and it has to be"* — and the march then asked `step_many` for `project=True`, which runs an exact Leray projection on each 128-cell window **separately and periodically**, so a disk's momentum sink had its elliptic response smeared over its own $4\,D$ box and wrapped onto that box's upstream image. The same call's `frame=(U_\infty, 0)` translates each window by $U\,\Delta t/\Delta x = 6.4$ cells with `spectral_shift`, periodic **on the window**, so a wake reaching a window's outflow edge re-entered its own inflow edge and had circulated it once every $N / 6.4 = 20$ macro-steps — which is why the manufactured deficit was flat in $x$: it is the wake's own $y$-profile, smeared along the direction it kept going round. Measured with R1 the only disk, at $1.25\,D$ upstream after ten macro-steps: $0.8244$ before, $0.9495$ after, referent $0.9966$; and a row holding no turbine at all had drifted to $0.976$. **It is not a halo failure and a wider overlap does not touch it** — a partition-of-unity dead zone giving the wrapped strip exactly zero weight moves the deficit by $0.012$, because W93 already measured this checkpoint's support reach as the *whole* window. `wake_array.transport_and_project` is the repair: one projection and one translation, on the domain, which is extended downstream by a tapered buffer so the extension is periodic-compatible — the projection is then exact and what wraps onto the inlet is the taper's zero, which is freestream | **`done`, and it was found by looking at the picture rather than at the numbers.** Every Tier 18 quantity is measured *at the state this march produces*, so all of §18 was re-measured against it. The headline movement is that most of the checkpoint's apparent disagreement was the coupling's: see §18 for the new table. The residual $5\%$ upstream is W93's and no coupling choice removes it |
| **W99** | build repo, `adapters.py` | **`FrozenFluidExpert.step` and `.step_many` silently DROP `force` when `galilean` is false.** The `if force is not None` block is nested inside `if gal:` in both, so a caller that moves the Galilean translation up to the domain — which W98's repair must — gets a forward pass with no body force, no error and no warning. The same branch also does `u1 -= u1.mean()` unconditionally, which pins each window's output fluctuation to zero mean and therefore **deletes the momentum deficit a disk has just deposited**, every step, whether or not that was the caller's intent | `open`, in the build repo, and small only because one caller now knows. `march` applies the force itself after assembly and preserves each window's incoming mean; nothing in the signature or the docstring warns the next caller |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

| Claim | Where | Now |
|---|---|---|
| *"that product is an EXPLICIT agent's domain of dependence"* | Tier 14, W69 | right, and it fixed one of the **two** branches it indicts — W93 |
| W75's support gate, *"two solves, no spectral threshold"* | Tier 15 | holds on a learned operator, and reads it as **`embedded`** for a reason that is architectural rather than physical. Declare what it measures and `L2/R10` refuses the whole graph |
| W68/W75, *"the two instruments are complements rather than alternatives"* | Tiers 14–15 | on the first black box where **both** have signal, they **agree**: `('embedded',)` and *"consistent with EMBEDDED"*, at $\omega = 0.819$, well above the floor |
| W87, *"a false referent corrupts $\tau$'s magnitude while leaving its localization correct"* | Tier 17 | measured on a second case and it is stronger than that: the localization is **exact** ($0$ on the unswapped side, every row) and the magnitude moves only $1.05\times$ over a $27\times$ band in the referent's own viscosity |
| W74, *"the base belongs to the SEAM"* | Tiers 14–15 | and it can be **hidden in the state**. Four case studies write the trace as a perturbation, so every fluid `probe_base` in this vault is zero and `base_disagreement` has read *consistent* on all of them while the two sides sat at their own states |
| §11's *"with a checkpoint you choose the domain to suit the window"* | Tier 11 | one step further: **the turbine spacing is a whole number of window strides**, and the Reynolds band ($\mathrm{Re} = 1020/S$) and the lateral room a wake needs are in direct conflict |

## The mechanism tally

Tier 17: *a rule can be correct, derived, and aimed at the wrong order of magnitude.* This tier's findings do not share that shape. **Three of the four are rules that were right and had never been evaluated where their premise fails** — W69's halo derivation stopped one branch short, W76's blindness was stated for a tiling and holds for a port pairing, W85's referent price assumed a referent existed — and the fourth is a gap the ledger already carried and no fixture could reach.

> **What made all four visible is the same thing: real geometry.** A schematic rotor can be hung off any ring, so it never forces the bond question; a synthetic seam has a base of zero, so it never forces the linearization question; and a solver you can run at any resolution has a monolith, so it never forces the referent question. **The five case studies before this one were each honest about being a fixture in exactly one dimension, and each of those dimensions was hiding a rule.**

# Tier 19 — the scaling ladder, and the criterion the whole program is scheduled on (2026-08-31)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §19. Reproduce with `python scripts/w100_scaling_ladder.py --steps 60 --acc-steps 20` then `python scripts/w100_timing.py --steps 8 --repeats 6`; artifacts `out/w100/w100.json`, `out/w100/state_<rung>.npz`, `out/w100/solo_<rung>.npz`. New case study `atlas/cases/scaling_ladder.py` — the **seventh** real one, and the first whose *variable* is the size of the graph.

> **Dating.** The marches ran 2026-08-30; the run finished and was written up **2026-08-31**, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

[[f1-pathmap-and-end-goal]] §5's **F1** fails if composition error grows super-linearly in interface count, §3.2 calls rung 9 *the rung that decides everything*, and §5 schedules this sweep as its early warning. **It had never been run at any size**, and [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4 schedules nine further case studies on the answer.

**The answer: F1 is not falsified.** Over $1 \to 24$ coupled windows, holding $\mathrm{d}x$, $\Delta t$, the overlap, the ramp, the viscosity and the disk model fixed, the composed defect grows with exponent $+0.804\ [+0.641, +0.967]$ classically and $+0.477\ [+0.362, +0.593]$ on the frozen checkpoint — sub-linear in both columns, with the upper 95% bound below $1$. Both controls pass: $N=1$ returns a composed defect of **exactly zero, bit for bit**, and $N=6$ reproduces the wake array's $25.27\%$ / $14.62\%$ array loss to the digit.

**And the run found something it was not looking for**, which is worth more than the headline: the classical composed rollout is **unstable** at $N \ge 6$. The assembly injects constraint residual the agent-side elliptic solve cannot remove — that is `L6/C2`, and `R12` now checks it — but the repair the tier first proposed was falsified the same day by marching it to $120$ steps instead of $20$: **adding** a global projection to agents that already project is worse than doing nothing, and the arrangement that survives is R10's own, with the elliptic part **moved** out of the agent. See **W100**, and **W105** for the gap that leaves.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Where | What it was | How it closed |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W58** | `atlas/graph.py`, `atlas/compiler.py`, `atlas/assembly.py`, `atlas/probe.py` | `cut_defect_bound` can mean $\lVert\sum_i\chi_i\lvert D_i\rvert\rVert$ (needs a monolith) or the reference-free neighbour disagreement, the record does not say which, and the two are provably equal only when the contaminated sets are pairwise disjoint | **`done`, and the row was asking about the second-order hypothesis.** Built: `MeasuredConstants.cut_defect_bound_form`, four named forms in `graph.CUT_DEFECT_FORMS`, `assembly.contaminated_multiplicity` deciding disjointness **from the declaration with no run**, and `L2/C2/W58` decertifying a value with no form, an unknown form, a substructuring form on the overlapping branch, and a reference-free form whose hypothesis fails. Then the measurement: **the disjointness the framework named moves the surrogate/bound ratio by 4% and an *aggregation* nobody had named moves it by 3$\times$.** `probe.neighbour_disagreement` is a max over neighbour PAIRS of a norm; L2/C2's max form is a norm over the whole grid of a cellwise max. Those coincide at one overlapping pair and diverge above it whatever the contaminated sets do — measured, the aggregated surrogate holds at $0.765 \to 0.802$ across the ladder while the pairwise one falls $0.765 \to \mathbf{0.234}$. `probe.aggregated_neighbour_disagreement` is the form that can be compared with the bound at any size, and the compile refuses the pairwise one above two subdomains. **The row has teeth on this run's own compiles**: the checkpoint's column can only supply a reference-free form (W95), `L2/C2` admits it at $N=2$ where the sets are disjoint, and **decertifies it at $N=6$, $12$ and $24$ purely on geometry** |

The row's own definition of done is a test that a changed harness moves the attribution: one fixed set of restriction defects, two partitions differing only in the ramp, the chi-weighted constant moves, **and the depth tag is identical on both**. This tier then supplied a fifth instance unprompted — a frozen disk force diverges, and that instability is the disk model's and would have been charged to the composition |
| **W81** | `atlas/composition.py`, `atlas/compiler.py` | $\beta_{\min}$ is **undefined anywhere in this framework**, it is the whole discriminating power of the plug-in guarantee, and the one sibling default is $10^{-12}$ at which every seam in the vault is blind | **`done`, both branches the row allows.** *Derived*: `beta_min_from_tolerance` returns $\varepsilon_{\text{tol}} = \min(\tau,\sigma)$ over the terms carrying a **positive** scale — W84's caveat is the reason for the filter, since $\tau=0$ is a real measurement and a plain minimum sets the tolerance to zero, which is the $10^{-12}$ default arriving from the other direction. *Reported*: `blind` and `passes` are both monotone in $\beta_{\min}$, so the whole verdict function is two numbers, and the certificate now exposes `visible_above` $= \beta - \lVert S_i\rVert$ and `fails_above` $= \beta - \lVert\Delta\rVert$ and returns them **instead of a verdict** when no tolerance is supplied. And the sibling default is gone: `schur_complement`'s parameter is renamed **`beta_int_floor`**, because it guards a singularity on the internal block where $10^{-12}$ is exactly right. Measured on one wake seam per rung: both thresholds are **negative** at all four, $\lVert\Delta\rVert$ exceeds $\beta$ by $19\times$, so the swap is refused at every admissible $\beta_{\min}$ and is **never blind** — and the derived $\varepsilon_{\text{tol}}$ agrees with the whole swept range. Set against **W97**'s rotor seam, blind on the entire admissible axis, the two thresholds now separate the seam types cleanly |

## The mechanism tally

Tier 18: *what made all four findings visible is the same thing — real geometry.* This tier's shape is different, and three of its findings share it.

**The measurement that decides is the one that removes a variable, and in each case the removed variable was one nobody had noticed was moving.** §19.3 removes the physics and the exponent falls by a factor of five. §19.6's positive control removes the elliptic placement and an instability disappears. §19.5 removes the aggregation and W58's stated hypothesis turns out to be the second-order one. In all three the headline measurement was correct and **uninterpretable on its own**.

> **A ladder measures a derivative, and a derivative taken while two things move measures neither.** The brief said to hold $\mathrm{d}x$, $\Delta t$, the overlap, the ramp, the viscosity and the disk model fixed, and the run did. It did not say to hold the **flow** fixed, because nobody had noticed that growing the farm grows the physics — and that control is worth more than the headline it qualifies.

# Tier 20 — does a certificate travel with the expert? (2026-08-31)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §20. Reproduce with `python scripts/w106_reuse_probe.py --steps 110`; artifacts `out/w106/w106.json`, `out/w106/state_N6.npz`, `out/w106/state_N12.npz`. New case study `atlas/cases/reuse_probe.py` — the **eighth** real one, and the first whose *variable* is the state a measurement is taken at rather than anything about the graph.

> **Dating.** Marches, probes and write-up all **2026-08-31**, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

[[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4 names **CS-8** as the only rung that tests the *foundation-model* claim rather than the coupling claim, and sharpens it to: *is a substitution certificate a property of the expert, or of the state it was probed at?* If each of a twenty-agent car's sixty certificates is state-specific, the plug-in claim is **per-design rather than per-expert** and the economic argument for a reusable expert library collapses into re-certifying every design.

**The answer: neither noun is right, and the state half dominates.** One swap — `reference.WindowNS` $\to$ Poseidon-T — at $55$ cells across four factors. Median movement as a fraction of each quantity's own level:

| quantity | **state** | **seam** | **replicate** | **geometry** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| $\lVert S_i\rVert$ | $2.49\%$ | $1.99\%$ | $0.037\%$ | $0.199\%$ |
| $\lVert\Lambda^{\text{expert}} - \Lambda^{\text{ref}}\rVert$ | $3.63\%$ | $2.97\%$ | $0.228\%$ | $0.156\%$ |
| $\beta$ | $6.46\%$ | $3.99\%$ | $0.769\%$ | $0.340\%$ |
| $\Xi$ (block) | $55.9\%$ | $53.9\%$ | $20.5\%$ | $1.61\%$ |
| `visible_above` | $155\%$ | $79.2\%$ | $3.56\%$ | $2.70\%$ |
| `fails_above` | $155\%$ | $96.0\%$ | $5.32\%$ | $3.66\%$ |

**Three orders of magnitude separate the most portable quantity from the least, and they are all fields of one certificate.** The norms travel to a few percent; the two $\beta_{\min}$ thresholds move by more than their own size, because each is a difference of two nearly equal large numbers ($\beta \approx 0.21$, both norms $\approx 0.22$) and inherits the amplification. **Doubling the graph costs twenty to forty times less than developing the flow** — a certificate is more portable across designs of different *size* than across *operating points* of one design, which is the wrong way round for the library argument.

**Both controls pass and both cost the tier something.** Control ZERO: at the freestream, seams the taxonomy calls different regimes return the **same operator, exactly** — worst range $0.0$ over eight quantities in all three port groups, which is what makes the seam factor interpretable. Control FLOOR: the identical cell re-probed with the state reloaded off disk and the experts rebuilt disagrees by **exactly $0.0$** on all eight quantities in all four floor cells — the pipeline is bit-reproducible, and that is what makes the framework's own *did-it-move* test vacuous (**W106**).

**Every graph this tier built was asserted to carry an `assembly.ProjectedAssembly`** — the $110$ probe graphs, the sixteen floor re-probes and the four compiled ones — because `R10` and `R10b` can both clear on a graph that never applies the elliptic part (**W105**). All four compiles return `admit-uncertified` with **zero refusals in both columns at both sizes**, which is the first real graph in this vault to manage it.

| $\Xi$ as the expert library's ranking axis | [[expert-library-atlas-0.1]], [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §4.5 | it moves $55.9\%$ with the probe state and disagrees by $20.5\%$ in the median — up to $49\%$ — between two seams the taxonomy calls the **same regime**. The axis is real and **its reproducibility had never been quoted**; ranking a library on it needs the state named |

So `travel_verdict`, which asks whether a movement exceeds the floor, returns `state-dependent` for a movement of $0.037\%$ and for one of $155\%$ alike. `ExpertCapabilities.reproducibility_floor` is a *declaration about the solver* ($\varepsilon_{\text{mach}}$ for the reference, $10^{-5}$ for the checkpoint) and neither it nor the measured floor is the right denominator | `open`. The two yardsticks that do discriminate are built and reported at every cell: the quantity's **own level** (range over mean magnitude) and a **same-regime replicate**. The mechanism is arithmetic and therefore general: both thresholds are $\beta$ minus a norm, the two are nearly equal at an interesting seam, and their difference is two orders below either term. **Any certificate decided near $\lVert\Delta\rVert \approx \beta$ inherits the amplification — which is the regime a *good* substitution is in.** Worse for the library argument, $\Xi$ — the axis proposed for *ranking* a library — disagrees by up to $49\%$ between same-regime replicates | `open`. Done when `SubstitutionCertificate` carries a per-field portability class, or when the reported object is the **pair of norms** rather than their difference. **[AI Inference]:** portability and admissibility may be in tension by construction; untested, and it needs a seam where $\lVert\Delta\rVert \ll \beta$, which this vault does not have |

W81 closed *where does the number come from*; this is *which state is it the number for*. Done when a derived $\varepsilon_{\text{tol}}$ carries the probe state it was derived at, and the compile refuses to reuse one derived at a different state without saying so |
| **W109** | `atlas/composition.py` | **The verdict's invariance is the invariance of a sign.** Swept over $\beta_{\min} \in \{0, 10^{-12}, 10^{-6}, 10^{-3}, 0.01, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5\}$ at all $55$ cells the answer is `refuse` everywhere — because both thresholds are negative exactly when $\lVert\Delta\rVert > \beta$, and that ratio exceeds $1$ at every cell. But it ranges $[\mathbf{1.00254}, \mathbf{1.38952}]$, and **its minimum is a quarter of one percent from flipping**, at `x0r1_full` $N{=}12$ macro-step $30$ — the seam with the *smallest* state-dependence in the study. The distance from the sign change varies $153\times$ across the grid | `open`. A stability claim read off a saturated verdict is not a stability claim. Done when the certificate reports the margin beside the verdict, so *"this swap is refused"* and *"this swap is refused by $0.25\%$"* are distinguishable without a re-probe. **[Tier 41, 2026-09-10: `done`.** `SubstitutionCertificate.decision_margin` is $(\beta-\beta_{\min})-\lVert\Delta\rVert$, signed, with `decision_margin_over_beta` beside it, in `as_dict` and in the pass and refuse messages — *"is refused by $0.00254$, $0.254\%$ of beta"* — and not on a blind pass, where no replacement could have failed. **No verdict moves**: a test restates the pre-Tier-41 truth table and reproduces every verdict of W177's census across its own $\beta_{\min}$ sweep. See Tier 41 below.]** |
| **W110** | `atlas/cases/reuse_probe.py` | `open`, small — **the divergence guard's ratio form is inert exactly where it is armed.** `probe_state_health` compares a probe state's $\lVert\nabla\cdot u\rVert_{\text{rms}}$ against the first snapshot's, and the first snapshot is the **freestream**, where the divergence is identically zero — so `div_ratio` came back `None` at every one of the ten snapshots and only the absolute value was ever checked. The reference should be the first *developed* snapshot. **Nothing above is affected**: the values are recorded, span $0.0312$ to $0.0671$, and reproduce §19.6's own exposed-and-projected column to three digits at both rungs | `open`. The general shape is W103's and W69's one level down: a guard whose hypothesis is a convention no signature expresses |

## The mechanism tally

Tier 19: *the measurement that decides is the one that removes a variable, and in each case the removed variable was one nobody had noticed was moving.* This tier is the mirror image.

**The measurement that decides here is the one that holds everything still and varies the thing nobody had called a variable.** Nine tiers have quoted $\beta$, $\Xi$, $\tau$ and a certificate verdict, each at **one** probe state, and no page had ever asked what the second reading would be. The earlier numbers are not wrong — control FLOOR says they reproduce bit for bit. **Seven of them were a sample of size one from a distribution nobody had drawn.**

> **A quantity measured once is a quantity whose variability is being asserted to be zero.** The floor being exactly $0.0$ is what makes that assertion visible *as* an assertion: this pipeline can tell a $0.037\%$ movement from a $155\%$ one, and until this tier it had never been asked to.

And a second, cheaper to state. **The control that could have invalidated the whole design cost one rung of the grid and returned exactly zero.** Had the four regime seams disagreed at the freestream, the seam factor would have been measuring the port rather than the flow, and every number in it would have been uninterpretable. Running it took five minutes.

# Tier 21 — a second machine, and two rows the PoC's own timing opened (2026-09-01)

Full record: [[poc1-retrospective-and-hybrid-roadmap]]. Not a case study — a re-read of [[poc1-results-differentiable-design]]'s demo, timed independently on a Mac, plus the two gaps that comparison exposed once it was checked against [[tier0-measurements]] §19.10 and [[interface-transfer-theory]] §9.

> **Dating.** Write-up 2026-09-01, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

The Mac's numbers ($10$–$25\%$ slower on the decomposed all-classical column, against $-16.5\%$ on the dev box) reproduce §19's own finding in *sign* across two independent memory hierarchies, which §19.10 had explicitly declined to claim (**W104**, "neither statement transfers off this host"). Checking that finding against [[tier0-measurements]]'s classical-vs-Poseidon-T table surfaced a crossover between $N=6$ and $N=12$ windows that nothing had previously stated as a crossover, and checking the customizability question against [[interface-transfer-theory]] §9 found that the one construction that would let the framework choose its own seams — $\mathcal Q(\Gamma)$ — has sat unused since the day it was proposed.

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W111** | `atlas/scheme.py`'s accelerator ($\mathcal K$) and window ($W$) fields | **Iterations to convergence at a seam, as a function of which expert sits there, has never been measured.** [[schwarz-iteration-atlas-0.1]] measured that a *periodic* window's Schwarz sweep is the identity bitwise — zero effective iterations, because there is nothing to converge — and that a *Dirichlet* sweep costs $14\times$ more **per iteration**, but no page has held a seam and a tolerance fixed and counted **sweep count** while swapping only the expert. The hypothesis worth testing: a classical expert's boundary response is well-approximated by a few Fourier modes (the same property that makes [[probed-dtn-coupling]]'s probe cheap for it), so it may converge in *fewer* sweeps than a globally-receptive learned expert independent of either one's per-sweep cost — which would make a classical expert doubly favoured at a genuinely iterative interface, not just per-call | `open`, proposed 2026-09-01. Done when one seam is instrumented for sweep count under a fixed $\varepsilon_{\text{tol}}$, classical vs. a frozen checkpoint, everything else held constant |
| **W112** | [[interface-transfer-theory]] §9, `compiler.py`'s L2 layer | **$\mathcal Q(\Gamma)$ has never chosen a cut.** Proposed 2026-08-27 as the form [[generalization-requirements]] G5's speculative *"cut where coupling is weakest"* criterion could be measured in, immediately flagged as **"the weakest claim on the page,"** and every case study since — six of them — has placed its windows by a person choosing `N_COL`/`N_ROW`, never by evaluating $\mathcal Q$ over candidates. This is the single missing piece of [[f1-pathmap-and-end-goal]]'s fourth end-goal capability (automatic, adaptive seam placement) and the one item [[prior-art-and-novelty-atlas-0.1]]'s verdict table finds no prior art for | **`done` 2026-09-02, Tier 23 — and the row's own premise was wrong when it was written.** $\mathcal Q$ **had** been falsified: [[tier0-measurements]] §10.2, 2026-08-28, four days after it was proposed, measured a rank correlation of $-0.853$ over twelve cut placements, a $361\times$ basis orbit and a criterion constant to $8\times10^{-11}$ where the truth spread $2.29\times$, and `probe.cut_score` has read `RETIRED` in its own docstring since that day. What had never happened is exactly what the *done when* clause asks for, and it ran as **CS-9★**: a search over **decompositions** under a **cost budget** on CS-7's **real geometry** against CS-7's own **hand-chosen tilings**. Result in both directions — at one exchange interval the search finds a cut at $0.2685\times$ the hand-chosen defect with the hand-chosen ranking 138th of 178, and marched $240$ intervals that same cut is up to $1.59\times$ **worse**. $\mathcal Q$ places fifth of eight criteria and ranks exactly $-1.000$ on the overlap axis. See [[case-study-seam-placement-atlas-0.1]] and Tier 23 |

## The mechanism tally

**Both rows have the same shape and it is not the shape of the last twenty.** Every prior tier's opened rows came from running something and finding a number that did not behave as declared. These two came from **reading two existing pages side by side against a number from a different machine** — no new march, no new probe, no new case study. [[tier0-measurements]] §19.10's table and [[interface-transfer-theory]] §9's flagged weak claim had both sat, unconnected, for a week; what closed the distance was a user's independent timing run landing inside the exact $N$ range where the two already-recorded facts (the crossover, and the unused score) mattered. **The cheapest measurement this tier makes is that a vault's own cross-references are themselves an instrument, if someone reads them together.**
