# Tier 22 — the bond for a volumetric coupling, and the first exercise of the amendment (2026-09-01)

Full record: [[case-study-thermal-strain-atlas-0.1]]. Reproduce with `python scripts/w94_thermal_strain.py`; artifact `out/w94/w94.json`; tests `tests/test_tier23_thermal_strain.py`.

> **Dating.** Measured and written 2026-09-01, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

CS-9, [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4's Phase B first row. `thermostruct2d.ThermoStruct2D` split into a conduction agent and an elasticity agent coupled through **thermal strain**, with the unsplit solve as the referent — the ninth real case study, and the **first co-located graph in this vault**: two agents on the same mesh over the same region, cut along the physics rather than the domain.

**The gate is met and the amendment refuses, and both halves matter.** Carried in full the volume term reproduces the monolith's stress **bit for bit**; lagged one macro-step it costs $7.116\times10^{-3}$, first order in $\Delta t$ ($\log_2$ ratios $1.003,\,1.002,\,1.001$). The volumetric power closes the *elasticity agent's own* balance to first order. And `PortAmendment`, exercised for the first time, **refuses on two of six fields** and turns out to need a seventh.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| **W94** | *"an actuator disk has no expressible bond against a frozen operator, by two independent routes"*, `open` since Tier 18 | **`done`.** A two-way volumetric coupling **has a bond and does not have a port**, and the obstruction is localized rather than argued. The conjugate pair $(\Delta T,\ \beta\,\mathrm{tr}\,\dot{\boldsymbol\varepsilon})$ exists, its product is a power density, and its reverse half is the *same operator transposed* — $G$ forward, $G^{\!\top}$ back — which is what makes it a bond. What fails is that the **pairing is not unique** (two readings differ by $3395\times$, their sum an exact total derivative of a shared energy) and that the **carrier has co-dimension zero**, so $\dim M=\dim V$ and a probe is $344$ full solves for an operator that *is* the coupled solve. `wake_array`'s third option — two ports at the nearest rings, `geometrically_coincident=False` — is now readable as a *surface approximation of a co-located bond*, and this tier measures what such an approximation drops |
| **W32** | **`PortAmendment`**: write the six-field procedure into [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] as a binding section and exercise it once, `in progress` since Tier 5 | **`done`, and the exercise changed the procedure.** [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §10 is now binding: seven fields, a conjunction, with `ports.spec_for` refusing any name outside the five so a case study cannot introduce one inline. Exercised on thermal strain: **`refuse`**, failing fields 1 (`bond`) and 4 (`dtn_reading`) with 5 (`distinctness`) the strongest and 3 (`transfer`) passing *vacuously*. `holes.py`'s own `inference_note` predicted the shortfall — *"the first exercise may well show a seventh field is needed"* — and it did: **field 7, `support`**, the co-dimension of the carrier and, at co-dimension zero, the argument that the two agents' free energies are additive. Fields 1–6 are all satisfiable by a co-located pair and not one of them notices that $\mathcal R$'s premise has failed |
| **W70** | `done` by reframing 2026-08-30 (*"one expert's internals and one-way, so nothing needs a bond"*), with the two-way case explicitly left open | **confirmed from the other side, and the reframing's blind spot named.** W70 said *"a sixth port type is not the fix, because the object is not a bond"*; measured, the object **is** a bond and what it is not is a **port**. And §16.7's *"volumetric two-way coupling between two agents"* row, `open` since Tier 16, closes with it. **Why W70 could not have seen it**: the equivalent-thermal-pressure reduction is *exact* when $\nabla\Delta T=0$ — measured, $\lVert G_{\text{body}}\Delta T\rVert$ is $6.8\times10^{-17}$ of the load at a uniform $\Delta T$ — and W70's shell was $8\,$mm of aluminium at $\mathrm{Bi}\approx3\times10^{-4}$, nearly isothermal. Put a gradient in it and the reduction has nowhere to put $\beta\nabla\Delta T$ |

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W113** | `atlas/compiler.py` `_l3_connections`, `atlas/emit.py` `validate` | *(opened and closed in this tier)* **Two rules contradicted each other and a graph with no connections could emit nothing.** `_l3_connections` stamped $\mathrm{E2}$ `unchecked` for a seamless graph; `emit.validate` refuses an unchecked $\mathrm{E2}$ outright, in its own words *"a defect in the compiler, not a property of the case"*, because $\mathrm{E1}$–$\mathrm{E4}$ are static properties of a declaration. So a zero-connection graph raised `EmitRefused` and produced no artifact | **`done`.** $\mathrm{E2}$ now *holds vacuously* — its statement is *"interfaces are static, and transfer between the two sides is declared"*, and over an empty interface set both clauses are true — while the L3 decertification carries the thing that matters, which is that nothing is coupled. Latent for the whole life of the compiler because **no graph in this vault had zero seams** until the `global-field` route; `tests/test_tier8_rules.py`'s note that *"the public path cannot express a single agent"* was a symptom of it |
| **W114** | `atlas/compiler.py` `_r10_elliptic` | **`L2/R10` refuses a co-located split and its own derivation does not reach one.** The rule fires on any graph with $\ge2$ agents one of which declares `elliptic_subsolve=embedded`, and its sentence is *"the graph decomposes the domain … so the decomposition changes the operator rather than restricting it"*. A co-located split **cuts no domain**: both agents own all of $\Omega$, and the quasi-static elasticity solve is over exactly the region it was over in the monolith. The rule is stated over `elliptic_subsolve` alone and never consults whether the decomposition cuts the agent, so it refuses every graph in CS-9 for a reason that has nothing to do with the bond. Compounding it: **there is no `split-step` escape on a quasi-static agent** — no time derivative, nothing to sub-step — so a structural agent is `EMBEDDED` or it is not an agent, and CS-8's *"start from the exposed-agent plus `ProjectedAssembly` column"* does not transfer to one | **`done` 2026-09-04 at CS-12** ([[case-study-wing-fsi-atlas-0.1]] §2, Tier 26), by the check this row asks for. R10 now consults whether another agent shares the `EMBEDDED` agent's `governing_family` — the compile-time signature of a decomposition that cut this agent's physics — before refusing. A proxy rather than a decision procedure, and conservative: two agents of one family on disjoint regions are still refused. **Four graphs' verdicts move and no measurement moves with them** (`thermal_strain` `surface-mech`, `brake_thermal` `as-built`, `thermal_seam` `as-built`, `wing_fsi`), while `window_ns` `as-built` keeps its refusal. `thermal_strain.R10_SCOPE` stays in the module as the record of what the refusal was |
| **W115** | `atlas/graph.py` `Decomposition` | **A co-located split has no decomposition axis.** `OVERLAPPING` and `NON_OVERLAPPING` are both wrong for two agents that share every cell: there is no cut to be non-overlapping about and no *partial* overlap to blend across, so there is no partition of unity, no halo and no cross-point. `thermal_strain.build` declares `OVERLAPPING` with `overlap` equal to the whole domain — the closer of the two — and says so in a comment, which is a declaration nothing checks. The axis fixes which rung R1/R2 can support and which of L2's branches runs, so a wrong one is not cosmetic | `open`, small to specify and load-bearing for three Phase C subsystems. Done when either a third member exists with the L2/L6 branches it needs stated, or the framework says in one place that a co-located split is out of scope for the composition layer and belongs to the agent that owns the splitting |
| **W116** | `atlas/graph.py` `MeasuredConstants` | **A co-located split's own measured defect is not declarable.** CS-9 measures a splitting error of $7.116\times10^{-3}$, first order in the exchange interval and reproduced over a $8\times$ sweep — exactly the kind of number `MeasuredConstants` exists to carry. There is no field for it. The nearest slot is $\sigma$, the **transmission** infidelity, which the master bound consumes as an interface-power quantity measured between two assemblies; the splitting error is a lagged-operator-splitting defect measured in relative stress, because there is no interface to take a power over. Putting one in the other's slot is **W56 with the sign reversed** — not a bound quoted from a constant nobody measured, but a bound quoted from a constant somebody measured *and that means something else* | `open`, small. `thermal_strain.MEASURED_W94` therefore declares `tau = 0` and **not** $\sigma$, and `SPLITTING_ERROR` sits beside it as a module constant nothing consumes, so the compile honestly reports $\sigma$ unmeasured on a graph whose only measured defect is not a $\sigma$. Found by nearly doing the wrong thing: the first version of this case study declared it as $\sigma$ on the seamless route and not on the surface one, which made the route comparison asymmetric and produced a headline that was an artefact of the declaration rather than of the routes |
| **W117** | `atlas/graph.py` `GlobalField`; `atlas/compiler.py` L3 | **The route that improves an envelope stamp by deleting the seam was not merely uncaught — it was unreadable.** Nothing in the framework read `global_fields` at all: `compiler.py`, `emit.py`, `composition.py` and `scheme.py` contained no reference to it, so a `GlobalField` was a **write-only declaration** that reached `to_dict` and stopped. And a rule could not be written for a second and sharper reason: the dataclass carried `name`, `applies_to` and `note`, and **none of the three said where the field's value comes from**, which is precisely the discriminator a rule needs. §10.4 stated the hazard as a warning to authors; a warning was the only instrument available, because the declaration did not record the fact a check would test. **And the census below said the trap was not hypothetical**: four of seven live declarations name `pressure`, the quantity that mediates the coupling between the very windows the graph decomposes | **`done` 2026-09-02.** `GlobalField` carries `produced_by`; `L3/global-field` decides the four cases from the declaration alone. **W117a ran before the rule was adopted and it fires on exactly one of seven** — CS-9's `global-field` route — and clears the other six, the four `pressure` declarations included, for a reason established by reading the rotor experts rather than by tuning a declaration. Pinned in `tests/test_tier24_global_field_provenance.py`. The undeclared case is **decertified, not admitted**: the default is `None` and not `()`, so silence is not a pass |

## What this tier says about $\mathcal R(t)$

[[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §6 asks for the global power residual *every macro-step, in every case study, from now on*, and calls it *"the cheapest possible physical-plausibility monitor"*. Measured on CS-9, **it is blind to the coupling under test by six orders of magnitude**: $P_\Omega/P_\Gamma = -1.04\times10^{-6}$ and $E_{\text{el}}/E_{\text{th}} = 1.17\times10^{-6}$. Declaring the volumetric term does move the relative residual — $2.909\times10^{-6}$ to $1.871\times10^{-6}$, a real $36\%$ — but both numbers are the same order as the backward-Euler differencing error. **The right residual for a co-located split is the receiving subsystem's own balance**, which the volumetric term closes to first order in $\Delta t$ ($6.02\times10^{-2}$ down to $3.66\times10^{-3}$ over a $16\times$ range, $\log_2$ ratios $1.020$–$1.003$). That bounds where §6 applies; nothing here retracts it, and §10.3 now records the bound on the page itself.

## And the route that is worse than the wrong one

Two things the *closed* vocabulary can already express, measured side by side on identical physics:

- **`surface-mech`** — the classical equivalent thermal pressure $\mathbf t=\beta\,\Delta T\,\mathbf n$, a real `MECH` bond on a real surface. The exact identity $G=G_{\text{surf}}+G_{\text{body}}$ (to $10^{-15}$) says what it carries and what it drops; the dropped body force is only $9.58\%$ of the load, and because the two halves nearly cancel on a soft strip the displacement is amplified $233\times$ and the **stress is wrong by $1279\times$**.
- **`global-field`** — the eigenstrain declared a `GlobalField`, which bypasses L3 entirely. **Numerically exact**, and no scale set, prolongation, adjoint, null space, `response_half`, $\tau$, $\sigma$ or $\beta$ is ever asked for. Compiled against the `surface-mech` graph it turns $\mathrm{E3}$ from `fails` to `holds` and $\mathrm{E7}$ from `fails` to `unchecked`, and carries three fewer decertifications: **`L1/E3`, `L4/E7/passivity`, `L4/block-share` and `L4/operator-content` — four seam properties — are traded for `L3/graph`, one line saying it is not a composition.** It is not uniformly better and that is on the record too: $\beta$ is a property of a probed seam, so it becomes *unmeasurable* and the unmeasured list gets one longer.

> **Declaring a coupling *out* of the port algebra improves its envelope stamp**, because the quantities that would have failed are the ones a seam defines. It is declarable today by anyone, nothing in a compile says so, and it is the purest instance of the silent-wrongness class this project has produced. [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §10.4 now states it; no rule catches it.

### The census, and why W117 is a judgement rather than a patch (2026-09-02)

Every `GlobalField` declared anywhere in the vault, read out of the seven `build` functions that declare one:

| Case study | Field | What its own `note` says it is | Provenance |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rocket.py` | `gravity` | *"a global field bypasses the port mechanism entirely"* | **external** — no agent produces it |
| `wind_farm.py` | `none` | *"isothermal, incompressible, no gravity term"* | **vacuous** — a declared absence |
| `window_ns.py` | `pressure` | *"the elliptic part. Global under `mode='split-step'` … per-window under `'as-built'`, which R10 refuses"* | **all agents**, via a global elliptic solve the composition layer owns |
| `channel_ns.py` | `pressure` | same, plus *"this projection is NON-SINGULAR … so R10's measured mechanism is absent while its stated reason is not"* | **all agents**, as above |
| `wind_farm_real.py` | `pressure` | *"global under `mode='split-step'`; per-region under `'as-built'`, which R10 refuses"* | **all agents**, as above |
| `wake_array.py` | `pressure` | *"the composition layer's, and it has to be … the field that mediates an actuator disk's momentum sink is not among its outputs"* | **all agents**, and it is **repairing** a checkpoint's missing channel |
| `thermal_strain.py` | `thermal-eigenstrain` | *"NEITHER uniform NOR external — it is the other agent's state"* | **one named other agent** |

**The candidate discriminator is provenance, and on this census it separates cleanly.** Gravity is external and is what §5.1 wrote the class for. The four `pressure` declarations are a genuine *global operation over every agent at once*, owned by the composition layer and declared as such — the thing a domain decomposition legitimately cannot localize. The eigenstrain is neither: it is **one named agent's state, handed into another agent's update**, which is a seam with the seam removed. Only the last is CS-9's silent-wrongness route.

**So the rule is one line and the decision is not.** A rule that fires when a global field's value is produced by a *named proper subset* of the graph's agents catches `thermal-eigenstrain` and clears the other six. But the discriminator is **not declarable today** — that is the row — and the four `pressure` rows are the reason the fix is a judgement: `wake_array`'s pressure field is doing repair work on a checkpoint whose pressure channel is *"a PLACEHOLDER its loader pins to 0"*, which is closer to CS-9's route than to gravity, and it sits under a `partition_of_unity` and a `projected_assembly` that a global-solve reading assumes. Whether that one is external, global, or a fourth thing decides whether the rule has one exception or a class of them.

> **W117a — the judgement the rule could not make for itself. Run 2026-09-02.** Re-declare all seven with a provenance field, run the candidate rule, and compare the firing pattern against each declaration's own stated intent. Firing on exactly `thermal-eigenstrain` means the rule is right and the row closes. **Firing on the four `pressure` declarations too would mean either the rule is wrong or those four are the same defect as CS-9's route — and this vault's four largest case studies would then be resting on it.** The experiment was designed to be able to see that.

**It fires on exactly one of seven.**

| Case study | Declared | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `rocket` | `produced_by=()` | admit — external |
| `wind_farm` | `produced_by=()` | admit — a declared absence |
| `window_ns` | `produced_by=` all four windows | admit — a global operation over every agent |
| `channel_ns` | `produced_by=NAMES` | admit — as above |
| `wind_farm_real` | `produced_by=applies_to=FLUID_IDS` | admit — crosses nothing |
| `wake_array` | `produced_by=applies_to=` the fluid windows | admit — crosses nothing |
| `thermal_strain` (`global-field`) | `produced_by=("cond",)`, `applies_to=("elas",)` | **refuse** — one agent's state in another's update, with no seam |

**The two rotor graphs are the ones that had to be checked rather than assumed**, and they clear for a reason read out of the experts rather than tuned into the declaration: `RotorDisk` has **no pressure input and no pressure state** — `traction(u)`, `respond(name, trace)`, `storage(u, v)` — so a disk's momentum sink enters the fluid through the declared `MECH` seams and the projection then acts on the assembled fluid field. The rotors neither produce the pressure field nor read it, so `applies_to` is the fluid windows and the field crosses nothing. Both had previously left `applies_to` empty, which *means every agent* and therefore declared the opposite of what the code does; W117 forced the declaration to become precise, which is the rule working before it ever refused anything.

**A second thing the rule caught, and it caught it on its author.** The first `channel_ns` declaration reused `tuple(tiling.names)` — correct for `window_ns`, wrong here, because `channel_ns` borrows `window_ns`'s `Tiling` (whose windows are `W`-prefixed) while naming its own agents `C00`–`C11`. The rule refused it at *"names no agent in this graph"* rather than silently admitting a provenance list that matched nothing. That branch exists for typos and it found one on its first run.

**What the rule costs the case study it was built from.** CS-9's `global-field` route **compiled** when the case study was written: numerically exact, certified by nothing, and carrying three fewer decertifications than the `surface-mech` route that gets the answer wrong by $1279\times$. It is now refused. The route is deliberately kept exactly as it was — a rule with nothing to fire on is not a rule — and §4.4's comparison is now a comparison between a route that is refused for being uncertifiable and one that is admitted and wrong, which is the shape the finding always had.

## Two small standing results the compile produced for free

Neither was looked for; both came out of running `compile_scheme` on the `surface-mech` route.

- **A sharper instance of W97.** The two blocks of the assembled operator measure $\lVert\Lambda_{\text{cond}}\rVert = 0$ **exactly** against $\lVert\Lambda_{\text{elas}}\rVert = 5.619\times10^{11}$: a temperature field does not know its boundary is moving, so the conduction agent's response is constant in the trace and the interface problem is **empty rather than hard** (`CASE-STUDY-GUIDE` mistake 6, spec §6.4(a)). W97's rotor seam is one-sided by a *ratio* set by the cell Reynolds number; this one is one-sided by a zero.
- **A fourth row for $n_0(\Gamma)$.** The seam was declared `expected_null_dim=0` and `L4/null-space` refused it; inspection resolved it in a minute, which is the cheap direction to be wrong in. The null direction is the **constant mode** to $5.7\times10^{-13}$ at $\sigma_{\min}/\sigma_{\max} = 2.4\times10^{-15}$, and it is **rigid-body translation normal to the face** — a uniform normal velocity on the only constrained face of a *free* elastic body is a rigid motion, so no strain and no reaction. `CASE-STUDY-GUIDE`'s table had fluid–fluid `MECH` ($1$, incompressibility), field↔lumped ($0$) and CHT `THERM` ($0$); a **solid–solid `MECH` seam on a free body** gets $1$ for none of those reasons — it is kinematics — and now has its own row.

---

## PoC 1a, the frozen-expert column — W118 closed, W119–W122 opened (2026-09-02)

Not a tier: [[atlas-proof-of-concept-1]] is a scoped demonstration and sits off the [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] ladder. Rows are numbered here because the campaign's numbering is the vault's only one. Full record: [[poc1a-frozen-expert-results]]; artefact `out/w118/w118.json`.

| # | where | what | status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W118** | `atlas/cases/wind_farm_design.py`, `scripts/w111_wind_farm_design.py --expert poseidon` | **The frozen-checkpoint run.** [[poc1-results-differentiable-design]] §0 measured the whole of PoC 1a on the *classical* `exposed_reference_solver` and stated plainly that the **frozen-pretrained** half of [[prior-art-and-novelty-atlas-0.1]] §2.1 was therefore not exercised, that the substitution was a `kind=` argument, and that no sentence on that page should be read as if it had been run | **`done` 2026-09-02.** Every window is Poseidon-T; the adjoint runs through 20.8 M frozen parameters and accumulates no parameter gradient. `TapedPoseidon` is asserted **bitwise** against `adapters.FrozenFluidExpert.step_many`, and the composed macro-step against `w93_wake_array`'s numpy march to $2.2\times10^{-16}$. Nothing in `ProjectedAssembly`, `exposed_reference_solver` or `wake_array.build` was touched. Pinned in `tests/test_tier26_poseidon_design.py` (17 assertions, CPU and CUDA) |
| **W119** | the objective, not the framework | **Is CMA-ES's advantage at $K=12$ caused by OP-3?** On the frozen column the derivative-free baseline reaches the tolerance in 144 evaluations and finishes $15.6\%$ **above** the gradient optimum, where on the classical column it never arrived in 700. The hypothesis is that a checkpoint retaining a quarter of the reference wake deficit weakens turbine–turbine coupling, which is what makes the layout objective non-separable — so the landscape flattens and a population method does better. Sweep the wake strength (the referent's viscosity, or the macro-step) with the optimiser, budget and constraints fixed, and see whether the gradient/CMA ratio tracks it | `open`, cheap. Three separate readings on that page ([[poc1a-frozen-expert-results]] §4.1, §6, §7.2) rest on this one **[AI Inference]**, so it is the highest-value hour on the list |
| **W120** | [[expert-donor-survey]]'s donor table | **Re-run the cross-evaluation with a second donor.** The frozen column's optimum, scored by the undivided classical solver, captures $71.2\%$ (K12) and $70.9\%$ (K25) of the classical column's verified gain — the *same* fraction across a factor of two in design dimension, which points at a systematic property of the expert rather than at search failure. Walrus (MIT), DPOT-Ti (Apache-2.0) and GPhyT (MIT) are permissively licensed, so this also removes the **CC-BY-NC-4.0** problem that makes the present result research-only | `open`. Turns *"a better expert is worth having and is not the leverage"* (OP-3) into a number on the one axis where it now demonstrably **is** the leverage |
| **W121** | [[open-problems-atlas-0.1]] OP-6, [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §5 | **A pinned batch layout buys reproducibility, not precision.** OP-6 is carried through the vault as *"$\sim10^{-6}$ batch-position dependence, removable by pinning the batch layout"*, and [[probed-dtn-coupling]]'s probe-step derivation rests on the removable reading. Measured on the frozen column: $J$ **is** bit-reproducible per machine once the layout is pinned, and the finite-difference floor is still $1.4\times10^{-3}$ at $h=10^{-2}$ against the classical column's $5.9\times10^{-9}$ at $h=10^{-5}$, because the forward pass is float32. Cross-machine, $J$ agrees only to $2.8\times10^{-6}$ | `open`, small, and it is a **correction to a sentence rather than to code**. Any future derivation citing OP-6's $10^{-6}$ as a removable floor should cite this instead |
| **W122** | the ablation, `--stage ablation --theta-from` | **Yaw's transferability differs between the two columns and one of the two readings is unsafe.** The classical column put the frozen-rollout gradient's yaw component at $\cos=0.95$; the frozen column reads $0.76$ at K25 and $0.17$ at K12, the latter with signs agreeing exactly half the time — on a laterally symmetric start layout where the full gradient's yaw norm is $0.166$, i.e. plausibly two near-zero vectors being compared. Re-run at an asymmetric design point | `open`, minutes. [[poc1a-frozen-expert-results]] §6 flags it as unreadable rather than reporting it as a finding, which is the correct handling and not a substitute for the measurement |

**One thing this campaign did *not* open a row for, and the reason is worth recording.** `L2/R10` still refuses the frozen decomposition — W93's global receptive field is unchanged and no measurement here touches it — and the column marched 70 macro-steps at both rungs anyway, stably. That is not a defect in R10 and it is not a licence to ignore it: it is the distinction [[composition-error-theory]] draws between a composed model that *runs* and one that is *certified*, arriving as two numbers from the same run. A row saying "R10 is too strict" would be exactly the wrong lesson.

# Tier 23 — where to cut, decided by an algorithm (2026-09-02)

Full record: [[tier0-measurements]] §21; readable companion [[case-study-seam-placement-atlas-0.1]]. Reproduce with `python scripts/w112_seam_placement.py` then `python scripts/w112_horizon.py --steps 240`; artifacts `out/w112/w112.json` and `out/w112/horizon.json`; reader `python scripts/w112_summary.py`; tests `tests/test_tier25_seam_placement.py` (42).

> **Dating.** Measured and written 2026-09-02, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

CS-9★, the insertion [[poc1-retrospective-and-hybrid-roadmap]] §4.1 proposes between CS-9 and CS-10, and the first exercise of [[f1-pathmap-and-end-goal]]'s fourth end-goal capability — the one [[prior-art-and-novelty-atlas-0.1]]'s verdict table finds no prior art for. A search over **decompositions**, under three budgets, on CS-7's own geometry and developed state, graded against CS-7's own hand-chosen tilings. $367$ candidates, eight criteria, four controls, $4524$ s.

**The gate is met at one exchange interval and reverses when marched, and both halves are the result.** The search finds a six-window decomposition at $\mathbf{0.2685\times}$ the hand-chosen tiling's composed defect with the hand-chosen ranking **138th of 178** — and marched $240$ intervals that same cut crosses $1.0$ at interval $10$ and peaks at $\mathbf{1.59\times}$ *worse*. The mechanism behind the first half is structural and needed no run to find: **every x-seam of CS-6 and CS-7 passes exactly through a rotor disc**, because `wake_array.Rotor` lives on an x-adjacency.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| **W112** | *"$\mathcal Q(\Gamma)$ has never chosen a cut"*, `open` since 2026-09-01, *"blocks adaptive seam placement entirely"* | **`done`, and the row's premise was wrong when it was written.** $\mathcal Q$ **had** been falsified — [[tier0-measurements]] §10.2, 2026-08-28, four days after it was proposed: rank correlation $-0.853$ over twelve placements, a $361\times$ basis orbit, constant to $8\times10^{-11}$ where the truth spread $2.29\times$, and `probe.cut_score` has read `RETIRED` in its own docstring ever since. What had never happened is what the row's *"done when"* actually asks for and is what ran here: a search over **decompositions** under a **cost budget** on **real geometry** against a **hand-chosen rung**. $\mathcal Q$ places fifth of eight criteria, is beaten by a free one, has a $2.8\times$ spread between its two aggregations, and ranks exactly $\mathbf{-1.000}$ on the overlap axis. The comparison is in `out/w112/w112.json` and every number in §21 is re-derived from it by `tests/test_tier25_seam_placement.py` |

## Rows this tier reframes

| # | Now |
|---|---|
| **W16 / G5** | §10.2's falsification stands and its **scope was wrong in both directions**. $\mathcal Q$ does **not** rank backwards on placement in a real wake field — $+0.404$/$+0.726$ (N=6), $+0.810$/$+0.873$ (N=12), $+0.759$/$+0.681$ (SOLO) — so the natural reading of §10.2 was too harsh; and it **does** rank backwards, exactly $-1.000$ with both factors, on the **overlap**, an axis §10.2 never varied, choosing the narrowest halo when the narrowest is worse by $4.993\times$. G5's slogan also gets its first number: `cut_shear`, the mean normal shear of the *state* along the cut lines, finds the exact optimum on three of five pools for the price of two array gradients |
| **W58** | **recurs one level up, on a criterion rather than a bound.** A max over seams and a sum over seams pick different candidates whose penalties differ by $2.8\times$ ($\mathcal Q$ `__max` at $1.336\times$ the best, `__sum` at $3.706\times$ and $0.9949\times$ the hand-chosen — i.e. not beating the person at all). W58 closed the aggregation question for the *cut defect bound*; it is open again for every criterion, and both aggregations are reported side by side throughout |
| **W105** | the arrangement §21.3's march runs in, and it behaves. Both columns are exposed agents with no projection anywhere: stable to $240$ intervals with $u_{\max}$ tracking the monolith to four digits ($1.6577$ against $1.6576$), and **not incompressible** ($\lVert\nabla\cdot u\rVert_{\text{rms}}=0.2133$, identical across all three decompositions to four digits — so the divergence is the state's and not the cut's). Whether the placement advantage survives a **projected** assembly is unmeasured |

## Opened by this tier

| # | Where | What | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W123** | `atlas/compiler.py`'s L2/C2, [[tier0-measurements]] §21.3, [[composition-error-theory]] | **The one-exchange-interval defect is not what a rollout accumulates, and every cut criterion in this vault predicts the former.** L2/C2 is exact over one exchange interval, which is why $\mathcal Q^\star$ ranks it at $+0.9998$ — a theorem cannot do otherwise. Over eight intervals that falls to $+0.718$, and the one-interval defect ranks the eight-interval defect at only $+0.719$. Marched $240$ intervals, the cut the best criterion selects crosses $1.0$ at interval $10$, peaks at $\mathbf{1.59\times}$ worse than the hand-chosen tiling at interval $90$, and returns to $0.90$ at $240$ — **two sign changes**. The eight-interval optimum ($0.5099\times$, a real $2\times$) is found by **no criterion**; the one-interval argmin sits $1.854\times$ above it | `open`, and it is the largest row this tier opens. L2/C2 is not wrong and nothing here retracts it — what is missing is any criterion for the quantity a decomposition is actually chosen for. Done when a cut criterion is stated **with a horizon** and measured over it, on a projected assembly rather than W105's bare one |
| **W124** | `atlas/cases/wake_array.py` `Rotor`, `ArrayTiling.offsets` | **Every x-seam of CS-6 and CS-7 passes exactly through a rotor disc, and the tiling rule cannot express anything else.** A `Rotor` is declared on an x-*adjacency* — the plane between window columns $i$ and $i+1$ — so a disk sits at the midpoint of an overlap by construction: the hand-chosen x-cuts are at cells $120$ and $232$ and the rotor planes are at cells $120$ and $232$. Worth $\mathbf{3.7\times}$ in one-interval composed defect, and the mean shear along the cut lines falls $0.3333 \to 0.0611$ when the search moves them off. **Every number those case studies published stands** — the seam was where it was and the instruments read what they read — but *"the tiling was chosen by hand"* understates it: the tiling could not have been chosen otherwise, which is why five case studies and a scaling ladder never surfaced it | `open`, small, and it is a **geometry** defect rather than a physics one. Pinned by `tests/test_tier25_seam_placement.py::test_the_hand_chosen_cuts_sit_exactly_on_the_rotor_planes`, so it cannot drift while it is open. Done when `ArrayTiling` can place a rotor off a window boundary — a change to the offset rule, touching no expert |
| **W125** | `atlas/compiler.py` `_cut_policy`, `atlas/graph.py` `MeasuredConstants.cut_defect_bound` | **A criterion can have a good argmin and a bad ordering, and nothing in the framework distinguishes them.** `cut_shear__mean` picks the exact optimum on three pools and ranks at $+0.291$; $\mathcal Q$ `__sum` ranks at $+0.726$ and picks a candidate $3.706\times$ off the best. **A search consumes the argmin; a bound consumes the ordering**; `_cut_policy` ingests one declared number and asks for neither, so a record cannot say which of the two properties its criterion has | `open`, small. Done when the record distinguishes *"this criterion selects well"* from *"this criterion bounds well"*, which is one field and a definition rather than a new measurement |
| **W126** | [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]], [[tier0-measurements]] §21.6, `atlas/cases/thermal_strain.py` | **A placement criterion for a co-located pair does not exist and cannot be a scalarization of one seam operator.** CS-9's two families share a domain, so a spatial cut cuts both — and they want it in different places. Distance from the heat streak ranks $\mathbf{-0.9988}$ against the conduction defect and $\mathbf{+0.9991}$ against the elasticity defect; over all $785$ candidates the two rank at $\mathbf{-0.0259}$, essentially uncorrelated. Conduction's optimum costs elasticity $3.60\times$; **elasticity's optimum costs conduction $795.4\times$**. The disagreement is not *in* the operator, so no basis fixes it: it is between two operators that share a domain. Three of four Phase C subsystems put two families on one body | `open`, and it inherits CS-9's own finding. Done when the framework can either state a joint criterion over both families' defects — a Pareto set, or a declared weighting with the weight on the record — or **refuse** to place a shared cut and say why, which is the honest option and is what W115's missing `Decomposition` member would attach to |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

**Two of the three go the other way from the usual direction, and it is worth naming.** Most tiers here find an earlier claim was too generous. This one finds §10.2's falsification was, on its headline axis, **too harsh** — $\mathcal Q$ is a weak positive predictor of placement in a real wake field, not an inverted one — while its *"the basis is doing unjustified work"* was **understated**: $42.5\%$ of $120$ real seams admit an orbit above $10\times$, the median is $5.652\times$ and the max $85.40\times$, with $\beta$ invariant to $2.859\times10^{-15}$ throughout.

And the third is a rule rather than a correction. **Three times now this vault has published a comparison that was true exactly as far as it was marched** — §18.5's state on a diverging trajectory, §19.6's repair that died at step $38$ from the developed state, and §21.3's placement that reverses at interval $10$. The standing rule §21.9 adopts: **a comparison between two configurations is not a result until it has been marched past the point where the two curves could cross, and the crossing has to be looked for rather than assumed absent.**

## The mechanism tally

Tier 19: *the measurement that decides is the one that removes a variable*. Tier 20: *the one that holds everything still and varies what nobody called a variable*. Tier 23's is about the question rather than the instrument.

**A criterion is not validated by predicting its own objective well.** $\mathcal Q^\star$ predicts the one-interval composed defect at $+0.9998$ because it is a theorem about that interval and cannot do otherwise, and that number says nothing about whether a decomposition chosen with it is a good decomposition. The correlation that mattered — objective against outcome — is $+0.719$, and it was the last thing measured rather than the first.

And a second, cheaper one: **the largest finding in the tier needed no run at all.** Comparing the hand-chosen x-cuts against the rotor planes is integer arithmetic on two declarations. It is now a test, and it says six case studies have been cutting through their own turbines since Tier 18.

# Tier 24 — the interface moves, and the design parameter is a knob (2026-09-03)

Full record: [[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]]. Reproduce with `python scripts/w127_ground_effect.py`; artifact `out/w127/w127.json`; tests `tests/test_tier27_ground_effect.py` (47), $879$ s end to end on the development box.

> **Dating.** Measured and written 2026-09-03, verified against the environment clock before stamping, per Tier 15's rule.

CS-10, [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4's Phase B second row and the eleventh real case study. A 2-D wing section over a **moving floor** at ride height $h$: six exposed `reference.WindowNS` windows and a `ProjectedAssembly` produce the field, and the integrated load crosses a **field-to-lumped `MECH` seam** to a two-line algebraic suspension, $k(h_0-h) = L(h)$, whose solution moves the interface every macro-step. The unsplit tightly-coupled solve is the referent and it is the *same code path* at a one-window tiling, so the zero-cut control is bitwise.

**The gate is met, and it is met in the direction Tier 23's standing rule was written about.** Over $240$ macro-steps the split reproduces the referent's ride-height history to $2.5\times10^{-3}$ at the peak of the transient and $1.3\times10^{-4}$ at the end — and it **crosses**: every lagged column peaks at step $\sim62$ on one side of the referent and finishes on the other. Read at step $62$ the split undershoots; read at $240$ it overshoots. This is the first case study to apply that rule as a gate rather than discover it.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| **W97** | *"a field-to-lumped `MECH` seam is one-sided by the cell Reynolds number, structurally"*, `open` since Tier 18, blocking *"every lumped subsystem in the car"* | **`done`, by the candidate repair the row itself names.** At the wing seam §2.2's normative effort measures $\lVert S_{\text{SUSP}}\rVert / \lVert S_{\text{fluid}}\rVert = \mathbf{19.4}$ at the exchange interval ($77.4$ at the macro-step — a spring's block is proportional to the interval it is probed over, so the ratio needs a **cadence** quoted with it exactly as $\sigma$ does under W86), and [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §4.1's conservative co-normal takes it to $\mathbf{0.3806}$, **below one**, with $\beta$ up $21\times$, $\kappa$ down $10\times$ and the informative window $85\times$ wider. **W47 scoped that form out at fluid–fluid seams because the advective term cancels across a shared ring cell; there is no second fluid side at a wing and it does not cancel** — which is what [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4 predicted. Two things the row did not anticipate are in [[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]] §5 |
| **W30** | **`done`** in Tier 0 with a drift number on *static* seams, emitted *"even on static runs"* so a moving one would have something to compare against | **the comparison is made, and it inverts the economics.** Moving: $\mathbf{3.77\times10^{-1}}$ relative over $K=10$, against $2.51\times10^{-2}$ on the same seam held still and $2.4\times10^{-4}$–$3.5\times10^{-3}$ on Tier 0's four static ones. The fluid–fluid seam beside it stays inside the Tier 0 band whether the wing moves or not, so the drift belongs to the **interface's motion** and not to the flow developing around it. At a $2\%$ staleness tolerance a cached $S$ survives **one macro-step**: $240$ re-probes over the march, $4320$ solves against the march's own $5760$, $\mathbf{75.0\%}$ of the whole run **for one seam of eight**. `InterfaceMotion`'s three `measurements_required` are supplied for the first time since the slot was written |
| **F5** | *"gradients through the composed stack are too noisy to optimize with"*, scheduled at **CS-10** by [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §7 after CS-8 spent its budget elsewhere | **measured, not falsified, and it fails in a way the criterion does not describe.** Reverse-mode adjoint of the settled downforce with respect to $h_0$ — through every sub-exchange of every window's solve, the blend, the global Leray projection and the seam's own Newton solve at both ends — agrees with a central finite difference to $\mathbf{1.32\times10^{-6}}$ at $\delta = 10^{-7}$, on a clean truncation branch monotone over five decades with the cancellation branch not yet reached. The objective is bit-reproducible ($0.0$). One gradient costs $4.3$–$6.7$ forward evaluations. **What bit is not a learned artefact — there is no learned expert in this column — it is the horizon: $\mathrm dJ/\mathrm dh_0$ is $+0.411$ at $40$ macro-steps and $-0.095$, $-0.152$, $-0.055$ at $80$, $160$ and $240$, and every one of those is confirmed by its own finite difference** |

|---|---|

A **design sensitivity** taken from a rollout has exactly that shape: measured here it changes **sign** between a $40$-step horizon and an $80$-step one. W123 asks for a cut criterion *stated with a horizon*; W128 asks the same of a gradient |

The level everything is read against is the plate's own settled unsteadiness, a median $\mathbf{1.256\%}$ peak-to-peak over the last quarter of a fixed-floor march |

| # | Where | What | Status |

| **W127** | `atlas/compiler.py` `_r10b_exchange_cadence`, `atlas/cases/ground_effect.py` `Suspension.dt` | **An expert's declared response interval is never compared against the exchange interval the compile derives, on any agent R10b does not read.** `CASE-STUDY-GUIDE` mistake 5 is this on the *field* side — hard-coding the sub-step count, measured at two orders of magnitude in $\tau$ — and **R10b** exists for it. R10b reads `substeps_per_macro_step` only on agents declaring `elliptic_subsolve=exposed`; a lumped algebraic agent declares `NONE`, is skipped, and its own response interval is never checked. Measured: `Suspension.dt` declared at the macro-step where R10b fixes `dt_native / substeps` makes the lumped block **four times too large** and moves W97's headline ratio from $19.4$ to $77.4$ — the whole of the number the row is about, with every other diagnostic healthy. **A fourth composition-layer defect wearing an agent's label** (W54's tally), and the first on the lumped side | `open`, small. Done when the compile compares each agent's declared response interval against the derived exchange interval and decertifies on a mismatch — one comparison, no new measurement |
| **W128** | `atlas/graph.py` `MeasuredConstants`, [[poc1-results-differentiable-design]], [[composition-error-theory]] | **A design sensitivity through a rollout is a function of the horizon and has no field on the record.** `sigma_lag` exists because **W86** established that $\sigma$ is a function of the lag and that quoting it without one is meaningless. A gradient has the same shape and a sharper failure: measured here it is $+0.411$ at $40$ macro-steps and $-0.152$ at $160$ — **it changes sign**, so a design search run at the short horizon moves the knob the wrong way, and every one of those numbers is confirmed by its own finite difference to $\le 2.5\times10^{-3}$. The mechanism is physics with two regimes (on the transient a higher $h_0$ shortens the descent, cuts $\lvert v_{\text{plate}}\rvert$ and *reduces* the aerodynamic unloading; at equilibrium the plate velocity is zero and only the ground effect is left, whose closed-form quasi-static limit is $-0.163$ and which the $160$-step adjoint matches to $7\%$). PoC 1a quotes its gradients at one horizon and says so in prose | `open`, and it is the largest row this tier opens because it is the one an optimiser consumes. R10 then decertifies it with *"an incompressible solver almost always contains a pressure solve"*. It is not a solver. `wake_array.RotorDisk` has carried the same decertification since Tier 18 and no page recorded it | `open`, small. The discriminator is already on the record — `stencil_radius = 0` and an `L_native` that is a body scale rather than a domain — so this is a condition on an existing branch rather than a new measurement |
| **W130** | `atlas/compiler.py` L4, [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §6.4(a), [[probed-dtn-coupling]] §4.5 | **A mis-wired agent and a genuinely uncoupled one produce an identical probed block, an identical $\Xi = 0$, an identical null count and an identical refusal.** Found by making the mistake: CS-10's plate is declared in *global* coordinates and a window sees it in its own frame, and with the x offset unsubtracted the plate sat outside the window's array, `grid_sample`'s border clamp returned a constant, and the probed fluid block came back **exactly zero**. The compile read $16$ excess null directions and refused at `L4/null-space` — a true statement about the matrix with nothing pointing at the cause. That is byte-identical to the case §6.4(a) describes and §4.5 celebrates, where a `bc_channel: none` agent's $\Lambda \equiv 0$ and *"the interface problem is not badly conditioned; it is empty"*. **The distinguishing evidence is free and nobody collects it**: an agent declaring a `bc_channel` above `NONE` and returning an identically zero block is contradicting its own declaration, which is `L1/E3`'s refusal class exactly | `open`, small, and it is a **diagnosis** defect rather than a gate one — no verdict changes, only what the refusal says. Pinned meanwhile by `test_the_probed_fluid_block_at_the_wing_seam_is_not_zero`. Done when an identically zero block on an agent that declares a boundary channel refuses *naming the contradiction* rather than refusing on the null count |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

**Two of the three named mechanisms turned out to be right about the class and wrong about the seam.** W97 says the one-sidedness is *"structurally, by the cell Reynolds number"* and quotes $81\times$; measured at a second lumped expert on a second geometry it is $19.4\times$ at one cadence and $77.4\times$ at another, and the sharper statement is that §2.2's effort is the **viscous traction alone** while the lumped side returns the **whole** traction — two different physical quantities under the same declared `response_half`, which `L3/C9` cannot see. W69's *"the wrong half differs from the right one by an unseparable $O(1)$"* has a counter-example here: the two **correct** halves differ by four orders of magnitude.

And W97's verdict — `blind` — does not transfer at all. At this seam the certificate is informative under every effort, because a **rigid** lumped partner contributes a rank-$1$ block against $\dim M = 17$ and $\beta$ collapses $90\times$ below the fluid block's own norm. The ratio and the rank are independent axes and only the first was named.

**And the third is Tier 23's own rule, discharged.** *A comparison between two configurations is not a result until it has been marched past the point where the two curves could cross.* This is the first tier to write that into a gate: every lagged column's difference from the referent peaks on the transient and finishes on the other side of zero, and the artifact records the sign-change count beside every headline. It also caught the tier's own largest finding — §6's gradient changes sign between horizons, and a study that had taken one number at $40$ macro-steps would have published the wrong sign with a finite-difference check confirming it to $2.9\times10^{-4}$.

## The mechanism tally

Tier 19: *the measurement that decides is the one that removes a variable*. Tier 20: *the one that holds everything still and varies what nobody called a variable*. Tier 23: *a criterion is not validated by predicting its own objective well.* Tier 24's is about what a check can and cannot see.

**A verified number can be verified against the wrong question.** Every finite difference in §6 confirms its adjoint to three or four digits, at every horizon, including the horizon whose sign is wrong. The check that would have caught it is not a better check on the derivative — the derivative is exact — it is asking *of what*. The same shape appears twice more in the tier: `L3/C9` verifies that both sides of a seam declare the same conjugate **half** and cannot see that they mean different **quantities**, and `L4/null-space` correctly counts sixteen null directions in a block that is zero for a reason it never asks about.

And a second, cheaper one, in the same family as Tier 23's: **the two findings that cost nothing were both comparisons against a number already in the vault.** W30's static drift band had sat unused since Tier 0 and is what makes $3.77\times10^{-1}$ mean anything; W97's $81\times$ is what makes $19.4$ readable as a confirmation rather than a fresh measurement. Neither needed a run.

# Tier 25 — the multirate defect gets a function, and a gradient gets a horizon (2026-09-04)

Full record: [[case-study-brake-thermal-atlas-0.1]]. Reproduce with `python scripts/w131_brake_thermal.py`; artifact `out/w131/w131.json`; tests `tests/test_tier28_brake_thermal.py` (42), $623$ s end to end on the development box.

> **Dating.** **Measured 2026-09-03, written 2026-09-04**, and both are on the record: the run finished at 22:01 and the page did not. Tier 15's rule is to verify the environment clock before stamping, and one date here would have been wrong by the other.

CS-11, [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4's Phase B third row and the twelfth real case study — the last row before Phase C. A brake disc against its cooling duct: `thermostruct2d.ThermoStruct2D` conducting through a $2.5\,\mathrm{mm}$ cast-iron wall, backward Euler, `EMBEDDED`, against a low-Mach convecting duct, explicit, at the **$500{:}1$ the physics gives**. One `THERM` seam. The **single-rate** solve — the same code path with the exchange interval set to the duct's own clock — is the referent, so the zero-lag control is exact rather than approximate.

**The gate is met on its first branch, and the finding is which variable the bound is in.** $\sigma$ is first order in the exchange interval and bounded by two constants a **probe** supplies plus one rate the **run** supplies; and the **clock ratio turns out not to be an independent variable at all** — $8\times$ of it at a fixed interval moves $\sigma$ by $1.00016\times$. That is what makes the bound usable: a real conjugate seam runs at $10^4$–$10^5$, no referent can be built there, and a bound checked at ratio $5$ applies at ratio $10^5$ because the ratio is not in it.

Ratio $1$ is the single-rate control and its defect is **exactly zero**. The candidate remedy the row names — **W17**'s $W>1$, admissible here because R3's `bc_time_varying` holds on both agents — is worth $3.6\times$ to $56.6\times$ per interval and $1.84\times$ over a $200$-interval rollout, non-monotonically, because the ratio between a first difference and a second is the trace's *curvature* |
| **W128** | *"A design sensitivity through a rollout is a function of the horizon and has no field on the record"*, `open` since Tier 24, *"the largest row that tier opens because it is the one an optimiser consumes"* | **`done`, as the definition the row asks for plus a second independent measurement.** [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] **§0.4** is the definition — a third numbered convention, binding on L7, L8 and L9, covering all three temporal parameters that silently move a reported quantity — and it promotes the gradient case to a **refusal** rather than a decertification, because a $\sigma$ without its lag is a bound of unknown tightness and a sensitivity without its horizon can point the other way. The measurement: $\mathrm dJ/\mathrm dU$ on a brake duct's inlet velocity runs $+0.0616 \to +0.1863$ (peak, $N=100$) $\to \mathbf{-2.402}$ at $N=1000$, **changing sign between $N=150$ and $N=200$**, on a truncation branch where three FD steps spanning $4\times$ agree to $1.77\times10^{-3}$ and the objective is bit-reproducible. **$N_{\text{sign}} = 200$ ($1\,\tau$); $N_{\text{valid}}(20\%) = 800$; $N_{\text{valid}}(10\%)$ is NOT DETERMINED over $1000$ macro-steps.** The sign is settled after one thermal time constant and the magnitude is not settled after five |

The flux at a cell is set by the temperature *at* that cell. So there is no cutoff wavelength to convert, and the arithmetic consequence is that **a rank-$m$ truncation discards exactly the fraction of modes it drops** — $81\%$ at the $m=11$ a cutoff-derived declaration would have given, $17.8\%$ even at $m=31$. `thermal_seam` declares $16$ modes on $48$ cells *"as everywhere else"*. **Nothing it published is wrong** — $\beta$ moves $0.05\%$ between $m=11$ and $m=32$, because an operator with no small singular directions has none to lose — so the truncation costs the interface **space** and not the conditioning, and what a $16$-mode space could not have represented is a seam disagreement above its sixteenth mode | `open`, small, and it is a **declaration** defect rather than a numerical one. Done when the record can say *"no cutoff: the response is diagonal-dominant at $\kappa = X$, declare the grid"* as a first-class value rather than forcing a mode count that implies a cutoff nobody measured |
| **W134** | `atlas/cases/*.py` `fourier_basis` | **The shared Fourier basis returns a zero column at the Nyquist mode.** At $k = n/2$ the cosine samples at $\cos(\pi(i+\tfrac12))$, which is zero at every cell centre on a midpoint grid, so `column_stack` hands back a rank-deficient matrix and every projection built on it silently drops a direction. The **sine** at that $k$ is the real Nyquist mode. `window_ns`, `thermal_seam` and `wake_array` all declare $m \ll n$ and cannot reach it; found by being the first graph to declare $m = n$, and fixed in `brake_thermal`'s own copy only | `open`, small, and it is **latent rather than active** — no published number is affected, because no other case study declares $m$ near $n$. Done when the four copies are one function that either skips a numerically-zero column or refuses to return a rank-deficient basis. Worth doing *before* the next case study that declares a full-rank interface space, which W133 argues every conjugate seam should |
| **W135** | `atlas/graph.py` `MeasuredConstants`, `atlas/multiphysics.py` `interface_power` | **`sigma` has no provenance field for which side's response the interface power was evaluated through, and the two sides differ by $5.7\times$–$8.6\times$.** Interface power is $\int_\Gamma e\,f$ and $f$ can be read off either agent's `boundary_response`; the two agree **only at a converged trace**, which is exactly the state a lag defect is defined away from. Measured here by making the mistake: $s_\Gamma$ taken through the disc's flux against a $\sigma$ taken through the duct's made a correct bound over-predict by up to $8.6\times$ and read as a lag-profile effect, which is a real phenomenon and was not this one. **This is W58's shape one field along** — *which of two inequivalent definitions was this number measured by* — and W58 closed that question for `cut_defect_bound` by adding `cut_defect_bound_form` | `open`, small. Done when `sigma` carries the side (or the convention) its power was evaluated through, the way `cut_defect_bound_form` carries its definition, with a decertification when it is absent. No new measurement: the discriminating pair is in `out/w131/w131.json` |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

**The gate asked for a function of one variable and the answer was that it is a function of a different one than the rule assumes.** R4 is written about the exchange interval and R9 about the clock ratio, and every discussion of multirate in this vault has treated them as one axis because R4 ties them together. Separated — by pinning the interval and refining the fast agent instead — they come apart cleanly: the **interval** carries the staleness and the **ratio** carries R9's flux transient, which is the $62\times$-smaller term. So the two rules were not disagreeing about size; they were about different quantities, and nothing said so.

**And the scope has a live edge that the substitution campaign owns.** The independence is measured for a fast agent that sub-steps internally at its own stability limit — a classical explicit solver, for which `dt_native` is a bookkeeping choice. A frozen checkpoint cannot refine its own march, so at a fixed interval a coarser ratio means genuinely larger steps and the ratio re-enters. That is R4's asymmetry restated as an experiment nobody has run.

## The mechanism tally

Tier 19: *the measurement that decides is the one that removes a variable*. Tier 20: *the one that holds everything still and varies what nobody called a variable*. Tier 23: *a criterion is not validated by predicting its own objective well*. Tier 24: *a verified number can be verified against the wrong question*.

Tier 25's is about the **norm**, and it cost the largest single error in the tier. **Two quantities that are equal at the state a bound is calibrated at are not equal at the state it is applied to.** $s_\Gamma$ and $\sigma$ were both interface power, both correct, both measured on the same seam over the same interval — and read through opposite sides of a bond that balances only at convergence. The bound over-predicted by $8.6\times$ and every diagnostic was healthy, because the defect was in *which* of two agreeing definitions each half used, at the one operating point where they stop agreeing. W58 is the same shape on `cut_defect_bound` and W66 on the `THERM` flow variable; this is the third, and the general form is that **a bond has two sides and a number read off it has to say which.**

And a second, in the family Tier 23 and Tier 24 both contributed to: **the protection and the destruction were in the same file.** `scripts/w131_brake_thermal.py` writes its artifact after every stage — the discipline that makes a $10$-minute sweep safe to start — and began each invocation from an empty dictionary, so `--stages constants` alone destroyed what the other nine had written. Fixed by loading before writing, and recorded because neither half is wrong on its own: incremental persistence is a protection only if the next invocation reads what it protected. `w106_reuse_probe.py` already did this correctly, by care rather than by rule.

# Tier 26 — a rule finds the premise it never checked, and an FSI seam that no certificate can see (2026-09-04)

Full record: [[case-study-wing-fsi-atlas-0.1]]. Reproduce with `python scripts/w136_wing_fsi.py`; artifact `out/w136/w136.json`; tests `tests/test_tier29_wing_fsi.py` (28).

CS-12, [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §4's **Phase C first row** and the thirteenth real case study. A 2-D wing section: the flow over $\Omega_{\text{fluid}}$ from CS-10's column **unchanged** — six exposed `reference.WindowNS` windows and a `ProjectedAssembly` — against **quasi-static plane-stress elasticity** over $\Omega_{\text{solid}}$ from `thermostruct2d.ThermoStruct2D.solve_mechanical`, meeting at the wetted surface as a co-dimension-1 `MECH` seam. **The first graph in this vault whose two agents solve different continuum problems, are coupled both ways, and meet on a surface.** The referent is the monolithic FSI solve at a one-window tiling with the same structural solve, so the zero-cut control is bitwise and the split differs by the cut alone.

**The row's gate is met, and the tier's headline is a rule rather than a number.** `L2/R10`'s own sentence names a premise — *"the graph decomposes the domain"* — that the rule never checked, and W114 had been open on exactly that since Tier 22. Checking it is one comparison, it clears **four** graphs' false refusals, and the control that says the narrowing is not an escape still refuses.

## Rows this tier closes

| # | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| **W114** | *"`L2/R10` refuses a co-located split and its own derivation does not reach one … Done when R10 consults whether any agent's own domain is actually cut — decidable from `Decomposition` plus the agents' declared domains — and CS-12's wing FSI is the next graph to hit it"*, `open` since Tier 22 | **`done`, by exactly the check the row asks for.** An `EMBEDDED` agent is refused only when the graph contains **another agent of the same `governing_family`** — the compile-time signature of *"a larger region of the same physics, of which this agent has been given a piece."* It is a **proxy** and it is **conservative**: two agents of one family on disjoint regions are still refused, which is a false refusal that inspection resolves where the other direction would be a silent admission. **Four graphs' verdicts move and no measurement in the vault moves with them** — `thermal_strain` `surface-mech`, `brake_thermal` `as-built`, `thermal_seam` `as-built` and `wing_fsi`, every one a multiphysics graph where nothing was cut — while **`window_ns` `as-built` keeps its refusal**, four windows of one family tiling one domain being exactly the class R10 was derived on. Checked by a control: declare the structural agent with the fluid's family and `L2/R10` fires again |

Done when `assemble_seam` consults `orientation` and E7's passivity is read on the oriented sum, with a test on this seam showing the defect moves |

Priced: an implied surface velocity of $2.43\,U_\infty$ and a load $120.9\times$ the one being balanced, against CS-10's $5.77\,U_\infty$ at a lumped seam. §4.2 was written as a rule about lumped partners and is a rule about partners with no mass |

## What this tier says about the ones before it

**A rule's own sentence can name a premise the rule does not check, and nothing downstream notices.** R10's text has said *"the graph decomposes the domain"* since Tier 0 and the implementation read `elliptic_subsolve` alone. It refused correctly for twenty-two tiers because every graph it saw was a tiling of one family; the first multiphysics graph it met was `thermal_seam`'s at Tier 13, and the refusal there was already wrong. **This is not the silent-wrongness class — a false refusal is loud — but it is its mirror, and it cost CS-11 a scheme variant built to escape a refusal it never needed.**

**And the largest single error in the run was a cadence, again.** `solve_interface` solved the seam's equation over the **exchange** interval and the lagged scheme then held the answer for a whole **macro-step** — four of them. That is a built-in overshoot of exactly `EXCHANGES`, and it diverged at macro-step 9 with every diagnostic healthy. It would have been reported as *the added-mass instability of loose Gauss–Seidel*, which is the exact question §4 of the ladder's CS-12 row asks, and the honest answer once the interval matched the hold is that loose coupling is stable at every one of eighteen $(E^*,\text{lag})$ cells. `CASE-STUDY-GUIDE` mistake 5 — *a composition-layer cadence wearing an agent's label* — arriving on the interface solve, and the **fourth** composition-layer defect on this project to do so.

**A second, smaller instance of the same shape, and it is why $\mathcal R$ is quoted where it is.** The interface power is applied `EXCHANGES` times per macro-step and was read once per macro-step, which is an $O(\Delta t)$ error in a quantity $\mathcal R$ is then asked to close to $O(\Delta t^2)$. It read as a coupling defect at $7.2\times10^{-2}$.

## The mechanism tally

Tier 19: *the measurement that decides is the one that removes a variable*. Tier 20: *the one that holds everything still and varies what nobody called a variable*. Tier 23: *a criterion is not validated by predicting its own objective well*. Tier 24: *a verified number can be verified against the wrong question*. Tier 25: *a bond has two sides and a number read off it has to say which*.

Tier 26's is about **premises**: **a rule states the hypothesis it needs and then tests something else, and for twenty-two tiers the two agreed.** R10 is the instance; the halo rule is the same defect one rule along (W136); and `Connection.orientation` is a third field that exists, is declared, and is read by nothing (W138). The general form is that **a declaration nobody consults is indistinguishable from a declaration nobody made** — and the way to find them is to build the first graph where the untested premise is false.

## Tier 26a — CS-12 verified before anything was built on it (2026-09-04)

**Nothing was allowed to assemble CS-12 until its own gates had been marched past the horizons they were read at.** Two of the three rollouts on [[case-study-wing-fsi-atlas-0.1]] were shorter than the gate they served — $\mathcal R$ at $120$ macro-steps against the gate's $240$, and the $(E^*,\text{lag})$ stability map at $80$, which is **two and a half exchanges** of the interface equation at its loosest cell. Both were re-run and **both gates hold and strengthen**. Two *sub*-claims do not survive, both for the same reason, and both are corrected on the page.

| what was checked | at the published horizon | re-run | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| $\mathcal R$ closes across the FSI seam **and the crossing is looked for** | $120$ steps, ordering read off **maxima** only | $480$ steps, ordering compared **step by step** | **holds and strengthens.** $\lvert\mathrm dE/\mathrm dt - P\rvert \ge \lvert\mathrm dE/\mathrm dt\rvert$ at $\mathbf{0}$ of $480$ steps, worst instantaneous ratio $0.289$; the settled residual falls from $6.206\times10^{-6}$ over $[60,120)$ to $\mathbf{1.263\times10^{-7}}$ over $[240,480)$ |
| the aeroelastic response matches the referent | $240$ steps, crossings found at $30,70,97,156,171,207$ | — | **holds as published.** Already marched past the crossing and the cut's own defect already peaked, at step $68$ of $240$ |
| **W114** has a deliberate resolution, not a bypass | — | read in `compiler.py` and re-run | **a genuine handle.** `_r10_elliptic` refuses an `EMBEDDED` agent only when another agent shares its `governing_family`; the FSI graph compiles with **zero** refusals; the **control** — the structure re-declared with the fluid's family — comes straight back at `L2/R10`; `window_ns` `as-built` keeps its refusal |
| the added-mass interface solve is **stable**, not merely undiverged | $80$ steps for every cell, i.e. $2.5$ exchanges at lag $32$ | $320$ steps, floor of **ten exchanges of each cell's own equation** | **holds, on evidence the short map could not give.** $18$ of $18$ undiverged over $10$–$320$ exchanges; the amplitude growth that exists is $2.672\times$ in the **tightly coupled** column against $2.664\times$ at lag $1$ and $0.223\times$ at lag $32$, so it belongs to the flow and not to the coupling, and at $0.60\%$ of the tip it is still under the plate's own $1.503\%$ settled unsteadiness |

**The two sub-claims that moved are the same defect twice, and it has a name now.**

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W139** | `atlas/cases/wing_fsi.py` `WingStructure.stress` | **The method returns the stress at $E_{\text{ref}}$, not at $E^*$.** It forms `p = 0.5 * (stiffness @ d) / scale`, which is the traction that would produce $\boldsymbol\delta$ at $E_{\text{ref}}$, and re-solves on an operator assembled at $E_{\text{ref}}$ — so the answer is $(E_{\text{ref}}/E^*)$ times the stress at the state it was asked about, a factor of $2$ at CS-12's own operating point | `open`, small, and **latent rather than active**: nothing on [[case-study-wing-fsi-atlas-0.1]] is a stress and the method is called nowhere in the package — CS-12 says in its own §11 that *"no stress figure here is a statement about an alloy."* W134's shape exactly: a defect that no published number depends on, found by being the first thing to need the quantity. Found while [[poc2-frontwing-results]] was building a stress ceiling, which needed a map that is linear in the traction and therefore built its own. Done when the method either carries the scale or is deleted in favour of the map |
| **W140** | [[end-to-end-architecture-spec]] §0.4 | **§0.4 makes a horizon binding on a SENSITIVITY and on nothing else, and the two quantities that moved here are neither.** §0.4 was derived from **W128**, where $\mathrm dJ/\mathrm dh_0$ changed *sign* with the horizon, and it is written about sensitivities: a gradient reported without its horizon is refused. CS-12 then quoted a **residual** over a *"settled half"* that a $4\times$ longer march shows was still transient by $49\times$ — the $99\%$ the half-step term removes there is $-7\%$ over a genuinely settled window — and a **drift** over a map $2.5$ exchanges deep, whose $5.0\%$ becomes $0.37\%$ at $320$ steps. **Neither number was wrong about its own horizon and neither was reported with one**, because the rule that would have required it does not reach them | `open`, and it is a **specification** row rather than a code one. Note the direction: both errors were *conservative* — the residual and the drift both improve when marched — so this is the safe end of the class and the next instance need not be. Done when §0.4's three fields bind any quantity read off a rollout, with the two cases distinguished: a quantity read at the **end** of a march needs a horizon, and one read over a **window** needs the window and the evidence the window is what it is called |

**And the discipline this tier adds to the case-study procedure.** A stability map's horizon is per cell and is counted in **exchanges of that cell's own interface equation**, never in macro-steps: `scripts/w136_wing_fsi.py`'s `MIN_LAG_INTERVALS` is a floor every cell is marched to in its own clock, and `_try_march` now returns the amplitude of the tip oscillation over successive quarters and the headroom to the constitutive envelope, because **a survival flag cannot distinguish a stable scheme from one that has not reached the bound yet.** The discriminator that settles it is free and nobody was collecting it: run the *tightly coupled* column through the same statistic, and whatever it shares with the loose ones is not the coupling's.

**One cell touches the expert's own envelope at the longer horizon**, and it is the one the next page's design box is drawn around: at $E^* = 3\times10^{4}$ the peak $\lvert\delta\rvert$ reaches $0.049822$ against `WingStructure.validity`'s $0.05$, on the release transient. Headroom is $1.62\times$ at $5\times10^{4}$ and $1.0036\times$ there.

## PoC 2 — CS-10 and CS-12 assembled, and what an assembly finds (2026-09-04 / 2026-09-05)

> **Dating.** The assembly, the gate, the residual and the first design search ran **2026-09-04**; **W145** was found late that night and the design search, the penalty sweep, the ablation and the cost measurement were all re-run on **2026-09-05**. Both dates are on the record, per Tier 15's rule — one of them alone would be wrong about half of this section.

[[poc2-frontwing-results]]. **A capability demonstration, not a ladder rung**: CS-10's ground-effect graph and CS-12's FSI graph composed into one, with **both surface seams live at once** on the same plate, differentiated in four design knobs, and a **constrained** search run on it against a population baseline. Nothing about the fluid column, the composition layer, the wing model or either partner was rewritten — `FrontWingRollout` *subclasses* `FSIRollout` so `cut`, `blend`, `project` and `band` are inherited rather than copied, which is the only way to say "unchanged" credibly.

**The assembly's own gate is that each parent is a LIMIT of it, and both are measured.** Send the spring rate to infinity and the remaining $32$ equations must be `wing_fsi.FSIRollout.solve_interface` — measured at $\mathbf{3.37\times10^{-10}}$ relative, with the mount's velocity at $4.1\times10^{-11}$. Send the structural stiffness to infinity and the remaining scalar must be CS-10's, *including* its analytic derivative — $\mathbf{6.36\times10^{-8}}$ relative, with the bending rate at $9.7\times10^{-10}$. The second is written out in the driver and in the test rather than shared, so what is compared is the **equation** and not a call into the same helper.

### Rows this opens

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W141** | `atlas/compiler.py` L4, `atlas/graph.py` `Connection` | **A per-seam verdict is derivable from what the compiler already emits, and nothing derived it.** Every `Decision` carries a `subject` — an agent, a seam id, or an `AGENT.PORT` pair — and grouping them by seam takes fifteen lines: a decision reaches a seam when its subject *is* the seam, or is one of the two ports it pairs, or is one of the two agents it joins, and a graph-level subject reaches all of them. Done here in the driver and in the demo, asserted equal. **What it buys is not cosmetic**: on this graph the two surface seams are `refuse` and the seven fluid–fluid ones are `admit-uncertified`, and the two surface seams carry *different* decertifications (`wet`: `L2/R10/halo`; `mount`: `L2/R10`) — so "the graph is refused" was hiding that seven of nine seams are not the problem and that the two that are are not the problem for the same reason | `open`, small, and it is an **emit** row rather than a gate one — no verdict changes, only what the compile can be asked. Done when `CompileResult` exposes the grouping itself, so a caller does not have to know that `<graph>` reaches everything and `F10.wet:MECH` reaches one seam. **And one thing it must carry with it**: measured over the sixteen corners of the design box, **zero** produce a different per-seam verdict map, so a per-seam light is a property of the *declaration* and a caller must not be able to read it as tracking the design |
| **W142** | `atlas/cases/front_wing.py` `objective`, and any future constrained search | **A `logsumexp` smooth maximum overshoots the maximum it smooths by $\text{temp}\times\log n$, and inside a constraint that is a constraint error wearing the word "smoothing".** A hard `max` over a rollout's tail has a subgradient that jumps between macro-steps and an optimiser stepping on it chatters, so the penalty wants a smooth maximum; at a temperature of $2\%$ of the ceiling over a $30$-step tail the overshoot is **$14\%$ of a stress reading**. Caught before it was published, by writing the arithmetic down rather than by a failed run. The fix here is local — the temperature is $0.5\%$ and `objective` returns **both** the smooth margins (which drive the gradient) and the **true** ones (which decide feasibility), so the gap is measured at the optimum | `open` as a **standing note** rather than a defect: nothing in `atlas/` provides a constrained objective, so the next one will have to make the same choice. Done when a smooth-maximum helper exists that returns its own overshoot, or when the framework says a constraint is judged on the true quantity and never on its surrogate |

PoC 1 measures that premium on its own page — *"a gradient costs six to nine forward evaluations"* — and then never divides its $17.9\times$ and $48.3\times$ by it; PoC 1a inherits the convention for $8.0\times$ and $>30.9\times$. Measured end-to-end here, the premium is $3.67$ and it takes a $14.0\times$ rollout ratio to a $\mathbf{3.82\times}$ wall-clock one. **A second, smaller error runs the other way and this PoC made it first**: dividing by the population's *whole budget* rather than by the evaluation at which it actually peaked reads $22.2\times$ where the honest figure is $14.0\times$ — PoC 1a's convention is the correct one (evaluations *to a common target*) and PoC 2's driver had to be corrected to match it | `open`. **It does not overturn either page's conclusion**, which rests on a *trend* in $n$ that a constant per-iterate factor leaves intact, and both remain the strongest available baselines. Done when the two pages carry a wall-clock row beside the rollout row, and when the comparison arithmetic lives in one place instead of in three drivers' print statements. Fixed on this page already: `_compare` in `scripts/w141_poc2_frontwing.py` derives all three ratios from the recorded histories, `stage_recompare` re-derives them without re-marching, and [[poc2-frontwing-results]] §9.3 quotes all three and says which is comparable with what |
