Measured here: the two knobs that set the release height ($h_0$, $k$ — through $h = h_0 - L_{\text{ref}}/k$) come back at $2.0\times10^{-1}$ and $1.7\times10^{-1}$ at a $10^{-2}$ relative step where the two structural knobs come back at $9.4\times10^{-5}$ and $1.9\times10^{-3}$; and fitting log-log slopes, CS-10's own published $\mathrm dJ/\mathrm dh_0$ table runs $0.99, 0.63, 0.86, 1.01, 1.57$ against a central difference's $2$, which CS-10 read as *"a clean truncation branch, monotone over five decades"* | `open`. **It does not overturn CS-10's F5 claim** — the adjoint agrees to $1.3\times10^{-6}$ and the agreement was the claim — but the *characterisation* was wrong and the defect was sitting in a published table nobody had fitted a slope to. **Two things it costs**: a single-step finite-difference check on a ride-height knob can read $10^{-1}$ and mean nothing (which is why `stage_fdsweep` exists), and the stamped force is a first-order quantisation error in the *forward* march as well as in the difference. Done when the stamping is either differentiable in $h$ (a partition-of-unity weight across the two boxes) or declared as a quantisation with its cell size stated, and when CS-10 §6's table carries its fitted order. **[AI Inference]** on the mechanism: it is argued from *which* two knobs misbehave and from the shape of their curves, and has not been confirmed by instrumenting the corner |
| **W145** | `atlas/cases/front_wing.py`, `atlas/cases/wing_fsi.py`, `atlas/cases/ground_effect.py` — all three `objective` methods, and `atlas/demo_frontwing/engine.py` (all four fixed) | **A declared envelope was enforced on the march and NOT on the path the design search uses — so the search optimised its way out of it and nothing fired.** `run` checked both experts' envelopes at every macro-step; `objective`, which is what `stage_design` and `stage_penalty` call, checked neither. The first constrained run therefore walked the ride height down through `Suspension.validity`'s declared floor of $h > 0.0703125$ ($4.5$ cells) and reported as its answer a design that **settles at $h = 0.0350$ — $2.24$ cells**, against the $4.8$ cells [[case-study-ground-effect-atlas-0.1]] §2.2 measured down to; marching that same design through `run` raises at macro-step $4$ of $120$. The loose-ceiling run is worse: its optimum settles at $h = \mathbf{-0.0070}$, the mount **below the rolling road**. Bounding $h_0$ does not bound $h$ — the search buys ride height by softening $k$, since $h = h_0 - L/k$ — and the box was drawn on $h_0$. **And it is not this graph's bug: `wing_fsi.FSIRollout.objective` and `ground_effect.GroundRollout.objective` have the identical asymmetry**, each with a `run` that checks and an `objective` that does not. PoC 2 is only where a search walked far enough to expose it. **The demo is a third path**: its march and its optimiser both drive `macro_step` directly and inherited no check at all | **`open` as a rule, `done` as a defect, in four places.** Fixed here: `check_envelopes` is one method called from both of `front_wing`'s paths and from `Engine._absorb`, which every demo path funnels through; the same check added to both parents' `objective` loops. All read **detached** values, so none can enter a tape or move a number — CS-10's and CS-12's suites are $82$ green and unchanged, and this graph's reference design is bitwise unaffected. The run is **re-done with the check live** and the pre-fix record is kept beside it as `design_unenforced` / `penalty_unenforced`. **What is still open is the general rule**: a framework that can decline and does not decline on the path a search uses is worse than one that cannot, because the search is the thing that goes looking for the edge. Done when a rollout cannot expose an objective that skips its own envelope checks. **And how it was found is the lesson**: the ablation was extended to run at the optimum purely to replace an **[AI Inference]** in [[poc2-frontwing-results]] §7.2 with a measurement, and it crashed. A measurement added to remove a hedge found a defect that no gate, no residual and no certificate had seen |

### And two things the assembly found by running, not by inspection

**The residual found a missing initial condition that the gate could not.** The plate starts flat, so the structure's strain energy at release is identically zero — which makes it easy to carry one receiver's initial value and not the other's. The suspension is *already carrying the load* at release by construction. Taking the spring's energy **after** the first macro-step as its value **before** put the first step's residual at $\mathbf{1.81}$ against a static accounting's $1.00$ — the gate **failed** — on a march whose every other step closed to $5\times10^{-8}$. **A two-receiver balance has two initial conditions and only one of them is conspicuous.**

**And the half-step energy term belongs to every receiver.** CS-12 accumulates $\tfrac12\Delta\boldsymbol\delta^\top S_e\Delta\boldsymbol\delta$ because it has one; the spring's matching term is $\tfrac12 k\Delta h^2$, with the same sign, and carrying one without the other corrects one agent's accounting on a balance that adds them. With both, the correction halves the settled residual where CS-12's single-receiver version did nothing on its own settled tail (**W140**).

### What it says about the cost of running any of this

**More CPU cores make this workload $\mathbf{1.9}$–$\mathbf{2.0\times}$ SLOWER**, measured three times: a bare composed macro-step is $33.3$–$37.1$ ms on one thread and $63.4$–$73.3$ ms on eight. Six $80\times80$ windows in float64 with a $33$-square dense solve and one FFT per exchange are tensors small enough that OpenMP's fork/join per operation costs more than the operation. **Checked bitwise first** — $12$ macro-steps at one thread and at eight agree to the bit on the load, the ride height, the tip deflection and the peak stress — so the thread count is a **clock and not a variable**.

> **And that is the answer to whether any of this wants a GPU.** A workload that will not use eight CPU cores is bound on per-operation overhead rather than on arithmetic, and an accelerator adds launch latency and host synchronisation to the same operation count. The one column that *would* be GPU-shaped is a **frozen neural expert in the windows** — dense matmul, which is what [[poc1a-frozen-expert-results]] ran on an A100 — and **W137** is the row that says what certifying such a swap at this seam would cost.

> **Corrected 2026-09-10 (Tier 41, W137).** W137's cost was blindness, read off the fluid's share of the assembled operator. The certificate does not read the share: it reads $\lVert S_F\rVert$ against $\beta$, and on CS-12's wetted seam under the effort the march uses that is $1.39$, so a replacement of the flow expert that ignores its boundary data is refused at every tolerance. Measured on `wing_fsi`'s seam, not re-probed on this graph's; W137 is closed in Tier 41 below.

One gradient iterate costs $\mathbf{5.4}$–$\mathbf{5.8}$ objective forwards here — $5.48$, $5.77$, $5.39$ — which is *inside* CS-10's $4.3$–$6.7$ on its own column. **The first version of this paragraph said $4.8\times$ and $9.47$ and both were artefacts**: `stage_cost` timed a taped, back-propagated objective against a **bare march** that computes none of the objective's quantities, unwarmed, over $8$ macro-steps. A ratio between two differently-defined quantities is not a ratio — and the $9.47$ had already been *explained* on [[poc2-frontwing-results]] as the assembly's adjoint carrying two seams rather than one, which is how a wrong number acquires a mechanism and starts to look measured.

## PoC 2's demo, packaged — 2026-09-05

[[poc2-demo-and-novelty]]. The demo built for [[poc2-frontwing-results]] turned into the project's first artifact aimed at somebody who has **not** read the vault: five staged beats over the live march, each a measurement this project already made, plus the standalone `poc2-frontwing-demo` branch built by `scripts/w146_build_frontwing_bundle.py` — **4.3 MB**, one command, macOS / Linux / Windows, and **no network access at run time or install time past `pip`**.

**The bundle carries no weights, and that is a consequence rather than a saving.** Beat 1 offers the compiler Poseidon-T's *capability record* and not its checkpoint, because the verdict is a function of the declaration — so where the PoC 1a bundle is ~90 MB of CC-BY-NC-4.0 weights plus an install-time fetch of `scOT` from a repository that publishes no licence (**W120**), this one is first-party throughout and sidesteps the question entirely.

**What it demonstrates, per beat, is on [[poc2-demo-and-novelty]] §2.** The one to lead with is beat 1: the same graph compiled three times with three *descriptions* of the fluid expert goes 2 red → 2 red → **9 red**, and nothing about any model changes. It is **W93** staged so it can be watched, and it needs the six-window column — on the referent nothing is decomposed, `L2/R10`'s premise does not hold, and the rule correctly stays quiet, which is **W114** working and which the panel says out loud so a quiet rule is not read as a passing one.

### Rows this opens

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W147** | `atlas/cases/front_wing.py` `check_envelopes` | **One message named two bounds for two physically different violations, and read as wrong when the framework was right.** The suspension check was `if not (h > H_FLOOR and 0 < h0 - h)` raising a single sentence quoting *both* numbers — so a wing at $h = 0.348$ above a free height of $0.20$ was reported *"against a floor of $0.0703$"*, which is true of neither the violation nor the reader's understanding of it. The two are different physics: below `H_FLOOR` the lumped suspension has run out of travel; above $h_0$ the spring is in **tension**, which `Suspension` is not declared to model either | `done`. Split into two raises with the same combined condition, in the same order, so **no number moves** and Tier 30's $52$ tests are unchanged — the assertion they make is on the substring `"suspension expert's declared envelope"`, which both keep. **Found by pressing the demo's own button**, which is the argument for building one: a message nobody reads is a message nobody checks |
| **W148** | `atlas/cases/front_wing.py`, `atlas/cases/wing_fsi.py`, `atlas/cases/ground_effect.py` | **W145's fix landed as three implementations of one rule.** `front_wing` got a shared `check_envelopes` method; `wing_fsi` and `ground_effect` each got the same rule written inline in their own `objective` loop. Three copies of one rule is the **exact shape that produced W145** — one path had the check and another did not, and nothing made the divergence visible — so the fix reproduced the defect's precondition while removing its instance | `open`. Done when one checker serves all three, most likely on the shared `FSIRollout` base with the subclasses extending it. Until then `tests/test_tier31_frontwing_demo.py::test_the_engine_enforces_both_envelopes_on_every_path_by_default` asserts the property (**can this `objective` raise on a declared envelope, and is it guarded on `self.motion`**) rather than the name, which is the weaker check the situation licenses |

### And three things the packaging found by running

**The race was measuring contention, not search.** Beat 4 reports a wall-clock ratio between two search columns, and the live march kept running underneath it: the race reached $4\%$ of its budget in $25$ seconds. Holding the march for the duration takes it to **$75$ seconds total**. `atlas/demo/`'s measurement panel already had this discipline and this demo had not inherited it.

**And it was measuring cold starts.** Unwarmed, a three-step race reads a $\mathbf{25\times}$ adjoint premium where a warmed one reads $\mathbf{5.06}$ — inside CS-10's $4.3$–$6.7$. The gradient column ran first and absorbed the whole first-call cost for both. This is `stage_cost`'s $9.47$ against its warmed $5.4$–$5.8$, a second time, on the same graph, three weeks later: **an unwarmed ratio is not a ratio**, and the only defence that has worked is to warm both sides and time the warm-up separately so its size is visible.

**The ablation could be handed the wrong operating point, and now cannot be.** `freeze_each_seam` **measures** the height it pins the frozen suspension at, from the live column it marches first, instead of taking it as an argument. At the reference design a twenty-step march ends at $0.236268$ against the sixty-step settled $0.231339$ — $2.13\%$ — and at the optimum the equivalent error is $37\%$, which [[poc2-frontwing-results]] §7.3 records as having inflated the suspension seam from $8.16\%$ to $10.42\%$. The driver already passed the right value; the demo removes the opportunity to pass a wrong one, which is the stronger fix.


## Tier 32 — virtual control: observability measured, and the objective that cannot use it (2026-09-06)

[[control-observability]]. **One measurement and its controls**, run before anything was built on it. The question came from an identity rather than a defect: a globally receptive agent has $d_i = \infty$, so [[master-error-bound]] §4.1's indicator is identically $1$ and $\Pi = \max_j\sum_i\chi_{ij} = 1$ **exactly**, pinned by the partition-of-unity identity for every halo width and every ramp. The bound is not violated — it is **vacuous**, and the only factor left free is $\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$, the staleness of the artificial-boundary datum. Attacking *that* factor means solving for the datum instead of lagging it, which is a question about **observability**, and nothing in this vault had measured it.

**The answer is favourable and it is half of the story.** On one seam, Poseidon-T's control-to-jump map is **full rank $32/32$ at $\kappa = 4.48$** — better conditioned than `WindowNS` carrying its own elliptic solve ($19.4$), against $1.95$ for the R10-compliant one — stable to $1.005\times$ across probe states separated by $10.6\%$ and to $1.004\times$ across three decades of probe step. **Global receptivity cost it $\Pi$ and did not cost it observability**, which is the hypothesis the tier was built to falsify and did not. The other half: **minimising the proposed objective moves $\sigma$ the wrong way** — $6.6\times$ to $21\times$ — under Tikhonov over ten decades, truncated SVD over six ranks, and a step-length sweep, with $\sigma$ re-evaluated by marching at every point. The monolith's own datum in the same control coordinates reaches $0.685$ against an available floor of $0.678$, so the **channel carries the correction and this objective's minimiser is not it**.

### Rows this opens

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W149** | `scripts/w149_control_observability.py`, `out/w149/w149.json`, [[control-observability]] | **Is a globally receptive operator uncontrollable, or merely unconfined?** Gate 0 — does the monolith's own trace reduce the proposed objective, at $\Delta t = 0.05$, $0.01$ and $0.002$ — and gate 1, the spectrum of $T = \partial(\text{jump})/\partial g$ on one seam, against a periodic positive control, `WindowNS` in both elliptic configurations, and Poseidon-T | **`done`.** Gate 0 passes on the R10-admissible column at all three $\Delta t$ ($0.9884$, $0.9907$, $0.9760$, monotone along the whole line) and **fails on the column `L2/R10` refuses** ($1.0752$, $1.0199$, $1.0015$) — with R10's signature rather than flux balance's, the defect **growing** with $\Delta t$ where flux balance's grew as $\Delta t$ shrank. Gate 1's table is [[control-observability]] §3. Five controls, and the one designed to be able to fail did: the checkpoint's $\varepsilon$ sweep converges at $10^{-1}/10^{-2}/10^{-3}$ and **breaks $5\%$ upward at $10^{-4}$**, which is cancellation against its declared $10^{-6}$ floor arriving exactly where the declaration says it should |
| **W150** | `atlas/capability.py` — an `Accelerator = VIRTUAL_CONTROL` axis; `atlas/compiler.py` L4/L5 | **The axis this tier exists to price, and it is NOT opened as ready.** A scheme that solves for the artificial-boundary datum rather than lagging it attacks $\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$, which is the one factor left free once $\Pi = 1$. The observability precondition is now **measured and met** — $T$ full rank at $\kappa = 4.48$ on a frozen checkpoint, and the true datum expressible in the control's own coordinates to within $2.3\%$ of the achievable floor | **`refused` 2026-09-07, Tier 33, with the reason recorded** — and it is a transferable negative result rather than a blocked row. Write each agent as exact-plus-defect, $\mathcal E_i = \mathcal S_i + e_i$, and linearise the jump about the true datum $g^\star$: the exact parts cancel by construction and **the defects do not**, so $\arg\min\lVert\text{jump}\rVert^2 = g^\star - T^{+}(e_i-e_j)$. The optimiser finds the datum at which the two agents' **errors cancel on the overlap**, and making two biased models agree moves both further from the truth in a correlated direction. That predicts all three things Tier 32 measured and could not explain: every regulariser fails the same way (each shrinks toward $g_{\text{lag}}$, not $g^\star$); a **richer** control space makes it worse ($3.21\times$ worse on the full ring — predicted before it was looked up); and the scheme comparison has a sign, $\sigma_{\text{ctrl}}\lesssim C_\mu\lVert\Delta_e\rVert/\beta_{\text{ctrl}}$ against $\sigma_{\text{lag}}\le C_\mu\Pi\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$, so **virtual control wins iff the agents are accurate relative to the lag** — and $\tau \gg \varepsilon$ is this project's founding premise about frozen operators. **So it is structurally wrong for exactly the class Atlas is built around.** The confound was checked too: the pathology was measured on the R10-ADMISSIBLE column, at a venue $14.8\times$ *looser* than the scheme it names, so it had more room than the real cadence leaves it and still lost. [[interaction-horizon]] §6. Previously: Done when an objective exists whose *minimiser* is the truth: the one measured here is not, and that is a measurement rather than a worry — it drives $\sigma$ up by $21\times$ unregularised and captures $12.8\%$ of an available $32.2\%$ at its most favourable damping. **Nothing is implemented this tier**, deliberately. The best case for the whole axis is converting a refusal into `admit-uncertified` with a measured $\sigma$; $\tau$ is untouched and $\Sigma$ has no $\tau$ axis, so no amount of this produces a seam the framework would certify |
| **W151** | `atlas/compiler.py` `_r10_elliptic`, and the `r10_premise` field W114 added | **R10's own sentence names a premise list that a solved-for control is not on.** The rule's derivation says the elliptic error is *"flat in distance from the cut, flat in dt, and untouched by any halo, partition of unity or interface condition"* — three named remedies, and **virtual control is a fourth thing that is none of them**. W114 already established the template for this: R10's premise is checkable, and when it does not hold the rule admits with `r10_premise` recording *why*, rather than refusing outside the class it was derived on | `open`. Done when `_r10_elliptic` consults whether the graph's transmission solves for its interface datum, and either admits with a third `r10_premise` value or states in the refusal why a solved datum does not clear it. **Do not implement before W150** — a premise clause for a scheme nobody can build is a clause nothing can test, and W114's own lesson is that the narrowing has to be checkable or it is an escape. The measurement that would settle it is in hand: the elliptic column's $\sigma$ floor under a perfect seam datum is $0.863\times$ lagged, against $0.678\times$ for the exposed one, so a solved control demonstrably does **less** where the elliptic part is embedded — which is evidence *for* R10 rather than against it |
| **W152** | `atlas/cases/window_ns.py` `window_capabilities`, and [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]]'s `MECH` pair | **The declared port carries one ring component and the datum has two, and it costs almost all of the correction.** A `MECH` port declares the normal effort–flow pair, so a control living in the declared port space moves the normal velocity alone. Measured on one seam: the normal-only control reaches $0.36$–$0.73$ of the jump and captures $\mathbf{7.6\%}$ of the available $\sigma$ reduction; **widening it to both ring components reaches $0.93$–$0.95$ and captures $97.7\%$** — for $6\%$ of $\beta_{\text{ctrl}}$ and essentially nothing in conditioning ($\kappa$ $2.17 \to 2.33$) | **`in progress` — the declaration landed 2026-09-07 (Tier 33), the port algebra has not.** `ports.RingActuation` and `PortDecl.ring_components` / `.actuation` follow `ResponseHalf`'s template exactly, including `UNDECLARED` as the default so a record that has not thought about it is not read as claiming the favourable answer; `window_ns` and `poseidon` both declare **2 carried, 1 driven**. **No number moves** — nothing consumes the fields yet, $\Lambda$ and the matching condition both want the conjugate pair, and only a CONTROL wants more. What is still open is the port-algebra half: the vocabulary should say which pairs are actuatable and in how many components, per port TYPE, rather than each case study declaring it. **And the prediction the row came with is confirmed**: a richer control space gives the optimiser more room to absorb $e_i-e_j$, so the full ring takes $J$'s pathology from $6.58\times$ to $21.14\times$ (**worse**, by $3.21\times$) while the controllable fraction goes $0.36\to 0.95$ — which is **W150**'s theorem showing its face. Previously: **This is a fact about the declaration, not about the physics or the agent**, and it is invisible until something tries to use a port as an **actuator** rather than as a matching condition — which nothing in this vault had done. Done when the port algebra says which of its pairs are actuatable and in how many components, and the prolongation carries that rather than the case study choosing it silently. Note this does **not** touch $\Lambda$ or any published $\beta$: the DtN probe and the matching condition both want the normal pair, and only a control wants more |

### And two things the tier found by running

**The probe-state horizon was absent, not short, and the first version did not notice.** `beta_ctrl` came back flat to $1.0000\times$ across three states — because the settled state is a **fixed point**: $200$ macro-steps from it move the field $6.03\times10^{-4}$ relative and it saturates by step $40$. Three states along that trajectory are one state wearing three labels, and quoting stability from them would have been *positive controls need a horizon* in its purest form. Spinning up from uniform inflow under the same forcing gives $10.5\%$, $7.9\%$ and $0\%$ from the settled state — **two orders more motion** — at cell Reynolds numbers $4.24$, $4.74$ and $4.83$ against `WindowAgent.validity`'s declared $8$, so all three are **inside** the envelope and the spread is not bought with an out-of-envelope state. The last mark reproduces `s0_state.npz` to $0.0\times10^{0}$, a free check that the trajectory is the one that made the artifact.

**And the first three attempts at "does it help" measured the wrong thing, in three different ways.** The composed field over a two-window strip with the elliptic part exposed and no projection anywhere read $1.393\times10^{-2}$ under **every** control, because that is the splitting error of a scheme nobody proposes. Applying the composition layer's global projection took it to $5.883\times10^{-4}$ — and every control still read $1.0000$, because at one exchange per macro-step **R10b**'s cadence mismatch inflates $\tau$ and the published $\tau$ and $\sigma$ for this graph are $1.34\times10^{-6}$ and $3.73\times10^{-8}$: the quantity a seam datum can move sat **four orders below the number being watched**. Only the third form is a measurement — the composed step run twice in lockstep, once under the candidate datum and once under the monolith's own, so $\tau$ cancels exactly, which is `tier0_window_ns.paired_step`'s own construction. **A ratio of $1.0000$ is not a null result; it is a signal below a floor, and the floor has to be found before the ratio is quoted.**


## Tier 33 — the envelope, not the scheme: what a 0/1 indicator was standing in for (2026-09-07)

[[interaction-horizon]]. **One measurement and its controls**, in Tier 32's shape. $\Pi$ is not a quantity — it is a **0/1 envelope standing in for a sensitivity nobody had measured**. [[master-error-bound]] §4.1 bounds the assembled error at cell $j$ by $\lvert\text{err}(j)\rvert\le\sum_i\chi_{ij}R_i(j)\lVert\delta\lambda\rVert$ with $R_i(j):=\lVert\partial\mathcal E_i(j)/\partial g_i\rVert$, then substitutes $R_i(j)\le C_\mu\mathbf 1[b_i(j)\le d_i]$. Replace the indicator with the thing it stands for — $\Pi_w:=\max_j\sum_i\chi_{ij}\widehat R_i(j)$ — and **at one exchange per macro-step, where $\Pi=\mathbf{1.0000}$ for every agent in this vault and the certificate is therefore unavailable, $\Pi_w$ separates them $0.186$ / $0.496$ / $\mathbf{0.790}$** — local solver, same solver carrying its own elliptic part, Poseidon-T. **The bound is non-vacuous for a frozen neural operator for the first time**, by a factor of $1.27\times$, which is a real number and a small one.

**It is the same bound with one factor swapped, and that is what makes the control a control.** $\Pi_w\le\Pi$ always and reduces to it **exactly** under a hard cutoff — measured $1.000000$ at halo 1, where the overlap is narrower than the domain of dependence — so no past verdict can move silently.

The answer: Poseidon-T has **no finite interaction horizon** at any threshold from $10^{-1}$ to $10^{-10}$, so `elliptic_subsolve = NONE` is contradicted and the refusal stands, now measured. **And the field turns out to be the wrong question** — see **W154** |

### And the confound Tier 32 left open, settled

**Both of Tier 32's headline pathologies came from the R10-ADMISSIBLE column, and its venue was $14.8\times$ *looser* than the scheme it names.** The objection was that a pathology measured against $\sigma=3.73\times10^{-8}$ is unwinnable by construction. Read from the artifact: the exposed column's lag baseline was $5.5365\times10^{-7}$, because it ran at one exchange per macro-step rather than per sub-step (**R10b**). **So virtual control was given an order of magnitude more room than the real scheme leaves it and still lost by $6.6\times$ to $21\times$** — the confound is real, it is named, and it cuts the other way. The embedded row is a free validation of the instrument: it reproduces the vault's published as-built $\sigma$ to $0.5\%$ without being told it.

## Tier 34 — the constants exist on the front-wing graph, and it is still not green (2026-09-08)

**Opened by [[poc2-novelty-audit]] §3**, which found that the compiler had never certified anything and that the reason was **provenance rather than physics**: $L$, $\sigma$ and $C_\mu$ were measured in tier 0, on `window_ns`, at another state and another scheme, and were simply not declared on the front-wing records — so **W56**'s backstop forced `admit-uncertified` on every compile of it. The refusal was real and it said nothing about this wing.

`scripts/w157_frontwing_constants.py` measured all three **here**. `unmeasured` is now empty, W56's backstop no longer fires, and four decertifications cleared (`E5`, `W49`, `W56`, `eps_tol`). **The graph still does not reach `admit`** — and that is the useful outcome, because what stands is now five *named rules* instead of an empty ledger.

## Tier 35 — three of the five fall, and two of the three were the checker's own (2026-09-09)

**Opened by Tier 34's own table.** Five named rules stood on the fixed-shape front wing, and the table said what each one was: two recorded *false alarms* (**W136**, **W138**), one missing *measurement* (**W159**), one question about a declaration, and one read as a real scheme finding. This tier took the three that had a fix identified. **The front wing now reports two objections instead of five, and both of the two turn out to be checker defects as well** — which is [[poc2-novelty-audit]] §4's point surviving a third time and getting sharper each time.

**Nothing was tuned.** Each of the three closures carries a control that fires when the rule's premise is genuinely true, and the two that were *not* closed are recorded with their evidence rather than repaired, because narrowing five rules in one session on the one graph being certified is the pattern an audit exists to catch.

It read `time_discretization` and `stencil_radius` off every agent in the graph, so on `front_wing` and `wing_fsi` it decertified STRUCT for being implicit with a nonzero stencil, on a seam where $\Gamma$ is a *physical* boundary of $\Omega_{\text{solid}}$ and there is no overlap for anything to outrun | **`done`.** The requirement is now checked only against the agents `_decomposition_cuts` reports cut — **R10's own premise predicate, factored out and shared rather than restated**, so the two rules cannot drift about which agents the decomposition touches. ### And W58 arrives again, on a second tiling

The aggregated reference-free surrogate equals the max form to $\mathbf{1.6\times10^{-5}}$ here — and the contaminated sets are **not** pairwise disjoint: $512$ of $7424$ contaminated cells belong to up to **three** subdomains. So the theorem's hypothesis fails and the equality holds anyway, *tighter* than the four-window tiling's $1.00007$. That is W58's own *"agreement without the hypothesis is luck, and luck does not transfer"* reproduced on a sixth-window graph, and it remains luck: nothing in the measurement makes it a theorem. The **pairwise** form under-estimates by $17\%$ ($0.809$–$0.844$ of the aggregated), which is W58's *second* hypothesis — the aggregation — behaving exactly as the row says it does above one overlapping pair.

| `InterfaceMotion` | 0 | **4 refusals** | a **named hole**, not a stability rule |
| **verdict** | `admit-uncertified` | **`refuse`** | |

**Both remaining objections on the fixed-shape column are now checker defects.** Nothing about this assembly's physics is currently what stops it being certified — the ledger is complete, the cut criterion is measured and tight, both surface seams are passive under the orientation the scheme actually differentiates, and the halo requirement is met by the agents the decomposition actually cuts. **The first honest `admit` is two premise-narrowings away, both diagnosed, both with the discriminating field already declared, and neither taken here.** That is a deliberate stopping point and not a blocked one: the case for taking them is strong and the case for not taking them on the same day, on the same graph, in the same session that took three others, is stronger. It is the user's call.

**And the count is the finding.** Of the five rules Tier 34 named, **four were defects in the checker** and one was a missing number. Zero were facts about the assembly. [[poc2-novelty-audit]] §4 said *"at the headline seam the compiler's output is its own two defects"*; measured through, it was its own four.


## Tier 36 — CS-13, the first directed cycle, and the compiler cannot see it (2026-09-09)

**Rung 6 of [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]]'s climb, and the first graph in this package whose ports form a directed cycle.** `atlas/cases/cooling_loop.py`: a solid block cooled by a **closed** coolant circuit — passage, hot line, radiator, cold line and pump — that returns to the passage it left. One real expert (`thermostruct2d.ThermoStruct2D.step_thermal`, imported unmodified) and four lumped legs of the `disk.ActuatorDisk` class: closed form, zero fitted parameters, four lines of algebra each.

**Why it is a new topology and not a fifth thermal seam.** `spec-wind-farm-wake` §5.1 raised path-dependence of message passing as a real concern, and `wind-farm-implementation-log` then **withdrew** the only measurement read as evidence for it — the mirror residual was the frozen operator's own asymmetry, reproduced with no graph, no ports and no agents in the loop. The log's own words: *"sweep-order path dependence may still be present — nothing here rules it out. What is ruled out is that it is NEEDED."* The concern has been open since 2026-08-23 with **no graph that can test it**, and the reason is specific: every cyclic graph built here is an **overlapping tiling**, where the cycle is undirected and the scheme is *additive*. `front_wing`'s six windows contain the 4-cycle $F00\!-\!F10\!-\!F11\!-\!F01$ and it tests nothing, because all six step from the same assembled field and are blended — no window's input is another window's output. A coolant loop is the opposite: the cycle is **directed**, carried by `ADVEC`, and every agent's inlet is another's outlet.

### What was measured

| quantity | value | what it says |
|---|---|---|
| spread over 8 sweep orders, **1 sweep/macro-step** | $\mathbf{1.54\ \mathrm K}$ ($4.8\times10^{-3}$ relative), 5 distinct answers | a composition layer that sweeps a cycle once per step has made a modelling choice **nothing in the declaration licenses** |
| the same, after 8 sweeps | $1.32\ \mathrm K$ | it is not a transient anybody outruns in practice |
| the same, **at the fixed point** | $3.5\times10^{-12}\ \mathrm K$ ($1.1\times10^{-14}$ relative) | the cure is not an ordering rule — it is **solving** the loop instead of sweeping it |
| per-sweep contraction of the spread | $0.97405$ (4 legs), $0.9618577$ (3 legs) | the path dependence decays at a rate the circuit's **own dissipation** sets |
| loop balance $Q_{\text{block}} + W_{\text{pump}} = \sum Q_{\text{rejected}}$ | residual $2.0\times10^{-14}$ | the ladder's gate — and it is an **identity of the fixed point**, stated as such |
| the block's own first law | residual $3.6\times10^{-7}\ \mathrm W$ against $1787\ \mathrm W$ | **the gate that can fail**, and the only one here about physics |
| iterated vs closed-form fixed point | $1.7\times10^{-16}$ relative | the positive control on the solve |

**The loop balance is an identity and the page says so.** Substituting the legs' own definitions turns $Q_{\text{block}} + W_{\text{pump}} = \sum Q_{\text{rejected}}$ into $T_{\text{return}} = T_{\text{return}}$. It is a consistency check on the arithmetic — a sign error in any leg's `heat_out` breaks it, and that is asserted — and it is **not** evidence that the coupled physics is right. What can fail on physics is the block's first law, $\mathrm d/\mathrm dt\int \rho c_p T\,\mathrm dV = Q_{\text{outer}} - Q_{\text{wall}}$, taken across one `step_thermal` through the same face machinery the loop uses. Its floor was **measured rather than assumed**: swept over four decades of $\mathrm dt$ the relative residual runs $8.1\times10^{-14}$, $3.3\times10^{-8}$, $4.1\times10^{-12}$, $5.3\times10^{-11}$ — **non-monotone**, which is a solver tolerance and not a discretisation error, since the latter would have a trend.

**A seven-figure identification was measured and then withdrawn, and the withdrawal is the more useful record.** On the three-leg circuit the per-sweep contraction matched $\sqrt{\text{loop gain}}$ to **seven figures** ($0.9618577$ against $0.9618576$), which reads as exact and was very nearly written down as a law. On four legs it does not hold: $0.97405$ over 256–512 sweeps and $0.97381$ over 512–1024 against $g^{1/3} = 0.973926$ — agreeing to $10^{-4}$ and **drifting** — and the spread underflows to bitwise zero by 1536 sweeps before any asymptote is reached. The match was **true as far as it was marched**. What survives is a bracket: the rate is strictly inside $(g, 1)$ and within $10^{-3}$ of $g^{1/(n-1)}$ at both leg counts.

### Rows this tier opens

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W162** | `atlas/graph.py` `CaseGraph.detected_cross_points`, `cross_points`; `L2/I2/G1` | **A triangle in the agent adjacency is read as a geometric cross-point, and on a circuit it is not one.** `detected_cross_points` returns every mutually-adjacent triple when `cross_points` is unset — a good proxy on a tiling, where three subdomains meeting pairwise really do share a corner, and wrong on a circuit, where three legs joined by three *distinct planes* share no point at all. Measured: the three-leg build of CS-13 is **refused** at `L2/I2/G1` for a multi-valued shared cell that does not exist. **And the graph has no way to say so** — the escape is `cross_points`, and an empty tuple is falsy, so `cross_points=()` falls through to detection: a graph can declare *which* cross-points it has and cannot declare that it has **none** | **`done` 2026-09-09, Tier 39.** Both halves. `CaseGraph.cross_points` now has **three states, not two**: `None` is undeclared and runs the adjacency proxy, a non-empty tuple names them, and **`()` is a declaration of NONE** — previously unsayable, because an empty tuple is falsy and fell through exactly as silence did. Both circuits declare `cross_points=()` and the claim is about **geometry**: legs meet at inlet and outlet *planes*, one per seam, at distinct places along the path, so no cell is in two seams. `cooling_loop.build(n_legs=3)` compiles. And the refusal now **names its proxy as a proxy** when the subject was inferred — it says the triple came from the adjacency, that the inference holds on a tiling and fails on a circuit, and what to declare instead. **Control**: declare a cross-point on the same three-leg graph and the refusal returns, saying it was declared (a sentinel distinct from "undeclared") and the adjacency proxy names itself as a proxy in the refusal. Reproducible rather than written up: `cooling_loop.build(n_legs=3)` |
| **W163** | the whole compiler; `atlas/graph.py` topology | **The compiler cannot tell a circuit from a chain.** Compile CS-13 with the return seam and without it: **same verdict, same rule set, same per-agent decisions**, and the only difference in the entire decision record is the extra seam's own per-seam rows. So nine layers of admissibility see a directed cycle as a list of independent seams — while the measured consequence of the cycle is $1.5\ \mathrm K$ of order dependence at one sweep per macro-step, which is larger than several defects the framework does refuse over | **`done` 2026-09-09, Tier 39, as `L5/R13`** — written against BOTH circuits, which is the condition the row set. **The [AI Inference] was checked and is FALSE**, and that is the row's main finding: the gain is *not* decidable from the declared response. `boundary_response` is a same-port DtN map and a cycle's gain is a **cross-port transfer**, and on CS-13 the two are unrelated quantities — perturbing a leg's ADVEC trace moves the response by $2303.89$ J/kg per unit mass flux while the loop's transport gain is $a = 0.99926$ in *temperature*, reachable only by setting `leg.t_in` on the expert, which is reaching around the declaration. On CS-14 the ELEC response **is** the series resistance. Two circuits, two answers, so the rule **asks** and decertifies rather than guessing. **And it says something different and true on each**: CS-13's loop composes to $0.9238$ and contracts; CS-14's composes to **exactly $1$**, structurally — each element maps $V \to V - e + IR$, a translation, so KVL round a loop is a *constraint* and the potential has no isolated fixed point — and swept it is **refused**, which is the conclusion `CircuitSolve` had already reached by hand when it chose to solve rather than sweep. **The rule's own first version was the defect it was closing**: it fired at L2 and refused `powertrain` for a scheme the compiler had not chosen, since both circuits compile to `DIRECT_SCHUR`, which is order-free (R5's own sentence). R13 now runs after `_l5_l7_scheme` and its gain binds only for a **sweeping** accelerator; the cycle is reported either way. **The row's actual complaint is closed**: the closed and open compiles now differ by a graph-level rule, `{'L5/R13'}`, on both graphs. **And the detector had a hang in it**, found by stress-testing rather than by reading: elementary-cycle enumeration is *factorial* in the worst case and `directed_cycles` did not return on a complete digraph at ten nodes in two minutes. It now carries a **visit budget and raises past it** — a budget rather than a cap on the output, because a partial cycle list is worse than none: a rule reasoning over *some* of a graph's cycles is silently reasoning about a different graph. R13 catches it and decertifies, and says a budget is not evidence the graph has many cycles. Bounded, the worst synthetic case returns in $80$ ms and both real circuits finish inside a budget $400\times$ smaller than the default. A rule invented the same day the first cyclic graph appeared is a rule validated on one graph, which is the failure mode this whole direction is meant to avoid. Done when a second cyclic graph exists to state it against — CS-14's ERS loop is one — and the rule is written against both. **[AI Inference]:** the shape it wants is not an ordering constraint but an admissibility condition on the loop map — *a directed cycle is admissible when its composed gain is a contraction* — which is decidable from the same declared response the probe already builds, and which `LoopSolve._closed_form` already raises on when it fails. Not built |

### The four declaration errors the first compile found

Recorded because they are the case study's own first result and because each one is a thing the guide warns about, arriving:

1. **`L3/C4`** — `ADVEC` is a multibond and needs a conjugate pair **per passenger** on top of the base triple. With one passenger the two coincide, which is exactly why omitting it looked harmless; a two-passenger port written the same way would silently price the second at the first's scale.
2. **`L4/null-space`** — 16 modes declared on a **lumped** leg with one state, so the probed block had 15 null directions and the rule reported all fifteen. `PORT_SPECS[ROT]`'s own note says the shape: *"a lumped port is the case $\dim M = 1$"*. This is `CASE-STUDY-GUIDE`'s *"claiming resolution nobody measured"* with the arithmetic to prove it.
3. **`L7/R9`** — a $50\!:\!1$ clock ratio the legs do not have. $T_{\text{out}} = T_{\text{in}} + Q/(\dot m c_p)$ contains no time; a quasi-steady algebraic leg's native step **is** the macro-step.
4. **`L4/probe-base`** — W74 again: `probe_base` is one callable per **expert** and takes the **port**, and a leg carries two kinds of port whose traces are different physical quantities. One base for both put $320\ \mathrm K$ against $500\ \mathrm{kg/(m^2 s)}$ on the same seam, and the rule reported the two sides as $56\%$ of the base norm apart — which they were.

**The compiler found all four before anything ran**, which is the claim [[poc2-novelty-audit]] §2 says is the only one that survives, exercised on a graph written after the audit.

## Tier 37 — CS-14, the fifth port type and the second directed cycle (2026-09-09)

**Rung 7.** [[case-study-powertrain-atlas-0.1]], `atlas/cases/powertrain.py`, `tests/test_tier37_powertrain.py`. `wake_array.rotor_capabilities` declares a `shaft:ROT` port whose note reads *"unconnected: no drivetrain. The extracted power leaves the system here, and under the port algebra that is an OPEN PORT with a measurable power flow rather than an absence."* **This connects it**: the shaft drives a motor-generator, the machine sits in a DC circuit with a bus, a battery and an inverter, and the power that used to leave the system is followed to where it is stored and to where it is lost.

**Three things it can say that no graph here could.**

**1. `ROT` and `ELEC` carried for the first time — TWO of the five were open, not one.** Censused rather than recalled: `MECH`, `THERM` and `ADVEC` had carried real connections; **`ROT` was declared three times (`wind_farm`, `wind_farm_real`, `wake_array`) and every declaration says *"unconnected: no drivetrain"***, so it had never been in a `Connection`; and **`ELEC` had never been declared anywhere at all** — `rocket.py`'s docstring says so in its fourth line. This graph gives both their first seam, so all five of the closed vocabulary are now exercised. **The cost was one scale set and one prolongation**, which is the affordability claim being tested rather than restated: the port algebra promises $O(K)$ declarations rather than $O(K^2)$ adapters, and until this session that had never run past two governing families. It now runs on four. The type forced one real declaration: `PORT_SPECS[ELEC]`'s flow is a **current density** and its power is per unit area, so a lumped terminal needs a declared cross-section for $s_e s_f = s_P$ to close.

**2. The first cross-domain energy balance that is NOT an identity of its own solve.** CS-13's loop balance reduces to $T_{\text{return}} = T_{\text{return}}$ and its page says so. This one does not: the mechanical side is `disk.ActuatorDisk`, which contains no circuit; the electrical side is Kirchhoff, which contains no wake; and they meet through exactly one fact, $k_e = k_t$ — the same air-gap flux linkage seen from the two sides of it. Measured, the balance closes to $\mathbf{1.9\times10^{-15}}$ and **breaks in exact proportion when the equality is broken**: at $k_e/k_t = 0.95$ and $0.90$ the bridge residual is $0.05$ and $0.10$ to machine precision. **A theorem the code cannot break is a theorem the code is not resting on**, and that is the difference between this gate and CS-13's.

**3. W163's second graph, on unrelated physics.** Compiled with the return conductor and without it: same verdict, same rule set, same per-agent decisions, the only difference in the whole record being the extra seam's own rows. CS-13 found it on a coolant loop with `ADVEC` and a conduction solver; this finds it on a DC loop with `ELEC` and an actuator disk. **One graph is an anecdote; two on unrelated physics is the statement.** The rule is still not written — see W163's own row.

**And the first `validity` predicate in this package to catch a real defect.** The first build gave the machine SI constants against `wake_array`'s **nondimensional** shaft, and `MachineAgent.validity` declined: $k_e\omega = 0.19$ against a $3.6$ V battery, so the machine motored where the graph declared generation. **It caught a units error across a seam**, which is what the nondimensionalisation half of the port algebra exists for. The module is nondimensional throughout now, on both sides of the shaft.

### Rows this tier opens

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W164** | `atlas/cases/wake_array.py` `RotorDisk.probe_base`; `L4/probe-base`, `L4/block-share` | **The wake array's shaft port is one-sided AND declares a stalled base, and only connecting it could show either.** `RotorDisk.respond` ignores the trace on `shaft:ROT` entirely — a disk at fixed induction and fixed inflow delivers a torque independent of the speed asked of it, because its $\omega$ is tied to the inflow rather than to the load — so the rotor's block is **identically zero** and the machine carries the whole seam operator. That is **W97**'s class on a `ROT` port. Beside it, `probe_base` returns zeros for that port, which is a *stalled* turbine, while the machine declares the operating speed, so `L4/probe-base` reports the two sides $100\%$ of the base norm apart | `open`, small. **Left as it is rather than repaired**, because the declaration belongs to `wake_array` and because the instrument that would settle it exists: the response is *constant* in the trace, so `probe.base_sensitivity` clears the decertification outright — the guide's own sentence. **That it is not wired into this compile is the row.** Done when `L4/probe-base` consults `base_sensitivity` before decertifying, which turns a disagreement that cannot matter into an `admit` that says why |

### W160 fires on a third graph, and that is what the row needed

`L2/R10`'s undeclared-pressure-solve branch fires on the ROTOR: an actuator disk declares the flow's `governing_family` — correctly, since an algebraic closure inside a continuum problem is not a different continuum problem — with `stencil_radius = 0`, so it has no field and no pressure solve to hide. With `ground_effect.Suspension` and CS-13's four coolant legs that is **three unrelated graphs and six agents**. A row that fires on one agent is about that agent; a row that fires on three graphs is about a class, and W160 is now the latter.

### And W138's shape arrived on a third port type, in this module's own declaration

The first version flipped the sign on each element's `lo` terminal, which made one of the two blocks at every node negative and left `L4/E7/passivity` reporting $1.5\times10^{-5}$ on `mgu_bus`. With each terminal reporting in its own element's sense the assembled operator at a node is the **series resistance** of the two elements meeting there, which is positive because a resistive network is passive — so no `effort_normal` is declared on the `ELEC` seams: this really is the own-outward convention. **The rule that caught it is the one Tier 35 taught to see it**, three hours earlier and on different physics.

### Rung 8 scoped and NOT started, with the budget written down

**CS-15 `tyre_contact.py` was in this session's brief and is deliberately not begun.** The brief asked for it to be scoped explicitly and for the answer to be said plainly if it does not fit. It does not fit, and the reason is not the amount of code.

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W165** | `atlas/cases/tyre_contact.py`; a contact expert; `L3`, `L4`, `R7`, `E7` | **Rung 8 needs physics nobody here has, and it needs a decision before it needs a module.** Three findings are foreseeable and each is the kind this framework exists to surface, which is why the rung is worth its budget and why it should not be bundled with the cheap ones | `open`, **large** — a session of its own. **[Tier 42, 2026-09-10: the decision is taken and it is a NAMED HOLE; the module reshapes.** [[inequality-seam-admissibility]]. Measured: an inequality-shaped seam compiles to the same verdict under the same eighteen decisions in the same order as a linear one, and at the solved trace the reported residual is $5\times10^{-16}$ where the port-matching condition it stands for is violated at $4\%$ of the trace scale — a ratio of $10^{13}$, against a linear control where the two agree. So the repair is `L3/C10` **refusing** a declared condition class (**W182**), not a widened rule: no rule in the package has the premise. Prediction (c) is confirmed and sharpened (**W181**); prediction (a) is confirmed and **its own proposed diagnostic is refuted** — at a positively homogeneous kink the $\varepsilon$ sweep is stable to nine digits, which is the smooth control's nine; prediction (b) is NOT measured and needs the expert. The row's `[AI Inference]` that the page should precede the module is confirmed: the page cost six seconds of `numpy`, and the budget above stands for the *rung* rather than for its first module.]** |

**1. The expert does not exist and cannot be borrowed.** The build repo's `solvers/` holds `thermostruct2d`, `compressible2d`, `thermo`, `grid`, `atmosphere`, `oracles`, `riemann`, `trajectory`. None does mechanical contact; the one match for *contact* is HLLC's **contact discontinuity**, a gas-dynamics wave. Rungs 6 and 7 were affordable because `step_thermal` and `disk.ActuatorDisk` already existed and the work was declaration; rung 8 is the first that is not.

**2. And the interface condition is not an equation.** Contact is a **complementarity** condition — gap $g \ge 0$, pressure $p \ge 0$, $gp = 0$ — with a stick–slip switch on the tangential side. That is not an effort–flow pair in the port algebra's sense. The **bond** exists (traction $\times$ slip velocity is a power, so `MECH` covers the *variables* and no `PortAmendment` is needed), but **admissibility is a different question**: nine layers of this compiler assume a seam whose condition is an equality, and whether an inequality-constrained seam can be compiled at all is very likely a named hole. **That question should be settled on a page before it is settled in a module**, which is [[theory-closure-audit]]'s own argument for writing the architecture down before implementing it.

**3. Three premises break, and each is a finding rather than a nuisance.**

  * **The probe measures a chord across a kink.** `probe_block` perturbs the trace and reads a linear response; at a stick–slip boundary the response is not differentiable, so the block it returns depends on the probe amplitude in a way no `epsilon` sweep will settle — and every `epsilon` sweep in this vault has come back stable to seven digits, which is what makes this one worth running.
  * **`L4/E7/passivity` will fire, and correctly.** Coulomb friction is **non-associated**: its tangent is genuinely non-symmetric, as physics rather than as convention. That is the opposite of **W138**, where the non-symmetry was an orientation artefact and flipping one sign took the defect to exactly zero — so the two together are the discriminating pair the rule has never had, and running them side by side is most of the rung's value.
  * **R7's finite-difference branch is not licensed.** It is selected by `differentiable=NONE` plus `deterministic=True` plus a reproducibility floor, and its own sentence is *"none but deterministic and smooth"*. A stick–slip transition is deterministic and **not smooth**, so the rule's premise is false and the branch it selects computes a difference quotient across a switch. **This is R10's and W136's shape a third time** — a rule that states a hypothesis it does not check — and unlike those two it can be found *before* the measurement, from the declaration alone.

**The budget, honestly.** Writing a credible contact expert (Hertzian or penalty normal, regularised Coulomb tangential, a stick–slip solve with its own convergence control), grading it against an oracle, declaring it, and then measuring the three items above **each with its own positive control** is more work than rungs 6 and 7 together, and the findings rather than the code are where the time goes. **[AI Inference]:** the highest-value first move is not the module but the page — decide whether an inequality-constrained seam is admissible, and what the compiler should do when it is not — because if the answer is a named hole then the module's job changes from *coupling a tyre* to *emitting the measurement the missing rule would constrain*, which is a different and much smaller module.

## Tier 38 — CS-S1: the learned operator on the interface, and the ceiling on a guess

**[[case-study-neural-interface-atlas-0.1]], `atlas/cases/neural_interface.py`, `scripts/w166_neural_interface.py`, `tests/test_tier38_neural_interface.py`, `out/w166/w166.json`. 2026-09-09.**

The proposal: **let the neural operator predict the interface trace and let classical solvers own the subdomains**, because R10 refuses a learned expert for its receptive field and the interface is the one place a global receptive field is an asset. Three claims, and only the third was a hypothesis — it *cannot* introduce a silent error, it *needs no certificate*, and it *should be good at this*. **The first two hold and are measured. The third is false, and not because of Poseidon.**

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W166** | `atlas/cases/neural_interface.py`; the substitution campaign | **A learned operator as an interface PREDICTOR was never tried, and it is the one arrangement its refusal does not reach.** Nine of nine seams have refused Poseidon-T on R10 — a receptive field of 64 cells against a declared 2 (W93) — and every response so far has been to attack the receptive field. A predictor seeds a *convergent* iteration, so it is a preconditioner and not an agent: a wrong guess costs sweeps, not correctness. **[Corrected 2026-09-10, W177: the *nine of nine* is quoted without its condition.** It is `front_wing` compiled with `elliptic_subsolve=embedded`, a value W60 records as undeclarable for a learned operator, and it is ONE graph-level `L2/R10` decision naming six agents rather than nine seam verdicts. Under the record `poseidon_capabilities` actually builds, that graph is `admit-uncertified` with **zero** refusals.]** | **`closed` with a NEGATIVE.** Built and measured. The safety half holds: five starts, one fixed point, agreeing within $2\,\varepsilon$ including from pure noise at $10\times$ the signal, and `predictor_is_not_an_agent` checks the structural half with a control that can fail. The speed half does not, and the reason is structural: $m = \log(d_0/\varepsilon)/\log(1/\rho)$, so **a predictor moves $d_0$ and only the operator moves $\rho$**. `OneSweep` — the best possible field predictor, being the first iterate itself — saves **exactly one sweep in both arrangements at every tolerance** and costs exactly the sweep it saves. **A field predictor's whole budget is one sweep.** Poseidon costs $61\times$ that budget in the admitted arrangement, $7\times$ in the refused one, and its skill is $-1.20$ against amplitude-matched noise's $-1.60$ |
| **W167** | `atlas/probe.py` `alpha_star`; `atlas/scheme.py` `Accelerator`; `atlas/solve.py` `solve_interface` | **`alpha_star` has been emitted since Tier 0 and has never been used as the transmission condition it is.** In a Fourier interface basis the diagonal *is* the measured symbol mode by mode — the optimal Robin coefficient, read off rather than derived, which for a frozen expert is the only available route ([[atlas-and-standard-dd-theory]] §7). One thing reads it: `SeamOperator.mode_shares`, as a ratio. **Used here for the first time, and it works — damped.** Undamped it *diverges* while cutting $\kappa$ from $22.686$ to $7.345$, because Richardson needs the preconditioned spectrum inside $(0,2)$ and $\rho(D^{-1}S) = 2.976$: a condition number is not an iteration. At $\omega = 1/\rho = 0.336$ it beats plain Richardson $594 \to 172$ expert calls at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$ | **`done` 2026-09-09, Tier 39.** `Accelerator.ROBIN_RICHARDSON`, with `probe.robin_condition` producing the coefficient and its damping **from one $S$ in one object**, so a caller cannot take one without the other. The damping is CS-S1's measured $\omega = \min(1, 1/\rho)$, matched against `out/w166/w166.json` in the suite rather than retyped. **The guard's first version was wrong and its own divergence check caught it**: damping by $1/\rho$ is right for a *positive real* spectrum — what a passive seam has, and what CS-S1 measured — and false the moment $D^{-1}S$ has a negative eigenvalue, because Richardson's iteration matrix is $I - \omega D^{-1}S$ and a negative $\mu$ puts $\lvert 1 - \omega\mu\rvert$ above one for *every* positive $\omega$. It diverged on an indefinite synthetic seam at iteration 707. The predicate is now the **contraction factor itself** rather than a proxy for it. Two declines — a singular diagonal and a non-contracting spectrum — and neither silently becomes plain Richardson: `ROBIN_RICHARDSON` raises rather than running a different scheme under this one's name, small. **The measurement is done and the wiring is not.** `Accelerator` has no Robin-preconditioned branch, and the damping factor $\rho(D^{-1}S)$ is computable from the same probe that already produces `alpha_star`. Done when `scheme.py` can select it and `solve_interface` applies it, with the divergence guard in the same place as the coefficient |
| **W168** | `atlas/compiler.py` `_r10_elliptic`'s refusal message; [[master-error-bound]] | **`L2/R10`'s refusal is worth $149\times$ in coupling sweeps and its message says nothing about that.** The rule refuses an embedded pressure solve because the composed *error* is elliptic — measured at 99.8% of the total defect, flat in distance and in $\Delta t$. Measured here as a *rate*: same four windows, same overlap, same state, and the iterated coupling goes from $74.81$ sweeps per decade to $0.50$. Both branches are asserted, and the figures are asserted against the artifact so the rule and its measurement cannot drift, small, and it is a message rather than a rule. Done when R10's refusal quotes the cost alongside the error argument, and when the halo sub-rule says which of the two things it is bounding — **accuracy always, convergence only for an embedded agent**. **[AI Inference]:** the deeper statement is that R10's two branches are one condition and not two, because it is the embedded elliptic solve that makes the overlap load-bearing at all; writing that down is a theory change and belongs on [[master-error-bound]] before it belongs in the compiler |

The matrix-free arrangement, where a residual is two expert calls, is the one CS-16 measures in, and the vault has no accelerator for it. `_gmres_like` cannot be lifted: it forms $A = S^{\top}S$, which needs $S$ assembled, and CG applied directly to $S$ does not converge because $S$ is non-symmetric (asymmetry $0.153$ at `elliptic_subsolve=embedded`) | `open`, small. Done when a matrix-free branch exists — **GMRES rather than CG**, since GMRES needs no $S^{\top}$ — and when `Budget.allow_assembly=False` selects it, which is a flag `ProbeBudget` already has and nothing consumes. **Priced first**: matrix-free Richardson costs 594 expert calls at $\varepsilon = 10^{-6}$ against the probe-then-Newton route's 44, so the probe wins by $13.5\times$ and a matrix-free branch is worth having for the case where the probe is *unaffordable*, not for the case where it is merely expensive |

Not built, and it is a schema change rather than a rule |

Every prolongation is declared on a **port** and owned by one agent (40 of them, $2.22$ per agent); the interface space is derived from $\dim M = \min_i m_i^{\text{eff}}$ and each side prolongs into it independently; scale sets number **5, one per port type**, constant in $K$. Against $153$ pairs the per-pair column is $0.0000$. **What it does not cover**: the three joining seams do not exist, so the count is of the union's *existing* structure at 5 families and not of the cost of *adding* a subsystem. Each new seam needs two new ports, one per side — $O(1)$ per seam by inspection, unmeasured | `open`. Done when the joining seams exist and the count is re-run across them, which needs **W171** first. **Do not quote the zero as the $O(K)$ result without the caveat**: it is strong evidence about the *algebra* and no evidence about the *integration cost*, and the difference is exactly the thing rung 9 exists to find out |

### The probed $S$ is the missing coarse space, and that is now measured rather than argued

[[atlas-and-standard-dd-theory]] §2.2 argues a geometric coarse space is unavailable to a frozen expert — enlarging the subdomain changes $\mathrm{Re}_{\text{eff}}$ rather than the resolution — and GenEO is unavailable because a checkpoint exposes no local eigenproblem; §3 names the **probed Schur complement** as the substitute. Priced in expert calls **including its own 34-call probe**, on the seam where the one-level method is slowest:

| accelerator | $\varepsilon = 10^{-2}$ | $10^{-3}$ | $10^{-6}$ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Richardson, $\theta = 1/\lVert S\rVert$ | 192 | 288 | 594 |
| Richardson, $\alpha^\star$ undamped | *diverges* | *diverges* | *diverges* |
| Richardson, $\alpha^\star$ damped | 76 | 100 | 172 |
| Newton on the probed $S$ | **40** | **40** | **44** |

$4.8\times$ at the loose tolerance and $13.5\times$ at the tight one. In the *admitted* arrangement all four collapse to 38–52 calls because $\kappa$ is $1.2045$ and there is nothing left to accelerate — which corroborates §8.3's calibration pair ($21.73$ embedded, $1.196$ exposed) on a tiling and a state neither was taken at.

### What was fixed while building it

An artificial face's ring can **end on the domain boundary**: `C00`'s `yhi` ring spans the window's full width and its first cell sits at global $x = 0$, the inlet. Writing the iterate there let the domain edge drift — wrecking the edge by $7.0$ moved the sweep's output by $9.2\times10^{-6}$. `SchwarzExchange._domain_edge_mask` holds those cells, on the principle that **a cell on the domain boundary is a boundary cell whichever face of whichever window it belongs to**. The test that found it is kept, with a control showing an interior seam *does* move the sweep.

### And a note on what this does not reopen

**Nothing here rehabilitates fine-tuning.** The refusal that blocks substitution is R10 and R10 is about the receptive field, not the loss. This study's negative is about the **ceiling on a starting guess**, which no amount of training raises above one sweep. The two are independent and neither helps the other.

### W171 is scoped, and its [AI Inference] is FALSE

**The row's own instruction was to verify it rather than trust it, and the verification changes the shape of the work.** Three findings, all from `scripts/w171_decomposition_scope.py` and pinned in `tests/test_tier40_epsilon_halo.py`:

1. **The count is wrong in both numbers.** The row says *five rules branch on the axis and return early on the wrong one*. **Seven** functions read the axis, and **three** return having emitted nothing — `_halo_rule`, `_r12_conservative_assembly`, `_w49_sigma_branch`. The other four emit on both branches, so on the wrong axis they would say the wrong thing **loudly**, which is the cheaper failure and not the silent one the row is about. `_cut_policy` is the one that looks silent and is not: its wrong-axis branch emits `L2/C3` and *then* returns.
2. **The natural carrier is not the AGENT.** A cut is a **relation among several agents**, not a property of one: two agents of one family cut two different ways is one fact about one region and is unrepresentable on either agent. And an agent that is *not* cut — `front_wing`'s STRUCT, the sole elasticity agent — has no axis at all, so an agent-carried field needs a third state that `_decomposition_cuts` already computes separately.
3. **The carrier that works is the CONNECTION, and the field is already declared.** `Connection.geometrically_coincident` separates the axis **exactly**, on four graphs and with no exception: `front_wing` $7$ non-coincident (the fluid tiling's cuts) and $2$ coincident (`wet`, `mount` — a fluid-structure and a fluid-lumped interface, where $\Gamma$ is a **physical** boundary the tiling did not create); `wing_fsi` $7$ and $1$; `cooling_loop` $0$ and $5$; `powertrain` $0$ and $5$. Two overlapping graphs and two non-overlapping ones, which is this vault's own two-unrelated-graphs bar, met twice.

**Budget, stated so it can be refused.** The change is still a schema change and it is smaller than the row assumed: no new declaration, one derived per-seam axis (`overlapping` where the seam is not coincident, `non-overlapping` where it is, *not a cut* where the two sides are of different families), three silent rules taught to iterate seams, four loud ones taught to scope their subject, and one field each on `CaseGraph` and `Scheme` that must become a **set** — which reaches `Scheme.as_dict`'s `D_decomposition` key and therefore the emitted artifact and every test that reads it. `CaseGraph.partition_of_unity`, `overlap` and `overlap_cells` stay graph-global and would each need the same treatment before a union graph could carry a tiling **and** two circuits, and that is the part that does **not** fit in one tier alongside a measurement. **Left open**, with the scope recorded so the next session prices it from a census rather than from the row's estimate.

### What this tier did NOT do, named

**No rule was written and no rule was narrowed.** W173's measurement says do not write the halo's tolerance branch; W175 is diagnosed and left open on Tier 39's own discipline; W171 is scoped and not built. The only code touched is a `__main__` guard on a script's stdout wrapper — re-wrapping on **import** garbage-collects the importer's wrapper and closes the shared buffer under it, which killed the transverse run on its first `print`.

**And one premise this tier was handed turned out to be wrong**, which is worth recording as a habit rather than as an incident: *"seams refusing the only live donor: 9 of 9, all on R10"* and *"learned experts admitted into a seam: ZERO, ever"* are both figures about the **compiler**, and the campaign's instrument is the **certificate**. Counted, the first is conditional on an undeclarable declaration and the second is false at three of eight sides. Neither was recalled wrongly; both were quoted without the condition they were measured under, which is the same class as W132's *a verified number can be verified against the wrong question*.

It is closed anyway**, because the measurement it waited on came back the other way; the derivation moves to W178, on the graph where its premise holds |

At a base carrying torsion the same comparison reads cosine $-1.000$ on $s_{yz}$ and $-0.999$ on $s_{xz}$, and with the sign corrected $+1.000$ and $+0.999$. | **W180** | `atlas/probe.py` finite-difference columns and `support_reach`; `scripts/w173_epsilon_halo.py` `dense_seam_operator` | *(opened)* **A learned forward can switch branches where its own classifier changes its call, and a one-sided probe reads the jump as a slope.** NeuberNet's production mode multiplies its torsion input and output shears by a sign network's call, and likewise tension. Measured: the call changes a finite step from zero load — at $a^\star=8.1\times10^{-3}$ along the torsion far field on the elastic zero-torsion base, $1.8\times10^{-2}$ on the plastic one, $2.5\times10^{-5}$ along the tension far field at zero tension — and across an interval of relative width $2\times10^{-3}$ there the flux jumps by $8.5\%$, $9.0\%$ and $0.6\%$ of the physical response to a unit step, three to five orders more than linear elasticity moves across the same interval. With both loads signed the call never changes and the ladder is flat at $1.00$–$1.04\times$ down to the float32 floor | `open`. `probe.py`'s finite-difference columns, `support_reach` and `dense_seam_operator` all difference forward from the base at one step, and the regression branch fits random probes against the same one-sided zero response; the amplitude ladder that found Poseidon-T's float32 floor separates nonlinearity from cancellation, not a jump from a slope. Done when the probe differences both ways and declines, naming the amplitude, wherever the two disagree beyond its floor. **[AI Inference]:** contact (W165) has a kink by physics and this is a jump by architecture; one instrument would have to see both |

### CS-S2: branch (a), probed once, and not compact

NeuberNet — a NOMAD DeepONet mapping the displacement on a ring of $36$ sensors at $5\,R_n$ around a V-notch to the elastic-plastic stress inside it — read through `w173_epsilon_halo.measure` and `w173_transverse_reach.depth_star` unchanged, beside linear elasticity solved classically on the same disc, with split-step `WindowNS` re-run as the positive control and reading exactly zero from $r=14$ again, to the digit.

| outcome the brief named | what it would have meant | measured |
|---|---|---|
| compact, with a reference pair | the first route to a real admit | **no** |
| compact, no reference pair | W95 binds even for bounded donors | **no** — W95 does bind, and the donor is not compact |
| **not compact** | branch (a) narrows | **this.** On its $29$-sensor ring the $u_x$ port keeps $0.69$ of its norm outside $r=4$ where linear elasticity keeps $0.14$, and reaches $10\%$ at no radius short of the whole ring where the physics reaches it at $r=9$; poked at one sensor, its response keeps $95$–$139\%$ of itself from $0.5$ to $2\,R_n$ deep where the physics keeps $12$–$17\%$. Effective rank $6$–$7$ of $87$ for the whole coupled map, against $72$ |

**And the response it has is its training manifold's.** It matches linear elasticity along the tension far field to $8.3\%$ and the torsion far field to $12.1\%$ where both loads are signed; one sensor's worth of boundary data gets $6\%$ of the classical flux, orthogonal to it; and on its in-plane ports its operator differs from the classical one by $100$–$110\%$ of the classical norm — W177's null-replacement signature on a second architecture family. **[AI Inference]:** a bounded patch is not a bounded response. Branch (a) narrows to donors whose locality is a property of the operator rather than of its domain — MACE's finite cutoff, DeepFlame's point — and both are unmeasured.

### How it had to be run: six things that went wrong, each kept as a measurement

1. **The first run died at the transverse stage** looking up the sensor at $180^\circ$, which the pipeline writes as $-180$, having written nothing. Every stage now flushes the artifact.
2. **A pointwise flux at the hat knots does not converge.** The classical control's operator moved $36$–$42\%$ between two meshes; the Galerkin dual moves $2.8$–$6.4\%$.
3. **A trace clamped at the notch flanks** made the two subjects disagree at the flank-end sensors only; the pipeline's own gap filling replaced it.
4. **The hoop input was the wrong way round (W179).** The second run's torsion numbers were read with it; that run is kept locally, and the torsion control states both conventions.
5. **A test pinned `<= 13` for a control whose first exact zero is at $r=14$.** Tier 40 wrote *past $r=13$*, which is right; [[epsilon-halo-measurement]] also wrote *at $r=13$* twice, and both now carry dated notes. The test pins `== 14`.
6. **The checkpoint carries $2{,}198{,}346$ subnormal weights**, $9.8\%$ of its $22{,}450{,}322$ parameters. On this CPU an unguarded forward of the port's $120$ quadrature points took $61.5$ s and $0.28$ s with denormals flushed, with the output bitwise equal; the adapter now sets the flush. The third run, made with it, reproduces the second bit for bit on every in-plane and classical number, so the flush moved none of them.

### What this tier did NOT do, named

- **Task C, W171 part 1.** Scoped and did not fit beside CS-S2: seven functions read the decomposition axis (Tier 40's census), the field is referenced $81$ times in $17$ test files, and the field must become a set that reaches the emitted artifact through `D_decomposition` — a schema change, against a tier already carrying CS-S2's three driver runs and two controls. The brief's *83 references across 22 files* did not reproduce.
- **Not done:** W175; rung 8's page or module; rung 9; reopening or re-scoping W95; building W176; rebuilding any demo bundle. **Nothing was downloaded** — not NeuberNet's CC BY 4.0 Zenodo analyses, not a finite-cutoff donor — and no GPU was rented: with denormals flushed, the whole CS-S2 driver runs in under five minutes on one CPU thread.
- **Two premises in the brief were wrong**: the build repository carried $14$ uncommitted files, not two; and W95's row was `open` where [[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §6 had filed it *closed by classical-first*. **A third, flagged as wrong going in, was right**: the brief put the suite at 11–23 minutes against a 4h26m baseline recorded before this tier, and at this tree the same $1067$ tests and $45$ new ones ran in about $22.5$ minutes of summed test time, the five clock-sensitive files taking $2.5$ minutes between them. The baseline was the outlier, and its cause was not measured.
