## Tier 42 — rung 8's admissibility question, answered: an inequality seam compiles, and that is the defect

**[[inequality-seam-admissibility]], `scripts/w181_inequality_seam_probe.py`, `tests/test_tier42_inequality_seam.py`, `out/w181/w181.json`. 2026-09-10.**

W165's own first step, taken: **the page before the module**, which is [[theory-closure-audit]] §6's argument applied to the one rung whose physics this vault does not have. The question is whether a seam whose interface condition is an **inequality** — $g\ge0$, $p\ge0$, $gp=0$, with a stick–slip switch tangentially — can be compiled at all. It can, and the answer is a defect rather than a capability: **three graphs differing only in the shape of one `boundary_response` compile to the same verdict under the same eighteen decisions in the same order.**

No contact expert was written, no tyre case study exists, and neither was needed: the whole finding is available from the declaration plus the *shape* of one callable, which is what W165's row predicted for one of its three items and turns out to be true for all three.

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W165** | rung 8; `L3`, `L4`, `L5`, `R7`, `E1`, `E7` | **Its own first step is done and the rung is not.** The page exists, the split it made holds at the code (the bond is `MECH`, admissibility is separate), and its `[AI Inference]` — *the highest-value first move is the page, because if the answer is a named hole the module's job changes* — is **confirmed by measurement** | **`open`, and RESHAPED.** The module's job is now *emit the measurement the missing rule would constrain*, which is [[inequality-seam-admissibility]] §7's three steps and needs no contact expert. Rung 8's own done-when is §8's five clauses, and **clause 4 still needs the expert** — the passivity pair is where W165 priced most of the rung's value and a fixture cannot supply it. The budget's *more work than rungs 6 and 7 together* stands for the rung and not for its first module |
| **W181** | `atlas/capability.py:532` `probe_route`; `atlas/probe.py:911–912`; `atlas/scheme.py` `RULES["R7"]` | *(opened)* **R7 states a hypothesis it does not check, and it is the third instance of R10's and W136's shape — found from the declaration, before any physics.** R7's sentence is *"deterministic and **smooth** $\to$ finite differences with the step set by the reproducibility floor"*; `probe_route` is three lines and has **no third clause**. A stick–slip transition is deterministic and not smooth, so the branch it selects differences across a switch. Two things beside it that the row did not foresee, both counted: **no decision anywhere cites R7** — zero across three compiles, because `probe_route` is called once inside the probe and reaches the record only as a string, so the defect is not auditable from a run artifact — and **the reproducibility floor sets the probe step for nobody**. `step = budget.fd_step or max(1e-2, 100 * floor)`, so a floor under $10^{-4}$ loses to the constant: **$0$ of $24$** constructible agents and **$0$ of $19$** resolvable declarations in `atlas/cases/`, every one of which is `np.finfo(float).eps` or $10^{-6}$, with Poseidon's default at $10^{-5}$. All $24$ take the finite-difference route | `open`, small-to-medium, **pinned by two defect-asserting tests** (`test_W181_the_defect_R7_selects_a_branch_whose_premise_it_does_not_check`, `test_W181_the_defect_no_decision_anywhere_cites_R7`). Done when the route selection **emits a decision citing R7** with the step it chose and why, and when the rule's sentence and the code agree about what sets the step — whichever way that is settled. **Do not just add a smoothness field**: nothing can measure smoothness from a black box, and W182's declaration is the carrier if one is wanted. The $10^{-2}$ floor is a defensible guard against differencing into noise; what is recorded is that R7's *sentence* describes a mechanism that is inoperative |
| **W182** | `atlas/graph.py` `Connection`; `atlas/admissibility.py` C5 and a new C10; `atlas/holes.py`; `atlas/solve.py` `InterfaceProblem` | *(opened)* **A seam whose condition is an inequality is a NAMED HOLE, and today it compiles.** The equality is written down in exactly one place — C5's docstring, `admissibility.py:281`, *"$e_A = e_B$ and $f_A = -f_B$ on the seam"* — and the check there is that `connection.orientation` is a non-empty string. **Measured**: smooth, kink-at-base and kink-at-gap fixtures give identical `(layer, rule, verdict, subject)` sequences, identical verdicts, an identical envelope stamp **including `E1: holds`**, and $\beta$ spanning $0.13\%$. At the solved trace the reported residual is $5\times10^{-16}$ and the port-matching condition it stands for is violated at $4.0$–$4.4\times10^{-2}$ of the trace scale — a ratio of $7.4\times10^{13}$ and $9.3\times10^{13}$, against a linear control where the two agree at $1.204$. Two layers break silently (L4's chord, L5's affine solve), E1 is stamped `holds` where it is false **and cannot be declared otherwise**, C5's runtime residual reads a correct open patch as a fault, and L9 is the one layer whose theory survives — the master bound needs Lipschitz continuity, not differentiability | `open`, **medium, and it is rung 8's first module**. Done when `Connection` carries a declared condition class (`EQUALITY` default, `COMPLEMENTARITY`), `L3/C10` **refuses** the second citing a missing rule, a `NamedHole` emits §6.2's three numbers — the branch gap $\lVert J_+-J_-\rVert/\lVert J_-\rVert$, the switch rate per macro-step, and the violated-condition residual — and **every existing graph compiles byte-identically**. `refuse` and not `admit-uncertified`: the split rule says refuse the silent-wrongness class, and the $10^{13}$ ratio measures that the class is right. `refuse` and not a fourth `FailureClass`: `SILENT_WRONGNESS` already names it |

### The epsilon sweep settles at a kink, and W165's own discriminator is the thing this corrects

**W165 predicted the probe block would depend on amplitude *"in a way no `epsilon` sweep will settle"*, and named the right positive control** — every sweep in this vault has come back stable to seven digits. Run over nine decades, $\varepsilon\in[10^{-8},10^{0}]$, on one instrument:

| fixture | max drift | digits stable |
|---|---|---|
| smooth **(control)** | $2.493\times10^{-10}$ | **9** |
| kink-at-base | $2.334\times10^{-10}$ | **9** |
| kink-at-gap | $1.372\times10^{-1}$ | **0** |

**The chord is confirmed and the diagnostic is refuted.** At a kink sitting *at* the probe base the sweep is exactly as stable as the smooth control's, and the matrix it returns so stably is $J_+$ to $1.7\times10^{-16}$ while $J_-$ sits $26.1\%$ away and is never mentioned. One line of algebra says why: $\mathrm{relu}$ is positively homogeneous, $\mathrm{relu}(\varepsilon x)=\varepsilon\,\mathrm{relu}(x)$, so the one-sided quotient is *exactly* independent of the step. **A sweep's stability is not evidence of differentiability**, and the row's proposed test would have passed the case it was written for.

The offset kink is the one the sweep does see, and how it sees it matters: for $\varepsilon\le g=10^{-3}$ the block is **bit-identical to the linear control** — $\lVert S\rVert=4.368458912$ and $\beta=3.561716938$ on both — and it crosses at $\varepsilon=10^{-2}$, which is the compiler's own default step. So the default probe straddles the gap and reports a partly engaged contact; a smaller step reports none; and neither says which.

**This is [[epsilon-halo-measurement]]'s shape a second time** — *the proposal's own wording names the wrong test* — and the instrument that separates all three cases is a **two-sided** probe that declines where the sides disagree beyond its floor. That is **W180**'s done-when reached from the other direction, and W180's own `[AI Inference]` said one instrument would have to see both a kink by physics and a jump by architecture. It was right.

### The refusal a contact graph gets today is about the wrong property, and the control says so

`MotionClass.SOLUTION_DEPENDENT` is the only field in the schema a contact seam can truthfully set and that any rule reads, because a contact patch's boundary moves with the solution. Set it and `L2/InterfaceMotion` refuses, alone — **and it refuses the smooth control just as hard**. A rule that cannot tell the two apart is not adjudicating between them, and it *masks* the question: an as-declared compile stops at L2 and never reaches L3, so W165's question is not merely unanswered, it is unasked. Drop the motion declaration — the quasi-static patch rung 8 would build first — and the compile admits.

### What this tier did NOT do, named

- **No rule was written and no schema was changed.** W182 is opened, scoped and left `open`; W181 is diagnosed and left `open` on Tier 39's discipline. The only files added are a script, a test and a page; `atlas/` is untouched at this tier's HEAD, and `test_W182_no_inequality_branch_was_written` fails if a complementarity branch appears in `solve.py` without the page being re-read first.
- **Prediction 4b was not measured, deliberately, and the page says why in full.** A synthetic non-symmetric block would confirm only that the rule reads the symmetric part's smallest eigenvalue correctly, which is arithmetic and is already covered on a symmetric indefinite block. The W138 pair is informative **only** if the non-symmetry arrives from a real non-associated flow rule, because the question it answers is whether anything other than the analyst's prior knowledge separates a convention artefact from irreducible physics. That needs the expert, and it is §8's clause 4.
- **L7 is enumerated and argued, not measured.** R9 checks that clocks nest and that both sides can integrate; nothing asks whether the integrand is continuous *inside* the interval, and a macro-step containing a switch has one that is not. It needs a two-branch response plus one clock ratio — the small module's step 2 — and was not done here.
- **Not started:** rung 8's module; **rung 3** (Task 2); **W171 part 1 and W172** (Task 3, rung 9). W175, W95, W176 and W178 are untouched. Nothing was downloaded and no machine was rented.
- **One thing the brief predicted correctly and it is worth recording as a habit**: the brief said prediction (c) was findable from the declaration alone, before any contact physics. It was — and so, as it turned out, were (a) and the whole admissibility verdict. **The measurement that settled the rung's central question cost six seconds of `numpy`**, against a budget the row priced at more than rungs 6 and 7 together. The budget is not wrong; what it prices is the *rung*, and the page is what separated the two.

## Tier 43 — rung 3 is built, and the graph compiles

**[[case-study-learned-pair-atlas-0.1]] (CS-17), `scripts/rung3_learned_pair.py`, `tests/test_tier43_rung3_learned_pair.py`, `out/rung3/rung3.json`. 2026-09-10.**

**The first Atlas graph ever to hold two learned experts of different governing families.** Poseidon-T across a `MECH` seam from NeuberNet, with a $2\times2$ against the classical incumbents on the identical geometry so that every decision the pair costs is charged to one expert or the other by a control. **Expected a refusal; got none, in any cell.** The load-bearing reason is `L2/R10`'s W114 premise, and R10's positive control refuses in the same compile the moment that premise is met — so the admit is the premise clearing, not the rule being weakened. **No rule was touched in this tier.**

Neither clause applies: this graph shares no agent, geometry or port with the wind farm, so there is nothing to regress against; and neither available learned expert has a `THERM` port or can have one — Poseidon-T is incompressible NS and declares four `face:MECH`, NeuberNet is quasi-static mechanics, and **neither carries a temperature field at all**. A replacement is proposed in §9.1 rather than substituted quietly: **G1** the graph compiles and every decision is attributable (**met**), **G2** the composition runs and $\mathcal R(t)$ closes across the learned seam (**not met, not attempted**), **G3** at least one expert carries a certificate at this seam (**not met, structurally**) | `open`. **Rung 3 is built as a COMPILE and not as a RUN**, and may not be marked complete on G1 alone. Done when the gate is agreed and recorded on both plan pages, and when G2 is either met or replaced. **G2 needs a seam that is not a fiction** (W184), which means a second learned expert whose port is a surface the first one's physics actually touches — [[expert-donor-survey]]'s open question 3 |

Handing fluid traction to an internal material cut is wrong in a way no prolongation repairs, and `PortDecl.geometry` is **free text**, so all nine layers admit it in silence. So the defect is the checkpoint's. It is non-symmetry as an **architecture artefact**: a learned operator that is not passive because nothing made it so, beside the classical operator it approximates, which is passive by construction. **[AI Inference]:** the three separate by their *controls* rather than by the defect — a convention artefact vanishes under re-declaration, a physics one does not and its classical peer is also non-symmetric, an architecture one does not and its classical peer **is** passive | `open`, small-to-medium. In a two-agent multiphysics graph each agent is the sole one of its family, so R10 admits — explicitly, as `L2/R10/sole-family` — and it does so **even when Poseidon-T declares the `embedded` that W93 measured**. | **W187** | `atlas/cases/window_ns.py` `window_capabilities`; `atlas/capability.py` `lambda_ref` | *(opened)* **`lambda_ref` is declared per EXPERT and its correct value is a property of the GRAPH.** `window_ns.window_capabilities` declares `lambda_ref=None`, which is right for a **tiling** — there the referent is the monolith the windows were cut from, not the window — and wrong at a **multiphysics seam**, where the same solver is its own referent. `wing_fsi` already works around it by writing its own record with `lambda_ref="itself: reference.WindowNS, exposed, at this cell"`, and this study had to do the same: without that correction **all four cells of the $2\times2$ read $\tau$ UNDEFINED** and nothing was attributable. One record, two correct values, chosen by the graph it is dropped into | `open`, small. Done when the referent is declared where it is decided — on the **connection or the graph**, not on the expert — or when `window_capabilities` takes the referent as an argument so a caller cannot get it silently wrong. **The failure mode is silent and was nearly hit here**: a missing `lambda_ref` reads as *no referent exists*, which is W95's signature, so a declaration defect is indistinguishable from a structural obstruction in the emitted artifact |

### What the second learned expert costs: exactly additive, and each cost attributed

| cell | fluid | solid | decertifications | extra over CC | $\tau$ | E7 | $\beta$ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CC** | `WindowNS` | classical patch | 10 | — | **defined** | holds | $3.2916$ |
| **CL** | `WindowNS` | **NeuberNet** | 11 | `L4/E7/passivity` | UNDEFINED | **fails** | $0.5199$ |
| **LC** | **Poseidon-T** | classical patch | 12 | `L2/R10`, `L4/R2b/W46` | UNDEFINED | holds | $2.9227$ |
| **LL** | **Poseidon-T** | **NeuberNet** | 13 | all three | UNDEFINED | **fails** | $0.8878$ |

$\text{extra}(\mathrm{LL}) = \text{extra}(\mathrm{CL}) \cup \text{extra}(\mathrm{LC})$ as **sets**, with empty intersection. At the level of *which rules fire* there is no interaction term. **[AI Inference]:** that is not additivity in the bound — $\beta$ moves $3.2916\to0.8878$ for the pair against $2.9227$ and $0.5199$ for the singles, so the rules are a union and the constants they name are not. Nothing here measures a composed error.

### And the reference pair is a PAIR, which is what no substitution can show

$\tau$ is defined in CC and UNDEFINED in all three cells holding a checkpoint — but the three are not the same situation. In **LC** and **CL** the missing half is one side's and the seam has a classical peer that *does* declare a referent, so the repair is a measurement somebody could make. In **LL** there is nothing on the seam to appeal to: NeuberNet is defined on one disc and cannot be its own referent (CS-S2 §9), and Poseidon-T is fixed at $128\times128$ and cannot be evaluated on a larger domain at any resolution. **That is W95 on both halves of one seam simultaneously**, and a campaign that swaps one side at a time structurally cannot exhibit it. §3.3's [AI Inference] — *a negative from the campaign does not answer rung 3's question, and a positive would not either* — is confirmed, and this is its sharpest form.

### The learned expert imports its own expressiveness into the seam

`effective_resolution` is *"the expert's own measured spectral cutoff, not a chosen number"* and nobody had measured NeuberNet's. Measured here on the $29\times29$ `ring:ux` block, CS-S2's amplitude and Galerkin flux: **effective rank $3$ at $99\%$ of the energy, against the classical patch's $25$** on the same ring — the classical spectrum nearly flat at $0.83$ for its first five singular values, the checkpoint's collapsing from $0.4618$ to $0.0682$ after one. Through $\dim M=\min_i m_i^{\text{eff}}$ that reaches the compiler directly: a $128$-cell fluid face meets a $29$-sensor ring through a **three-dimensional** interface space, and the three is the checkpoint's. The rule is right — representing the trace more finely than the coarser expert can respond to buys nothing — and the consequence is that a learned expert sets the interface space for **every peer it touches, however capable**.

Beside it, `L4/block-share` reports the fluid **blind at every cell**: its share of the assembled operator is $1.49\times10^{-5}$ learned and $5.46\times10^{-4}$ classical, so Poseidon-T's block is $0.8\%$ of `WindowNS`'s on the identical geometry, state and face. **$1.49\times10^{-5}$ is W137's number on a third graph** — it read $1.44\times10^{-5}$ at `wing_fsi`'s aero-structure seam — so a fluid-structure `MECH` seam is the solid's operator whoever computes it, which is W97's one-sidedness promoted from a port-type pairing to a physics pairing.

### What this tier did NOT do, named

- **No rollout.** The verdict is `admit-uncertified`, so the framework calls the graph runnable, and it was not run. Two reasons, both about the fixture: the seam is a geometric fiction (W184), so $\mathcal R(t)$ across it would be a number about the fixture; and the two experts have no common time base — Poseidon-T's macro-step is a lead time into a one-shot map and NeuberNet is quasi-static, so the solid's `dt_native` is declared equal to the fluid's on `wing_fsi`'s precedent, which makes `E4` and `R9` pass trivially and is **a declaration of convenience** wherever a real rollout would need it to mean something. That is the gate's **G2** and it is open.
- **No rule was written, narrowed or weakened, and `atlas/` is unchanged.** The rung-3 graph lives in `scripts/` on CS-S2's precedent, because NeuberNet is unlicensed and local-only; **no test loads it**, so the suite passes on a machine that has never had the weights.
- **No composition error was measured.** No $\tau$, no $\sigma$, no $L$. The additivity result is about which rules fire.
- **W184's schema change was not built**, and its row says to price it against `_decomposition_cuts` first rather than build it for rung 3 alone.
- **Rung 9 (W171, W172) and rung 8's module (W182) were not started.** Nothing was downloaded and no machine was rented.

## Tier 46 — W172: the joining seams exist, and a join is O(1) in declarations and not in state

**[[joining-seam-cost]], `atlas/cases/integration_union.py`, `scripts/w172_joining_cost.py`, `tests/test_tier46_joining_seams.py`, `out/w172/`. 2026-09-11.**

[[case-study-ladder-to-f1]] §19.6's first item. **What joins what was checked against the port algebra before anything was built**, and two of the three candidates are not constructible: the tiling carries no temperature for `THERM`, and the clamped structure has neither a rotational degree of freedom nor a second loaded face. They were replaced by a radiator core in the flow (J1, `MECH`) and the rotor in the wing's wake (J3, `MECH`), beside the machine's heat into the block (J2, `THERM`) — one join per pair of subsystems.

| **W172** | the integration graph's joining seams, and the $O(K)$ count across them | **Build the three joining seams on a union carrying a real tiling and both circuits; re-run the count separating existing structure from joining cost, per seam and per family count** | **`done`, 2026-09-11.** The joined union — 18 agents, 5 families, 48 ports, 24 seams, **no open port and zero per-pair declarations** — compiles; its one refusal is `L7/R9`, which the disjoint union has too, and reconciling ten agents' clocks admits both. The counter reproduced Tier 39's table first, and the disjoint union equals its three parts declaration for declaration. **A join's declaration cost is constant in the family count** — J1 identical at 4 and 5 families, J2 at 4, 5 and 5 with J3, J3's declarations at 3 and 5 — and **no join costs "two new ports and nothing else"**: J1 splits an existing seam, J2 moves an existing port's response and needs a unit reconciliation, J3 re-declares the rotor's two open ports for its host. **Adding a subsystem adds exactly its own Tier 39 structure** in both insertion orders, plus nine graph-level fields reconciled once per union. **The caveat as written is retired and replaced**: declarations are $O(1)$ per join, and what a join re-derives is not local to it (W196) |
| **W192** | `CaseGraph.regions`, W114's proxy | *(priced)* **No join moves a region; adding `powertrain` to the tiling does, in both orders** | `open`, unchanged in kind and now priced: across both insertion orders `ROTOR` is the only agent whose region changes, and it changes when its subsystem meets the tiling, never when a join is made. W136's scope defect, one rule along, on the clocks | `open`, **medium**. Both ways to pay today are union-level — re-declare ten agents' clocks, or give eighteen an integrated response. Done when R9's premise is the multirate seam and its requirement the agents at one, with the disjoint union as the control that stops refusing and `thermal_seam` at native clocks as the one that must still refuse |

Done when L3 compares the two sides' reconstructions of a common coefficient, with the host-measure form as the control that still admits |

Ports on agents **off J3's own seams** that move at their own base: **3 without J2, 6 with it** — at the same agents, families and pairs. - **Rung 9's integration graph — the brief's third task — was not started.** W182, W183–W187, W190, W191, W193, W175, W178 untouched. Nothing downloaded, no machine rented, NeuberNet not loaded.

## Tier 47 — rung 9's gate, restated: neither clause fits the full-vehicle graph as written

**[[rung9-gate-restated]], `scripts/rung9_gate.py`, `tests/test_tier47_rung9_gate.py`, `out/rung9/`. 2026-09-11.**

The brief's third task. [[f1-pathmap-and-end-goal]] §3 gives rung 9 the gate *"$\mathcal R$ closes; composition error sub-linear in $N$"*; Tier 46 built the graph, and this tier held each clause against it before attempting anything else — the brief's instruction, and W183's precedent.

| # | Gap | Task | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **W198** | [[f1-pathmap-and-end-goal]] §3's rung-9 row; [[rung9-gate-restated]] §5 | *(opened)* **Rung 9's gate has no subject on the graph rung 9 can be built as.** *Composition error sub-linear in $N$*: the vehicle's unions sit at $N = 8, 10, 13, 18$, $N = 13$ is two graphs with 3 and 4 families, and every step up adds a different subsystem's physics — $N$ is not a dial. *$\mathcal R$ closes*: two of its four terms have no declaration (W200) and its units are unrelated (W201). A replacement is proposed rather than substituted: **G1** $O(1)$ declarations per join (**met**, Tier 46); **G2** each join's receiving subsystem closes its own balance with the join's term and fails without it, over a march (**at the release state: J2 passes, J3 fails (W199), J1 carries no power**; no march); **G3** each join's composition error against a tightly coupled referent, and whether the joins' errors add (**not built**); the $N$ clause withdrawn to CS-7, where $N$ is a dial | `open`. **Rung 9 is built as a compile and measured at its joins' release state, and is not complete.** Done when the gate is agreed on both plan pages and G2 and G3 are run on a march, or the gate is restated again |
| **W199** | `integration_union`'s J3; `wake_array.RotorDisk`; `powertrain.CircuitSolve.torque_at` | *(opened)* **The rotor keeps its native size in its new host, and the machine was sized for it.** `disk.ActuatorDisk` is a rotor of swept width $D = 1$; its face in the host is $32 \times 1/64 = 0.5$. The power the flow gives up through the face over the power the shaft delivers is **exactly $0.5$** at uniform inflow — $1.0$ in the native geometry, the control — and $0.5$ times the inflow non-uniformity at the settled one; re-sizing the disk to its face closes it to the non-uniformity alone. Re-sized, the declared machine **has no operating point**: the shaft speed doubles, the loop current follows, and the torque demanded is $31\times$ the largest the disk delivers in its clamp. Tier 46's host re-declaration fixed the ports and not the device, and `CircuitSolve.torque_at` builds the disk at its default width and cannot be told | `open`, **medium**: a declaration that a device's geometry is its host's, and a powertrain sized for the rotor that is actually there — a design decision. Relates to W195 (a measure) and W196 (one more state J3 re-derives) |
| **W200** | `ExpertCapabilities`, `CaseGraph`; [[port-algebra-atlas-0.1]] §6 | *(opened)* **$\mathcal R(t)$ cannot be assembled from the records on any graph with a dissipative agent or an open domain.** §6's residual has four terms. Storage is declared on all 18 agents and seam powers come from the ports; but **no field can carry dissipation** — 13 of the 18 dissipate by their own constants ($\nu$, $UA$, $I^2R$, the core's $K$) — and a tiling's domain boundaries are not ports: ten window faces carry inflow, outflow and wall energy through nothing declared. Every $\mathcal R$ that has closed here was a receiver balance written by hand (CS-9, CS-12, the front-wing demo), and rung 2's own gate was closed as not measurable (OP-4) | `open`, **medium**. Done when dissipation and physical-boundary power are declarable — or §6 is restated to say its residual is per receiver, which is what every closure measured |
| **W201** | `ExpertCapabilities.dt_native`, `L_native`; `compiler._clocks`, `_r9_flux_matching` | *(opened)* **No declaration carries a unit of time or length.** The union's three clocks — $0.0125$ in the tiling's convective units, $0.05$ s, and $0.2$ in `wake_array`'s rotor-diameter units — are compared by `L7/R9` as bare numbers, so Tier 46's "reconciled clocks" admit equal numbers and not equal times; `L_native` reads $1.25$, $1.0$ and $0.2$ m in the same three systems | `open`, **medium**. **[AI Inference]:** relating them is choosing which vehicle this is — its speed, its wing chord, its rotor — and none of the three case studies was written at one physical scale. Done when a record declares its units of time and length, and R9 compares clocks in one |

The shaft's power enters through no declared port | `open`, unchanged |

- **W198–W201 were opened and not fixed.** The rotor is not re-sized in `integration_union`, `CircuitSolve` is unchanged, and the pathmap's rung table keeps its original gate — the restatement lives in §3.3 and on the new page, as W183's did.
